# The China Year Book 1929-30 — Who's Who & Directories

852 entries, LLM-transcribed. These entries were transcribed by the vision model of Opus 5.5. Before using any of these details, you must verify specific details with the original source which is linked to whenever possible.


## Who's Who

**HUANG K'ai-wen, (黃開文)** HUANG K'AI-WEN, (黃開文).—Courtesy name: Hsi-ch'en (錫臣). Prefers to sign himself: Wong Kai-wen. Born 1865 in and native of Chiaoling, Kwangtung. Graduate, Peking Telegraph College. Sometime managing director, Tao-Ching Railway; superintendent of Hankow Customs; director general, Tung-Cheng Railway. 1914-28, grand master of ceremonies, President's Office. — p. 951

**HUANG Kuo-en, (黃國恩)** HUANG KUO-EN, (黃國恩).—Prefers to sign himself; K. U. Wong (Wong Kuo-un). Born 1887; native of Kwangtung. 1910, LL.B. (Wisconsin). Sometime secretary, Ministry of Finance and Commission on Extraterritoriality. 1914-28, technical expert, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce. — p. 951

**HUANG Pao-huan, (黃[illegible]寰)** HUANG PAO-HUAN, (黃[illegible]寰).—Courtesy name: Kao-ch'ing (杲[illegible]). Prefers English rendering: William P. H. Hwang. Born 1897 in and native of Chungshan, Kwangtung. Graduate, Nanking Teachers College. 1922, M. S. (North Carolina State College). 1923, M.A. (Columbia). Sometime principal, Young Wo School, San Francisco, and editor, The Young China. Compiler, Legislative Yuan, since December 1928. — p. 951

**HUANG Shih-heng, (黃士衡)** HUANG SHIH-HENG, (黃士衡).—Courtesy name: Chien-p'ing (劍平). Prefers English rendering: S. H. Hwang. Born 1890; native of Hunan. 1917, B.A. (Iowa). 1919, M.A. (Columbia). 1920-4, instructor in Changsha, Chengtu, and Shanghai. 1925, principal, Changsha Commercial College. 1926, commissioner of industry, Changsha, and concurrently president, Hunan University. 1927, commissioner of education. Reappointed commissioner of education and member, Hunan Provincial Government, April 1929. — p. 951

**HUANG Shou-min, (黃首民)** HUANG SHOU-MIN, (黃首民).—Prefers to sign himself: Wong Shu-min. Born 1890; native of Chinshan, Chekiang. Studied in a missionary school in Huchow. 1912, business manager, Chung Hua Min Pao, Shanghai. 1913, went to U.S.A. and worked his way through college. 1917, graduate, Alabama Polytechnic Institute. 1920, associate general manager, Hua Feng Cotton Spinning & Weaving Co. General Manager of the million-dollar Tai Shan Brick & Tile Co., since 1927. — p. 951

**HUANG Tsung-fa, (黃宗法)** HUANG TSUNG-FA, (黃宗法).—Courtesy name: Yueh-san (約三). Prefers to sign himself: Hwang Tzon-fah. Born 1888 in and native of Wuwei, Anhwei. Doctor of Jurisprudence (New York). Sometime secretary and councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs; director, compilation and translation department, delegation to Washington Conference; technical expert, Customs Conference. Legal practitioner in Tientsin, since 1928. — p. 951

**HUANG Tzu-fang, (黃子方)** HUANG TZU-FANG, (黃子方).—Prefers English rendering: Tsefang F. Huang. Born 1899; native of Amoy, Fukien. 1921, S.B.; 1922, S.M.; 1924, M.D. (Chicago). 1924-6, technical expert, and chief, department of public health, National Epidemic Prevention Bureau, Peking. 1927, commissioner of health, Hankow: health adviser, Municipal Government of Greater Shanghai. Commissioner of Health, Municipal Government of Greater Peiping, since July 1928. Honorary lecturer in hygiene and public health, Yenching University, and Peking Union Medical College. — p. 951

**HUANG, Y. L** HUANG, Y. L., see HUANG JUNG-LIANG. — p. 951

**HUANG Yen-p'ei, (黃炎培)** HUANG YEN-P'EI, (黃炎培).—Courtesy name: Chih-chih (植之). Born 1879; native of Shanghai. Graduate, Nanyang College; went to Japan to specialize in education. 1911-2, head of educational bureau, Kiangsu. 1914, resigned to investigate educational conditions in Yangtze provinces. 1915, studied industrial conditions in U.S.A. 1916, acquired national fame by writing an article entitled "Nine Lessons" upon Yuan Shih-kai's demise. For many years vice-chairman, Kiangsu Provincial Educational Association; chairman, National Association for Vocational Education; member, Kiangsu Provincial Assembly. December 1921, appointed minister of education, but declined. June 1922, reappointed to same post and again declined. — p. 951

**HUNG See-lu** HUNG SEE-LU, see below. — p. 952

**HUNG Shih-lu, (洪式閭)** HUNG SHIH-LU, (洪式閭).—Courtesy name: Po-yung (百容). Prefers to sign himself: Hung See-lu. Born 1895; native of Loching, Chekiang. Graduate, Peking National Medical University. 1920-3, studied in Germany. 1923, professor and president, National Medical University. Author of several medical books. — p. 952

**HUNG Wei-kuo, (洪維國)** HUNG WEI-KUO, (洪維國).—Courtesy name: Ching-min (敬民). Born 1893 in and native of Ihsien, Fengtien. Graduate, Chinese National Institute, Woosung. 1920-5, chief secretary to Jehol Tutung. 1926-7, councillor, Ministry of War, director, department of mines, Ministry of Industry. Author of "A Guide to the Industries of China." — p. 952

**HUNG, William** HUNG, WILLIAM, see below. — p. 952

**HUNG Yeh, (洪業)** HUNG YEH, (洪業).—Courtesy name: Wei-lien (煨蓮). Prefers English rendering: William Hung. Born 1893; native of Fukien. 1917, B.A. (Ohio Wesleyan). 1919, M.A. (Columbia). 1920, B.D. (New York Union Theological Seminary). 1921-2, Chinese secretary, Board of Foreign Missions of Methodist Episcopal Church; lecturer under Lyceum and Chautauqua organizations; Horizon lecturer, De Pauw University. 1922-4, head, department of history, Yenching University, Peking. 1924-7, concurrently dean, Men's College, same University. 1927, delegate, Institute of Pacific Relations, Honolulu. Professor of history and theology, Yenching University, since July 1927. — p. 952

**HUO Kuo-kuang, (賀國光)** HUO KUO-KUANG, (賀國光).—Courtesy name: Yuan-ch'ing (元靖). Born 1885; native of P'uch'i, Hupeh. Graduate, Peking Military College. 1926, commander, Nationalist reorganized 4th army; garrison commander, Wuhan. 1927, senior councillor, Military Council, Nanking. Director of infantry training, Directorate-General of Military Training, since 1928. — p. 952

**HWANG, S. H** HWANG, S. H., see HUANG SHIH-LENG. — p. 952

**HWANG, William P. H** HWANG, WILLIAM P. H , see HUANG PAO-HUAN. — p. 952

**HWANG Tzon-fah** HWANG TZON-FAH, see HUANG TSUNG-FA. — p. 952

**I Tsan-chou, (伊贊周)** I TSAN-CHOU, (伊贊周).—Prefers to sign himself: T. C. Ellebo. Born 1895; native of Wanp'ing, Chihli. Graduate, Chefoo and Nanking naval schools, and Nanyuan Aviation School. 1924, flight leader, Mukden army air service. 1926-7, chief of general affairs division, Aeronautical Deparment, Ministry of Military Affairs. — p. 952

**I P'ei-chi, (易培基)** I P'EI-CHI, (易培基).—Courtesy name: Yin-ts'un (寅村). Born 1889; native of Changsha, Hunan. Graduate, Wuchang Language School. 1920, principal, Hunan 1st normal college. 1921, chief secretary to civil governor of same province. 1925, minister of education and concurrently president, Girls' Normal College, Peking. 1926, minister of education, Canton. 1927, president, Labour University, Shanghai. Minister of Agriculture and Mining since April 1928. — p. 952

**I Tsung-k'uei, (易宗夔)** I TSUNG-K'UEI, (易宗夔).—Born 1875; native of Hunan. Studied in Japan. 1912 and 1917, member of parliament. 1925, director, Law Compilation Bureau. Author of "Huang Hai Lou Essays and Poetry." — p. 952

**JAO Meng-jen, (饒孟任)** JAO MENG-JEN, (饒孟任).—Courtesy name: Ching-po (敬伯). Prefers to sign himself: Loe Meng Len. Born 1872; native of Nanchang, Kiangsi. Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. 1908, barrister-at-law (Lincoln's Inn, London). Sometime councillor, Law Compilation Bureau, and senator. Legal practitioner in Peiping since 1918. — p. 952

**JEN Ch'uan-pang, (任傳榜)** JEN CH'UAN-PANG, (任傳榜).—Courtesy name: Hsiao-san (筱珊). Prefers English rendering: C. P. Yin. Born 1878 in and native of Wukiang, Kiangsi. 1911, B.A. in railway administration (Illinois). Sometime managing director, Peking-Suiyuan, Shanghai-Nanking, and Shanghai-Hangchow-Ningpo Railways. — p. 953

**JEN Hung-chun, (任鴻雋)** JEN HUNG-CHUN, (任鴻雋).—Courtesy name: Shu-yung (叔永). Prefers to sign himself: Zen Hung-chiun. Born 1886; native of Szechwan. 1916, B.A. (Cornell). 1917, M.A. (Columbia). 1924-5, vice-president, Nanking Southeastern University. 1926-8, financial secretary, China Foundation for Promotion of Education and Culture, Peking. Trustee and director, same foundation, since January, 1929. 1914-23, president, Science Society of China. Translator of Spencer's "Essay on Education." — p. 953

**KALACHIN, Prince** KALACHIN, PRINCE, see KUNG-SUNG-NO-ERH-PU. — p. 953

**K'ANG T'ung-[illegible]ih, (康同璧)** K'ANG T'UNG-[illegible]IH, (康同璧).—Born 1888 in and native of Nanhai, Kwangtung. Wife of Lo Ch'ang and daughter of late K'ang Yu-wei. 1909, B.A. (Columbia). 1920, delegate, International Council of Women Quinquennial Conference, Christiania. 1920-1924, vice-president, International Council of Women. 1921, vice-president, London Lyceum Club. 1925, president, National Council of Women of China. Author of a book of poems. — p. 953

**KAO En-hung, (高恩洪)** KAO EN-HUNG, (高恩洪).—Courtesy name: Ting-an (定庵). Born 1875 in and native of P'englai, Shantung. Studied in Chefoo Temple Hill College and King's College, London. Sometime director of telegraphs, Hankow and Chengtu. 1922-3, minister of communications and education. 1924-5, director general, Kiaochow Port Administration. — p. 953

**KAO Lin-wei, (高凌霨)** KAO LIN-WEI, (高凌霨).—Courtesy name: Tseh-yü (澤畬). Born in and native of Tientsin, Chihli. 1917, member of parliament. 1920, vice-minister of agriculture and commerce. 1921-2, minister of finance and communications; civil governor of Chihli. 1923-4, premier and director general of customs revenue administration. — p. 953

**KAO Lu, (高[illegible])** KAO LU, (高[illegible]).—Courtesy name: Shu-ch'ing (曙青). Born 1878; native of Ch'anglo, Fukien. Graduate, Brussels University. 1912, secretary, President's Office, and departmental chief, Ministry of Interior, Nanking. 1913-20, director, Peking Observatory. 1920, superintendent of students, Europe. 1922, reappointed director of Peiping Observatory. 1927, chief secretary, Ministry of Education, Nanking, and concurrently member, Kiangsu Provincial Government; director, Nanking Observatory. Minister to Paris, since October 1928. Delegate to many scientific conferences and author of several treatises on astronomy. — p. 953

**KE Ching-en, (葛敬恩)** KE CHING-EN, (葛敬恩).—Courtesy name. Chan-hou (湛侯). Born 1889; native of Kashing, Kiangsu. 1920, graduate, Peking and Tokyo Military Colleges. Vice-Minister, Board of General Staff, since November 1928. Concurrently chief of staff, Commander-in-Chief's Headquarters, Nanking. — p. 953

**K'E Shao-min, (柯劭忞)** K'E SHAO-MIN, (柯劭忞). Native of Shantung. 1927, honorary doctor of literature, Tokyo Imperial University. First Chinese to be given such distinction. 1927-8, director, Bureau for Compilation of Ch'ing Dynasty History. — p. 953

**KOO, S. S** KOO, S. S., see KU SHU-SHEN. — p. 953

**KOO, V. K. Wellington** KOO, V. K. WELLINGTON, see KU WEI-CHUN. — p. 953

**KOO Ying-fan** KOO YING-FAN, see KU YING-FEN. — p. 953

**KU Lang, (顧琅)** KU LANG, (顧琅). Courtesy name: Shih-ch'en (石臣). Born 1880; native of Nanking. Bachelor of Engineering, Tokyo Imperial University. Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. Sometime councillor and chief technical expert, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce; councillor, Ministry of Industry. Author of three volumes on mines and mining industry in China. — p. 953

**KU Meng-yu, (顧孟餘)** KU MENG-YU, (顧孟餘).—Born 1888; native of Hopei (Chihli). Graduate, Berlin University. 1922-5, dean, department of economics, Peiping National University. 1926, elected member, Kuomintang Cenral Executive Committee. 1927, director, publicity department. Central Kuomintang Headquarters, and minister of education, Hankow Nationalist Government. 1928, left for Europe together with Wang Ching-wei. — p. 954

**KU Shu-shen, (顧樹森)** KU SHU-SHEN, (顧樹森).—Courtesy name: Yin-t'ing (蔭亭). Prefers English rendering: S. S. Koo. Born 1886; native of Kiating, Kiangsu. Graduate, Shanghai Lungmen Normal School. Studied in London University. Sometime principal, Shanghai Vocational School; secretary, delegation to League of Nations; magistrate of Wuchin (Kiangsu) and Chinhsien (Chekiang). Commissioner of Education, Municipal Government of Greater Nanking, since November 1928. — p. 954

**KU Tsung-lin, (顧宗林)** KU TSUNG-LIN, (顧宗林).—Prefers English rendering: T. L. Ku. Native of Shanyü, Chekiang. B.A. (Illinois). M.A. (Pennsylvania). Chief accountant of various Government railways in China for many years. 1927-8, director, deparment of posts, Ministry of Communications. — p. 954

**KU Wei-chun, (顧維鈞)** KU WEI-CHUN, (顧維鈞).—Courtesy name: Shao-ch'uan (少川). Prefers English rendering: V. K. Wellington Koo. Born 1887 in Shanghai; native of Chiating, Kiangsu. Studied in St. John's University. 1908, B.A.; 1909, M.A.; 1912, Ph.D (Columbia). 1916, Honorary LL.D. (Yale). 1915-22, minister to Mexico, United States, and Great Britain; delegate to Paris and Washington Conferences; representative on League of Nations Council. 1922-4, minister for foreign affairs and acting premier. 1926-7, minister of finance; transferred to foreign portfolio and premier of Regency Cabinet. Proscribed by National Government, since August 1928. Author of "The Status of Aliens in China" (doctoral dissertation). — p. 954

**KU Ying-fen, (古應芬)** KU YING-FEN, (古應芬).—Courtesy name: Hsiang-ch'ün (相勤). Prefers to sign himself: Koo Ying-fan. Born 1873; native of P'anyü, Kwangtung. Graduate, Tokyo Law College. Member, Tung Meng Hui. 1912, chief secretary to Dr. Sun Yat-sen. 1918, chief secretary, Canton Parliament. 1920, director, department of political affairs, Canton. 1923-6, chief secretary, Generalissimo's headquarters, minister of finance; commissioner of finance and director of civil affairs department, Canton. Chief Secretary, National Government Headquarters, since October 1928. Member, Kuomintang Central Supervisory Committee, since 1926. Member, Kuomintang Central Political Council, since 1927. — p. 954

**KUAN Chun, (關烱)** KUAN CHUN, (關烱).—Courtesy name: Chün-chih (烱之). Native of Hanyang, Hupeh. Chüjen of imperial examinations. 1907, magistrate, Kiangyin (Kiangsu). 1911-26, magistrate, Shanghai Mixed Court (now Shanghai Provisional Court). — p. 954

**KUAN Keng-lin, (關[illegible][illegible])** KUAN KENG-LIN, (關[illegible][illegible]).—Courtesy name: Yin-jen (頴人). Born 1880 in and native of Nanhai, Kwangtung. Chinshih of imperial examinations. Graduate, Peking National University; studied in Japan. 1913-5, director, Peking-Hankow Railway. 1917-9, director, railway department, Ministry of Communications; chief of through traffic department. 1920-7, director, Hankow-Canton-Szechwan Railway; acting vice-minister of communications. Councillor, Ministry of Railways, since December 1928. — p. 954

**K'UANG Fu-shao, (鄺富灼)** K'UANG FU-SHAO, (鄺富灼).—Prefers anglicized Cantonese rendering: Fong F. Sec. Born 1869 in and native of Sunning, Kwangtung. 1882, went to America and worked his way through college. 1905, B.A. (California). 1906, M.A. (Columbia); 1905, B.A. (California). 1906, M.A. (Columbia); 1907, Chinshih of imperial examinations. 1922, honorary LL.D. (Pomona College, California). English editor-in-chief, Commercial Press, Shanghai, for last twenty years. Author of many textbooks. — p. 954

**KUEI Chih, (桂植)** KUEI CHIH, (桂植).—Courtesy name: Tung-yuan (東原). Native of Nanhai, Kwangtung. Sometime 2nd secretary, Legation in London; consul to New Zealand; consul general to Philippines. Consul General to North Borneo since 1926. — p. 955

**KUEI Ch'ung-chi, (桂崇基)** KUEI CH'UNG-CHI, (桂崇基).—Prefers to sign himself: Chung-gi Kwei. Born 1900 in and native of Kueich'i, Kiangsi. 1925, Ph.D. (Columbia). Sometime dean, department of political science, Chungshan University, Canton; director, land bureau, Municipal Government of Greater Nanking; professor of political science, National Central University, Nanking. Councillor, and chief of compilation bureau, Examination Yuan, since October 1928. March 1929, elected reserve member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. — p. 955

**KUNG Chi-ping, (龔積柄)** KUNG CHI-PING, (龔積柄). Courtesy name: Po-heng (伯衡). Born 1872: native of Anhwei. 1912, salt commissioner for Shantung. 1921, finance commissioner for Shantung. 1924-6, civil governor of Shantung. — p. 955

**KUNG, H. H** KUNG, H. H., see below. — p. 955

**K'UNG Hsiang-hsi, (孔祥熙)** K'UNG HSIANG-HSI, (孔祥熙).—Courtesy name: Yung-chih (庸之). Prefers English rendering: H. H. Kung. Born 1881 in and native of T'aiku, Shansi. 75th descendant of Confucius. Brother-in-law of Dr. Sun Yat-sen. 1906, B.A. (Oberlin). 1907, M.A. (Yale). 1926, honorary LL.D. (Oberlin). Founder and principal, Shansi Oberlin Memorial College, T'aiku, since 1907. 1924-5, assistant director-general of Sino-Russian negotiations. 1926-7, commissioner of industry and acting minister of finance, Canton Government. Minister of Industry, Labour and Commerce, since May 1928. 1926-8, member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee and Central Political Council. Reserve member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee and Central Political Council, since March-May 1929. — p. 955

**KUNG Hsin-chan, (龔心湛)** KUNG HSIN-CHAN, (龔心湛).—Native of Anhwei. 1918, minister of finance and premier. 1925, minister of interior. 1926, minister of communications. President, Chinese Industrial Bank, Tientsin, since 1926. — p. 955

**KUNG-SANG-NO-ERH-PU, (貢桑諾爾布)** KUNG-SANG-NO-ERH-PU, (貢桑諾爾布).—Courtesy name: Lo-t'ing (樂亭). Better known as Prince Kalachin. Born 1867, in Chosot'u League, Inner Mongolia. Studied in Japan. Upon return to Mongolia, introduced many reforms and sent young Mongols to Japan to be educated. October 1912, promoted First Class Prince for loyalty to the Republic. 1913, senator. 1914-28, president, Bureau of Mongolian and Tibetan Affairs. — p. 955

**KUNG Tzu-chih, (龔自知)** KUNG TZU-CHIH, (龔自知).—Courtesy name: Chung-chün (仲鈞). Prefers to sign as: Tsuchi Kung. Born 1895; native of Takuan, Szechwan. 1918, graduate, Peking Language School. Chief secretary, Yunnan Provincial Government, and chief secretary, 13th Route Army commander-in-chief's headquarters, since 1928. — p. 955

**KUO Ch'ung-hsi, (郭崇熙)** KUO CH'UNG-HSI, (郭崇熙).—Born 1892, native of Chihli. Graduate, Russian Language School, Peking, and Anglo-Chinese College, Tientsin. 1922, assistant traffic manager, Peking-Mukden Railway. 1926, assistant general manager, Chinese Eastern Railway. — p. 955

**KUO Jen-yuen, (郭任遠)** KUO JEN-YUEN, (郭任遠).—Prefers to sign himself: Zing Yang Kuo. Born 1898; native of Kwangtung. Graduate of Fuhtan and Columbia universities. 1924, acting president, Fuhtan University, Shanghai. Dean, school of biological sciences, and professor of psychology, same University, since 1925. — p. 955

**KUO Mo-jo, (郭沫若)** KUO MO-JO, (郭沫若).—Born 1887; native of Szechwan. Studied in Japan. Editor of The Flood. Leader of "Creative Society," and known as the fighting poet and novelist. Most noted for translation of Goethe's "Die Leiden des Jungen Werthers" (少年維持的煩惱). — p. 955

**KUO Ping-wen, (郭秉文)** KUO PING-WEN, (郭秉文).—Courtesy name: Hung-sheng (鴻聲). Born 1880 in and native of Shanghai. Graduate, Lowrie Institute, Shanghai. 1911, Ph.B. (Wooster). 1912, M.A.; 1914, Ph.D. (Columbia). Sometime president, Shanghai Lowrie institute and Chekiang Provincial College, Hangchow. 1917-9, chairman, educational mission to Japan and Philippines, Europe and U.S.A. 1918-25, president, Southeastern University, Nanking. Director, China Institute (of China Foundation for Promotion of Education and Culture) in New York, since 1925. Vice-President, World Federation of Educational Associations, since 1926. — p. 956

**KUO T'ai-ch'i, (郭泰琪)** KUO T'AI-CH'I, (郭泰琪).—Courtesy name: Fu-ch'u (復初). Prefers to sign himself: Quo Tai-chi. Born 1888 in and native of Wusueh, Hupeh. 1911, B.S. (Pennsylvania). Sometime secretary to President Li Yuan-hung; member of Canton Government's diplomatic mission to U.S.A.; technical expert, delegation to Paris Conference. 1923-4, vice-minister for foreign affairs, Canton. 1927, commissioner for foreign affairs, Shanghai, and vice-minister for foreign affairs, Nanking. Member, Legislative Yuan, since October 1928. March 1929, appointed Minister to Italy. — p. 956

**KUO Yun-kuan, (郭雲觀)** KUO YUN-KUAN, (郭雲觀).—Courtesy name: Min-ch'ou (閔疇). Prefers English rendering: Y. K. Kuo. Born 1888 in and native of Wenchow, Chekiang. Graduate, Fuhtan College, Shanghai. 1914, LL.B. (Peiyang). Postgraduate work in Columbia University. Sometime secretary, delegation to Paris and Washington Conferences; compiler, Law Codification Commission. 1922-5, judge, Supreme Court. Assistant professor in political science, Yenching University, Peking, since September 1926. — p. 956

**KUO, Zing-yang** KUO, ZING-YANG, see KUO JEN-YUEN. — p. 956

**KWEI, Chung-gi** KWEI, CHUNG-GI, see KUEI CH'UNG-CHI. — p. 956

**LOY, Hsiaomin Soule** LOY, HSIAOMIN SOULE, see LEI HSIAO-MIN. — p. 956

**LAIN, S. H** LAIN, S. H., see LIEN SHENG-HAI. — p. 956

**LAN Chien-ch'u, (藍建樞)** LAN CHIEN-CH'U, (藍建樞).—Courtesy name: Chi-pei (季北). Prefers English rendering: K. K. Lang. Born 1858; native of Fukien. Educated at Foochow naval college. 1912, senior member, naval general staff. 1918-20, commander-in-chief of navy. — p. 956

**LANG, K. K** LANG, K. K. see above. — p. 956

**LAO Chih-ch'ang, (勞之常)** LAO CHIH-CH'ANG, (勞之常).—Prefers English rendering: S. W. Lao. Native of Shantung. Sometime director, Shantung conservancy bureau. 1922-3, officiating minister of communications; delegate, Shantung Rehabilitation Conference, Peking. — p. 956

**LAO, S. W** LAO, S. W., see above. — p. 956

**LAU Tong-sun** LAU TONG-SUN, see LOU T'UNG-SUN. — p. 956

**LAY, Hsiaomin Soule** LAY, HSIAOMIN SOULE, see LEI HSIAO-MIN. — p. 956

**LEE Haw-shen** LEE HAW-SHEN, see LI HOU-SHEN. — p. 956

**LEE, T. H** LEE, T. H., see LI TENG-HUI. — p. 956

**LEE Ting-sing** LEE TING-SING, see LI TING-HSIN. — p. 956

**LEI Hsiao-min, (雷孝敏)** LEI HSIAO-MIN, (雷孝敏).—Courtesy name: Shu-li (叔禮). Prefers English rendering: Hsiaomin Soule Lay. Born 1880; native of Kwangtung. Studied in Hongkong Queen's College and Harvard Law School. Chüjen of imperial examinations. 1912, B.A. (George Washington). 1913, M.A. (Pennsylvania). 1916, B.C.L. (Yale). 2nd secretary, Legation in Mexico City, since 1923. — p. 956

**LEUNG, C. W** LEUNG, C. W., see LIANG CHIH-HUAI. — p. 956

**LEW Yuk-lin** LEW YUK-LIN, see LIU YU-LIN. — p. 956

**LI Chai-sum** LI CHAI-SUM, see LI CHI-SHEN. — p. 956

**LI Chi-hung, (李基鴻)** LI CHI-HUNG, (李基鴻).—Courtesy name: Tzu-k'uan (子寬). Native of Yinch'eng, Hupeh. Graduate, Tokyo Law College. Sometime commissioner of finance, Canton; chief commissary officer, Kwangtung army's headquarters. Commissioner of Finance, Municipal Government of Greater Nanking, since July 1928. — p. 957

**LI Chi-shen, (李濟深)** LI CHI-SHEN, (李濟深).—Courtesy name: Jen-ch'ao (任潮). Prefers anglicized Cantonese rendering: Li Chai-sum. Born 1886; native of Ts'angwu, Kwangsi. Graduate, Peking Military College. 1921, chief of staff, Kwangtung 1st division. 1924, garrison commander at Wuchow; sided with Dr. Sun Yat-sen upon Ch'en Chün-ming's defection. 1927, suppressed peasant-labour "red" uprising in Tungkiang, Kwangtung. 1928, commander, 8th Route Army; chief of staff, Nationalist armies; chairman, Canton branch, Kuomintang Central Political Council. 1927-8, member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee and Central Political Council. October 1928, appointed State Councillor and concurrently Chief of General Staff. March 1929, deprived of all posts upon defection of Kwangsi generals in Wuhan. — p. 957

**LI Chia-yu, (李家鈺)** LI CHIA-YU, (李家鈺). Courtesy name: Ch'i-hsiang (其相). Born 1889 in and native of P'uchiang, Szechwan. 1924, commander, Szechwan 1st division. 1925, commander-in-chief, Szechwan frontier defence. Investigation commissioner for the organization of Hsi Kang Province, since November 1928. — p. 957

**LI Ching-fang, (李經芳)** LI CHING-FANG, (李經芳).—Native of Hofei, Anhwei. Better known as Lord Li Ching-fong. Nephew of Marquis Li Hung-chang. 1907-10, minister to London. 1911-2, director-general of posts. — p. 957

**LI Ch'ing-fang, (李慶芳)** LI CH'ING-FANG, (李慶芳).—Courtesy name: Feng-p'u (楓[illegible]). Born 1879; native of Shansi. Graduate, Shansi University. LL.B. (Tokyo Imperial). 1913-7, member of parliament; chief secretary, National Assembly. 1920-7, Peking representative of Governor Yen Hsi-shan. Director, Department of Communications, Peking and Tientsin Garrison Commander-in-chief's headquarters, since July 1928. Managing director, "New Morning Post", Peking, since August 1928. — p. 957

**LI Ching-hsi, (李景曦)** LI CHING-HSI, (李景曦).—Native of Fukien. Educated in Foochow naval college. 1924, commander of naval forces in Shanghai and Woosung. 1927-8, superintendent of customs, Shanghai. — p. 957

**LI Ching-lin, (李景林)** LI CHING-LIN, (李景林).—Courtesy name: Fang-ch'en (芳岑). Born 1884; native of Chihli. Graduate, Paoting military academy. 1925, military and civil governor of Chihli. 1926, sided against Feng Yü-hsiang. 1927-8, joined the Nationalists. Adviser to General Chang Hsueh-liang since March 1929. — p. 957

**LI Ching-lun, (李錦綸)** LI CHING-LUN, (李錦綸).—Prefers English rendering: Frank W. Chinglun Lee. Born 1886 in New York City; native of Kwangtung. M.A. and LL.B. (New York and Chicago). 1906, returned to China. 1911-3, head of political department, Bureau of Foreign Affairs, Canton. 1913-7, exiled with other Kuomintang members. 1917-8, secretary to Dr. Sun Yat-sen. 1918-20, director, political affairs department, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Canton Military Government. 1920-2, commissioner for foreign affairs, Canton. 1922-7, professor of political science and history, Shanghai College. 1927, director, political affairs department, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Nanking. 1927-8, representative of the National Government in United States; appointed member of Permanent Court of Arbitration at The Hague. Minister to Mexico, since October, 1928. — p. 957

**LEW Yuk-lin** LEW YUK-LIN, see LIU YU-LIN. — p. 957

**LI Ching-mai, (李經邁)** LI CHING-MAI, (李經邁).—Courtesy name: Chi-kao (季皋). Born 1876; native of Anhwei. Nephew of Marquis Li Hung-chang. 1905-7, minister to Austria-Hungary. 1908-10, governor of Honan and Chekiang. 1911-2, vice-minister of interior and communications. Retired. — p. 957

**LI Ch'uan-shih, (李權時)** LI CH'UAN-SHIH, (李權時).—Courtesy name: Yü-sheng (雨生). Born 1895; native of Chekiang. Graduate, Tsing Hua College. 1920, B.A. (Beloit College). 1921, M.A. (Chicago). 1922, Ph.D. (Columbia). Professor and dean, School of Commerce, Fuhtan University, Shanghai, since 1922. Author of "Central and Local Finance in China" (doctoral dissertation), "Principles of Business Economics" and "Business Statistics." — p. 958

**LI Chung-kung, (李仲公)** LI CHUNG-KUNG, (李仲公).—Born 1889; native of Kueiyang, Kweichow. Graduate, Peking Government University. 1913, editor, Kuo Feng Jih Pao (Shanghai). 1916, chief secretary, House of Representatives. 1917, chief secretary, Kweichow Provincial Government. 1926, superintendent of customs and commissioner of foreign affairs, Swatow. 1927, active in Party work. Political Vice-Minister of Communications, since 1928; concurrently member, Kweichow Provincial Government, and chairman, Yangtze River Improvement Commission. — p. 958

**LI Fu-lin, (李福林)** LI FU-LIN, (李福林).—Courtesy name: Teng-t'ung (登同). Born 1877; native of P'anyü, Kwangtung. 1918, occupation commissioner, Waichow. 1920, commander, Canton garrison. 1925, commander, Kwangtung 3rd army. 1926, commander, Northern Expedition 5th Army. — p. 958

**LI Hou-chi, (李厚基)** LI HOU-CHI, (李厚基).—Born 1872; native of Kiangsu. 1913-4, defence commissioner of Fukien. 1916-22, civil governor of Fukien. — p. 958

**LI Hou-shen, (李厚身)** LI HOU-SHEN, (李厚身).—Courtesy name: Meng-po (孟博). Prefers to sign as: Lee Haw-shen. Born 1889 in and native of Yüyao, Chekiang. 1916, C. E. (Cornell). 1916-8, member of engineering staff, Baltimore and Ohio Railroad. Sometime technical secretary, Shanghai-Nanking and Shanghai-Hangchow-Ningpo Railways; assistant engineer, Chefoo Weihsien Road Construction Bureau. 1927-8, managing director, Shanghai-Nanking and Shanghai-Hangchow-Ningpo Railways. — p. 958

**LI Keng-yuan, (李根源)** LI KENG-YUAN, (李根源).—Courtesy name: Yin-ch'uan (印泉). Born 1878 in and native of Tengyueh, Yunnan. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. 1913, member of parliament. 1916, civil governor of Shensi. 1918-9, adviser, Canton Military Government. 1922, member of parliament; minister of agriculture and commerce. 1923, acting premier. — p. 958

**LI Kuo-ch'in, (李國欽)** LI KUO-CH'IN, (李國欽).—Courtesy name: Ping-lin (炳[illegible]). Prefers English rendering: K. C. Li. Born 1892 in and native of Changsha, Hunan. Graduate, Hunan Technical College. M.E. (London Royal School of Mines). President, Wah Chang Trading Corporation, since 1918. Director, China Society of America. — p. 958

**LI Lieh-chun, (李烈鈞)** LI LIEH-CHUN, (李烈鈞).—Courtesy name: Hsieh-ho (協和). Born 1883; native of Nanchang, Kiangsi. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. 1910, head, Kiangsi branch of Tung Meng Hui (revolutionary party). 1911 Revolution, Tutuh of Kiangsi. 1913, fled to Japan after unsuccessful revolt. 1919, chief of staff, Canton Military Government. 1920, civil governor of Kiangsi. 1921, chief of general staff, Canton Government. 1926, chairman, Kiangsi Provincial Government. 1927-8, State Councillor and chairman, Chinese Overseas Commission. 1924-8, member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. — p. 958

**LI Ming, (李[illegible])** LI MING, (李[illegible]).—Courtesy name: Fu-sun (馥蓀). Born 1886 in and native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. Graduate, High Commercial College, Yamaguchi, Japan. 1913, Shanghai manager, Chekiang Bank, (since re-organized and renamed the Chekiang Industrial Bank, Ltd.). General manager, same bank, since 1925. At present chairman, Supervisory Committee, Central Bank, Shanghai; chairman, Board of Directors, Bank of China; general liquidator of the Russo-Asiatic Bank in China; councillor, Shanghai Municipal Council. — p. 958

**LI Pa, (李茇)** LI PA, (李茇).—Courtesy name: Fei-t'ang (芾棠). Born 1880; native of Pingkiang, Hunan. Graduate, Peking Law College. 1912, chief judge, Changsha Local Court. 1917, magistrate of Hsiang-hsiang, Hunan. 1922, chief judge, Changsha High Court. 1926, chief procurator, Kwangsi High Procuratorate. Presiding judge, civil division, Supreme Court, since December 1927. — p. 959

**LI P'ei-t'ien, (李培天)** LI P'EI-T'IEN, (李培天).—Courtesy name: Tzu-hou (子厚). Prefers English rendering: P. T. Li. Born 1895; native of Pingch'uan, Yunnan. Graduate, Meji University. 1923, dean, Peking Law College. 1926, magistrate, Chenkiang (Yunnan). Member, Treaty Commission, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, since August 1928. Member, Commission on Mongolian and Tibetan Affairs, since November 1928. — p. 959

**LI Sheng-to, (李盛鐸)** LI SHENG-TO, (李盛鐸).—Courtesy name: Mu-chai (木齋). Native of Kiukiang, Kiangsi. Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. 1895, minister to Japan. 1897, governor of Peking. 1905, one of five high commissioners to investigate constitutional systems of government in Europe and America. 1917, acting minister of agriculture and commerce. 1918-20, speaker of the Senate. — p. 959

**LI Shih-hao, (李思浩)** LI SHIH-HAO, (李思浩).—Courtesy name: Tsan-hou (贊侯). Born 1880 in and native of Ningpo, Chekiang Chüjen of imperial examinations. 1919-20, minister of finance; proscribed for membership of Anfu Party until pardoned. 1924, minister of finance — p. 959

**LI Shih-mou, (李實茂)** LI SHIH-MOU, (李實茂).—Courtesy name: Ch'uan-sun (荃蓀). Native of Hsiaokan, Hupeh. Graduate, Tokyo Officers Training Academy. Sometime chief of staff to Shansi 2nd division and headquarters of Taiyuan garrison commander. Councillor, Directorate General of Military Training, since December 1928. — p. 959

**LI Shih-tseng** LI SHIH-TSENG, see LI YU-YING. — p. 959

**LI Shih-wei, (李士偉)** LI SHIH-WEI, (李士偉).—Courtesy name: Po-chih (伯芝). Born 1880; native of Kiangsi. Graduate, Waseda University, Tokyo. 1921, minister of finance. — p. 959

**LI Shu-hua, (李書華)** LI SHU-HUA, (李書華).—Courtesy name: Jun-chang (潤章). Prefers French rendering: Li Shou Houa. Born 1889 in and native of Ch'angli, Hopei (Chihli). 1922, docteur en sciences (Paris). 1922-7, professor of physics, Peking National University. 1925-6, concurrently dean, physics department, same University. 1925-7, acting rector, Franco-Chinese University, Peiping. Vice-chancellor, Peiping National University, since 1928. Author of "La permabilité selective des membranes polarisées" (doctoral dissertation). — p. 959

**LI T'ang, (李侻)** LI T'ANG, (李侻).—Born 1884; native of Siangtan, Hunan. Graduate, Berlin and Waseda Universities. 1914-25, councillor, Law Compilation Bureau. 1927-8, councillor, Ministry of Justice. — p. 959

**LI Teng-hui, (李登輝)** LI TENG-HUI, (李登輝).—Prefers English rendering: T. H. Lee. Born 1872 in Batavia, Java; native of Tungan, Fukien. 1899, B.A. (Yale). Honorary D. Litt. (St. John's). 1912-3, chief editor, Republican Advocate. President, Fuhtan University, Shanghai, since 1919. Founder, World's Chinese Students Federation, Shanghai. Member, National Opium Suppression Commission, since October 1928. Author of several textbooks. — p. 959

**LI T'ien-lu, (李天祿)** LI T'IEN-LU, (李天祿).—Courtesy name: Fu-t'ien (福田). Born 1884, native of T'aian, Shantung. 1908, B.A. (Peking Methodist); 1914, M.A.; 1916, Ph.D. (Vanderbilt). 1919-22, principal, Peking Academy. 1923-6, dean, school of arts and science, Shantung Christian University, Tsinan. Vice-president, Shantung Christian University, since summer 1927. Author of "Congressional Policy on Chinese Immigration" (doctoral dissertation). — p. 959

**LI Ting-hsin, (李鼎新)** LI TING-HSIN, (李鼎新).—Courtesy name: Ch'eng-mei (承梅). Prefers to sign himself: Lee Ting-sing. Born 1862 in and native of Foochow, Fukien. Graduate, Foochow and Greenwich naval colleges. 1913, commander-in-chief of navy. 1916, commander of 1st Fleet. 1921-4, minister of navy; made full admiral. — p. 960

**LI To, (李鐸)** LI TO, (李鐸).—Courtesy name: Hsiang-ch'uan ([illegible]荃). Prefers to sign himself: Li Tok. Born 1881 in and native of Liling, Hunan. Graduate, Tokyo Officers Training Academy. 1922, councillor, Generalissimo's headquarters, Canton. 1924, chief of staff to Northern Expeditionary forces attacking Hupeh. 1925, dean of instruction, Whampoa Military Academy. 1927, councillor, Commander-in-Chief's headquarters Chief, department of compilation, Directorate General of Military Training, since 1928; concurrently member of National Reconstruction Commission. — p. 960

**LI Tok** LI TOK, see above. — p. 960

**LI Tsung-jen, (李宗仁)** LI TSUNG-JEN, (李宗仁).—Courtesy name: Teh-ling (德鄰). Born 1890; native of Kweilin, Kwangsi. Graduate, Kweilin military academy. Rose to divisional commander. 1926, commander, Nationalist 7th army. 1927, commander, 3rd Route Army. 1928, chairman, Wuhan branch, Kuomintang Central Political Council; concurrently commander-in-chief, 4th Group Army. October 1928, appointed State Councillor and Chairman, Military Council. March 1929, deprived of all posts, upon defection of Kwangsi generals in Wuhan. — p. 960

**LI Tung, (李棟)** LI TUNG, (李棟).—Courtesy name: Chi-yün (紀雲 or 寄耘). Born 1879 in and native of Kaohsien, Chihli. 1909, LL.B. (Meiji). 1910, Chinshih of imperial examinations. 1917, judge, Supreme Court. 1927-8, director, department of civil suits, Ministry of Justice. — p. 960

**LI Wen-fan, (李文範)** LI WEN-FAN, (李文範).—Courtesy name: Chün-p'ei (君培). Native of P'anyü,, Kwangtung. Graduate, Tokyo Law College. 1926, councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Canton. 1927, member, Kwangtung Provincial Government and director, department of civil affairs. 1928, member, Canton branch of Kuomintang Central Political Council. Chief Secretary, Legislative Yuan, since January 1929. Member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee, since March 1929. — p. 960

**LI Yu-ying, (李煜瀛)** LI YU-YING, (李煜瀛).—Courtesy name: Shih-tseng (石曾). Born 1882; native of Kaoyang, Chihli. Studied in France and established the first Chinese beancurd factory in Paris. Declined several offers of appointment by Yuan Shih-k'ai, and associated with Wu Chih-hui, Wang Chao-ming, Ts'ai Yuan-p'ei and other revolutionary leaders. 1919, dean of law department, Peking National University. 1928, chairman, Peiping branch, Kuomintang Central Political Council. Member, Kuomintang Central Supervisory Committee, since 1924. Member, Kuomintang Central Political Council, since 1926. One of most influential Kuomintang "Elder Statesmen". President, Franco-Chinese University, Peiping, since 1925. President, Peiping National University, since August 1928. — p. 960

**LI Yuan, (李垣)** LI YUAN, (李垣).—Courtesy name: Ch'ien-liu (謙六). Born 1881 in Peking. Graduate, Peking Language School. Studied in Russia and France. Sometime assistant Amban at Kiachta; acting pacification commissioner for Urga-Kobdo-Uliassutai. 1926-7, mayor of Peking. — p. 960

**LIANG Chih-huai, (梁植槐)** LIANG CHIH-HUAI, (梁植槐).—Courtesy name: Sung-san (頌三). Prefers anglicized Cantonese rendering: C. W. Leung (Leung Chik-wai). Born 1896 in and native of Canton, Kwangtung. 1923, B.A. (Stetson). 1924, M.A. (Columbia). 1927, professor of International law, Chungshan University, Canton; commissioner of education. 1928, senior secretary to commissioner for foreign affairs, Canton. — p. 960

**LIANG, H. C** LIANG, H. C., see below. — p. 960

**LIANG Han-ts'ao, (梁寒操)** LIANG HAN-TS'AO, (梁寒操).—Courtesy name: Chün-mo (均默). Prefers English rendering: H. C. Liang. Born 1901; native of Kaoyao, Kwangtung. Studied in Shanghai Baptist College and Canton Teachers College. 1927, secretary, Ministry of Communications, Nanking; councillor, Ministry of Finance. Senior Secretary, Ministry of Railways, since November 1928. — p. 961

**LIANG Ju-hao, (梁如浩)** LIANG JU-HAO, (梁如浩).—Courtesy name: Meng-t'ing (孟亭). Better known as: M. T. Liang. Born 1860 in and native of Hsiangshan, Kwangtung. Educated in America. 1906-7, Taotai at Newchwang, Tientsin and Shanghai; chief secretary to governor of Mukden. 1912, minister for foreign affairs. 1921, high adviser, delegation to Washington Conference. 1922, commissioner for negotiating the retrocession of Weihaiwei. President, China International Famine Relief Commission, since January 1922, 1928, vice-president, Tientsin Rotary Club. — p. 961

**LIANG, M. T** LIANG, M. T., see above. — p. 961

**LIANG Pao-chien, (梁寶鑑)** LIANG PAO-CHIEN, (梁寶鑑).—Prefers English rendering: P. K. Liang (Liang Pao-kan). Son of M. T. Liang. Born 1890 in and native of Hsiangshan, Kwangtung. M.A., M.B., Ch.B. (Cantab.), M.R.C.S. (England), and F.R.C.S. (Edinburg). Sometime medical officer, Peking Mukden and Tientsin-Pukow Railways. At present, private practice in Tientsin; honorary surgeon, Mackenzie Memorial Hospital, and medical officer, Kailan Mining Administration, Tientsin. — p. 961

**LIANG, P. K** LIANG, P. K., see above. — p. 961

**LIANG Shih-yi, (梁士詒)** LIANG SHIH-YI, (梁士詒).—Courtesy name: Yen-sun (燕蓀). Born 1869 in and native of Samshui, Kwangtung. 1899, Optimus of imperial examinations. 1912, acting minister of communications; chief secretary to Yuan-Shih-k'ai. 1913, acting vice-minister of finance 1915 and 1927, director general, Customs administration. 1918, speaker of the Senate. December 1921, appointed premier, but opposed by Wu Pei-fu. 1925, delegate to Customs Conference; chairman, financial rehabilitation commission. 1924-7, president, Bank of Communications. — p. 961

**LIANG Shu-min, (梁漱溟)** LIANG SHU-MIN, (梁漱溟).—Original name: Huan-ting (煥鼎). Born 1894; native of Kweilin, Kwangsi. 1918-9, secretary, Ministry of Justice. 1919-24, professor of philosophy, Peking National University. 1925, founder, Tsaochow Middle School, Shantung. 1927, member, Kwangtung Provincial Government. 1928, member, Canton branch, Kuomintang Central Provincial Council; acting chairman, reconstruction commission, Canton. Adviser, Kwangsi Provincial Government, and concurrently principal, Canton 1st Middle School, since January 1929. A famous Buddhist scholar. Author of many treatises in Indian philosophy, Oriental and Occidental civilizations and philosophies, etc. — p. 961

**LIAU, C. Y** LIAU, C. Y., see LIU CHUNG-YING. — p. 961

**LIEN Sheng-hai, (連聲海)** LIEN SHENG-HAI, (連聲海).—Prefers English rendering: S. H. Lain. Born 1885; native of Shunteh, Kwangtung. Graduate, Waseda University. 1917, chief, bureau of printing and engraving, Generalissimo's headquarters, Canton. 1924-5, chief secretary, Kwangtung Provincial Government. 1926-7, officiating minister of communications, Canton. 1927-8, chief secretary to Nationalist Government, Nanking. Political Vice Minister of Communications, since November 1928. — p. 961

**LIM Boon-keng** LIM BOON-KENG, see LIN WEN-CH'ING. — p. 961

**LIM, Robert Kho-seng** LIM, ROBERT KHO-SENG, see LIN K'O-SHENG. — p. 961

**LIN, D. Y** LIN, D. Y., see LIN TAO-YANG. — p. 961

**LIN Feng-mien, (林風眠)** LIN FENG-MIEN, (林風眠).—Born 1898 in and native of Meihsien, Kwangtung. Graduate, Ecole des Beaux Arts (Paris). 1926-7, principal, College of Fine Arts, Peking. Principal, National College of Fine Arts, Hangchow, since March 1928. Well-known painter. — p. 961

**LIN Hsiu-chu, (林修竹)** LIN HSIU-CHU, (林修竹).—Courtesy name: Mou-ch'uan (茂泉). Native of Yenhsien, Shantung. Graduate, Tokyo Engineering College. 1920, member of parliament. 1923-4, chief, bureau of public works, Tsinan. 1927-8, vice-minister of education. — p. 962

**LIN Hung-hsun, (凌鴻勛)** LIN HUNG-HSUN, (凌鴻勛).—Courtesy name: Tso-ming (作銘). Prefers English rendering: H. H. Ling. Born 1894; native of Kwangtung. 1923-7, professor, dean and president, Communications University, Shanghai Author of two volumes on municipal and railway engineering. — p. 962

**LIN K'o-sheng, (林可勝)** LIN K'O-SHENG, (林可勝).—Prefers English rendering: Robert Kho-seng Lim. Son of Lim Boon-keng. Born 1897; native of Amoy, Fukien. 1919, M.B., Ch.B.; 1920, Ph.D.; 1924, D.Sc. (Edinburgh). F.R.S. (Edinburgh). F.R.M.S. Sometime house physician, Royal Infirmary, Edinburgh. Professor and head, department of physiology, Peking Union Medical College, since 1924. Editor, Chinese Journal of Physiology (Peking), since 1926. — p. 962

**LIN Ping, (凌冰)** LIN PING, (凌冰).—Courtesy name: Chi-tung (濟東). Born 1894 in Kushih, Honan 1916, B.A. (Stanford). 1917, M.A. (Columbia). 1919, Ps.D. (Clark). 1919-25, dean, Nankai University, Tientsin. 1922, also 1928, commissioner of education, Kaifeng. 1926, research director, National Association for Advancement of of Education. 1928, trustee, Tsing-Hua University, Peiping; president, Chung Shan University, Kaifeng. January-April 1929, vice-president, Treaty Commission, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. Senior commissioner for the retrocession of Belgian Concession in Tientsin since May 1929. Author of "The Effect of War upon the School Children," "Heredity and Feeblemindedness," etc. — p. 962

**LIN Shen, (林森)** LIN SHEN, (林森).—Courtesy name: Tzu-ch'ao (子超). Born 1862; native of Foochow, Fukien. Resided in California. 1912-23, senator. 1924, member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. 1927, member, Chekiang branch of Kuomintang Central Political Council. 1928, permanent member, Chinese Overseas Commission. State Councillor and Vice-President, Examination Yuan, since October 1928. March 1929, elected member, Kuomintang Central Supervisory Committee. — p. 962

**LIN Tao-yang, (凌道揚)** LIN TAO-YANG, (凌道揚).—Prefers English rendering: D. Y. Lin. Born 1888 in and native of Paoan, Kwangtung. Graduate, St. John's University. 1912, B. Sc. (Massachusetts) 1914, M.F. (Yale) 1915, lecturer on forest conservation, National Y.M.C.A., Shanghai. 1917, technical expert, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce; professor of forestry, Nanking University. 1919, commissioner of forestry, Shantung. 1923-8, director, Bureau of Agriculture and Forestry, Tsingtao. — p. 962

**LIN Wen-ch'ing, (林文慶)** LIN WEN-CH'ING, (林文慶).—Prefers anglicized Amoy rendering: Lim Boon-keng. Born 1870; native of Amoy, Fukien. Educated in Scotland under auspices of Straits Settlements government. M.B., C.M. (Edinburgh). 1894, returned to Singapore. Member on the Legislative Council of the Colony for over ten years. President, Amoy University, since 1923. 1910, adviser, Ministry of Interior, Peking. 1911, delegate to International Hygienic Congress, Dresden. Order of British Empire and Order of Crown of Italy. — p. 962

**LIN Yu-t'ang, (林語堂)** LIN YU-T'ANG, (林語堂).—Born 1895 in and native of Changchow, Amoy. 1916, B.A. (St. John's). 1921, M.A. (Harvard). 1923, Ph.D. (Leipzig). 1916-9, English instructor, Tsing Hua College. Peking. 1923-6, professor of English philology, National University; dean of faculties, National Women's University, Peking; dean of arts, Amoy University. 1927, secretary, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, and managing editor, People's Tribune, Hankow. Research fellow in ancient Chinese phonology, Ministry of Education, residing in Shanghai, since October 1928. Inventor of a Chinese index system. Author of "War-time Essays" (Commercial Press, 1929) and the Kaiming English Readers; translator of Heine's poems. — p. 962-963

**Ling, H. H** LING, H. H., see LIN HUNG-HSUN. — p. 963

**LIU Chan-en, (劉湛恩)** LIU CHAN-EN, (劉湛恩).—Prefers English rendering: Herman Chanen Liu. Born 1896; native of Nanyang, Hupeh. 1918, B.Sc. (Soochow). 1920, M.A. (Chicago). 1922, Ph.D. (Columbia). Sometime secretary, Chinese Educational Commission to U.S., and International Students Federation, New York. 1924-7, secretary, educational department, National Committee Y.M.C.A., Shanghai. President, Shanghai Baptist College, since summer 1928. Author of "Non-verbal tests for use in China" (doctoral dissertation), "Co-education," etc. — p. 963

**LIU Cheh, (劉哲)** LIU CHEH, (劉哲).—Courtesy name: Ching-yü (敬輿). Native of Kirin. Graduate, Peking National University. Sometime civil governor of Kirin; senator. 1927-8, minister of education. — p. 963

**LIU Chen-hua, (劉振華)** LIU CHEN-HUA, (劉振華).—Courtesy name: Hsien-chou (仙洲). Born 1890 in and native of Wanhsien, Szechwan. 1918, B.Sc. (Hongkong). Inventor of two irrigating machines which received certificates of honour from Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce and Chihli provincial exhibition of engineering products. Tientsin. 1921, professor of physics, Hopei University. Author of text-books on applied mechanics and internal combustion engines. — p. 963

**LIU Chen-hua, (劉鎮華)** LIU CHEN-HUA, (劉鎮華).—Courtesy name: Hsueh-ya (雪亞). Born 1883 in and native of Kunghsien, Honan. Graduate, Peking Law College. Sometime civil and military governor of Shensi. 1927, assistant commander-in-chief, Nationalist 2nd Group Army (Northern Route). 1928, commissioner of reconstruction, Honan Provincial Government; member. Peiping branch, Kuomintang Central Political Council. Commander-in-chief, 8th army corps, 2nd Group Army, and member, Honan Provincial Government, since October 1928. — p. 963

**LIU Chi-wen, (劉紀文)** LIU CHI-WEN, (劉紀文).—Born 1890; native of Tungkuan. Kwangtung. Graduate, Tokyo Law College. Sometime supervisor, Kwangtung provincial treasury; chief auditor, Generalissimo's headquarters; quarter-master-general of Nationalist armies. 1923-6, studied municipal government in Great Britain and U.S.A. 1926, commissioner of agriculture and labour, Canton. Mayor of Nanking since April 1928. March 1929, elected member. Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. — p. 963

**LIU Chih-chou, (劉治洲)** LIU CHIH-CHOU, (劉治洲).—Courtesy name: Ting-wu (定五). Born 1883 in and native of Fenghsiang, Shensi. 1912, member, Shensi provincial assembly. 1913 and 1922, member of parliament. 1914-6. field manager, national oil administration. 1917-8, member of parliament, Canton. 1922 and 1925, vice-minister of agriculture and commerce. — p. 963

**LIU Ching-hsin, (劉景新)** LIU CHING-HSIN, (劉景新).—Courtesy name: Ta-i (大易). Born 1889; native of Wuyuan, Shansi. Graduate, Tokyo Law College. Ph.D. (Columbia). 1917, councillor, Generalissimo's headquarters, Canton. Member, Legislative Yuan, since November 1928. — p. 963

**LIU Ching-i, (劉敬宜)** LIU CHING-I, (劉敬宜).—Courtesy name: Pen-i (本義) Prefers English rendering: C. Y. Liu. Born 1897 in and native of Kaifeng, Honan. 1922, B.S. in aviation and motor engineering (Michigan). 1925, professor of engineering, Northeastern University, Mukden. 1926, technical expert, aeronautical department; editor-in-chief, Chinese Aviation Monthly. 1927, vice-minister of agriculture and commerce. — p. 963

**LIU Ching-jen, (劉鏡人)** LIU CHING-JEN, (劉鏡人).—Courtesy name: Shih-hsi (士熙). Born 1868 in and native if Paoshan, Kiangsu. Graduate, Peking College of Languages. 1911, minister to Holland. 1912-8, minister to Russia. 1923-6, vice-chairman, commission on foreign affairs, and commission on Sino-Russian Conference. — p. 964

**LIU Ching-shan, (劉景山)** LIU CHING-SHAN, (劉景山).—Courtesy name: Chu-chün (竹君). Prefers English rendering: C. S. Liu. Born 1882 in and native of Tientsin, Chihli. 1909, B.S.; 1910, M.A. (Pennsylvania). 1918, vice-chairman, standing committee on unification of railway accounts and statistics. 1911, vice-president, Chinese Eastern Railway. 1922-7, director, railway department, Ministry of Communications; chief auditor, Bank of China, Peking. Councillor, Ministry of Railways, since October 1928. — p. 964

**LIU Ch'ung-chieh, (劉崇傑)** LIU CH'UNG-CHIEH, (劉崇傑).—Courtesy name: Tzu-k'ai (子楷). Born 1880 in and native of Foochow, Fukien. Graduate, Tokyo Waseda University. 1917, councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. 1919, technical expert, delegation to Paris Conference. 1920-28, minister to Spain. — p. 964

**Liu, C. Y** LIU, C. Y., see LIU CHING-I. — p. 964

**LIU Chung-ying, (劉鍾英)** LIU CHUNG-YING, (劉鍾英).—Courtesy name: Hsiang-shan (湘山). Prefers to sign as: C. Y. Liau. Born 1876, native of Hunan. Chüjen of imperial examinations. Graduate, Peking Law College. 1917, senior judge, Supreme Court. Reappointed senior judge, Supreme Court, Nanking, since December 1928. — p. 964

**LIU Dze-ru** LIU DZE-RU, see LIU TZU-JU. — p. 964

**Liu, Herman Chanen** LIU, HERMAN CHANEN, see LIU CHAN-EN. — p. 964

**LIU Jui-heng, (劉瑞恒)** LIU JUI-HENG, (劉瑞恒).—Courtesy name.: Yueh-ju (月如). Prefers English rendering: J. Heng Liu. Born 1890 in and native of Tientsin, Chihli. 1909, B.S. (Harvard). 1915, M.D. (Harvard Medical School). 1915-8, associate in surgery, Harvard Medical School of China; surgeon, Red Cross General Hospital, Shanghai. 1918-24, associate and associate professor of surgery, superintendent, Peking Union Medical College. Vice-Minister, Ministry of Health, since October 1928. — p. 964

**LIU K'e-chun, (劉克儁)** LIU K'E-CHUN, (劉克儁).—Courtesy name: Cho-wu (卓吾). Prefers English rendering: Keetsin Liu. Born 1893; native of Anfu, Kiangsi. 1926, doctor of jurisprudence (Munchen). 1926-7, professor of law, Chungshan University, Wuchang; compiler, Law Compilation bureau, Nanking. Member, Legislative Yuan, since December 1928. Author of "Der Begriff der Identitat der Iat im geltenden deutschen Strafprozetsrecht" (doctoral dissertation). — p. 964

**Liu, Keetsin** LIU, KEETSIN, see above. — p. 964

**LIU Lu-yun, (劉盧[illegible])** LIU LU-YUN, (劉盧[illegible]).—Born 1891; native of Yungfeng, Kiangsi. Graduate, Fuhtan University, Shanghai. LL.B. (California). Sometime dean, department of sociology, Fuhtan University; secretary, publicity department, Central Kuomintang Headquarters. Director, compilation department, Legislative Yuan, since October 1928. March 1929, elected member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. — p. 964

**LIU Shang-ch'ing, (劉尚清)** LIU SHANG-CH'ING, (劉尚清).—Courtesy name. Hai-ch'uan (海泉). Native of Haich'eng, Fengtien. Graduate, Mukden Law College. 1914, managing director, Mukden Provincial Bank. 1919, commissioner of finance, Heilungkiang. 1920, managing director, Holikang Coal Mining Co., T'angyuan (Heilungkiang) 1927, minister of agriculture and commerce; civil governor of Mukden. — p. 964

**LIU Shih, (劉峙)** LIU SHIH, (劉峙). Courtesy name: Ching-fu (經扶). Born 1891; native of Generalissimo's headquarters, Canton; instructor. Whampoa Military Academy. Commander, 1st division, National Army, since 1927, with headquarters in Hsuchow and Haichow (Kiangsu). March 1929, elected member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. — p. 965

**LIU Shih-hsun, (劉式訓)** LIU SHIH-HSUN, (劉式訓).—Courtesy name: Sun-sheng (笋笙). Born 1868 in and native of Nanhui, Kiangsu. 1906-12, minister to Paris. 1914-6, minister to Brazil. 1913, 1917, and 1921, vice-minister of foreign affairs. 1922-3, vice-chairman, commission for the study of treaties. — p. 965

**LIU Shih-shun, (劉師舜)** LIU SHIH-SHUN, (劉師舜)—Courtesy name: Ch'in-wu (琴五). Born 1900; native of Ifeng, Kiangsi. 1920, graduate, Tsing Hua College. 1921, A.B. (Johns Hopkins). 1923, A.M. (Harvard). 1922-4, fellow in international law, Carnegie Endowment for International Peace. 1925, Ph.D. in public law (Columbia). 1925-7, professor of International relations, Tsing Hua College. Senior member, Treaty Commission, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, since autumn, 1927. Author of "Extraterritoriality: Its Rise and Its Decline," 1925; "The Problem of Extraterritoriality" (in Chinese) 1929. — p. 965

**LIU Shu-fan, (劉書蕃)** LIU SHU-FAN, (劉書蕃).—Courtesy name: Chien-hou (劍侯). Born 1880; native of Fukien. Graduate, Foochow College. 1910-5, deputy postal commissioner for Chihli. 1915-27, postal commissioner for Kweichow, Shensi, and Anhwei. Director General of Posts, since October 1927. — p. 965

**Liu, Timothy Tingfang** LIU, TIMOTHY TINGFANG, see below. — p. 965

**LIU T'ing-fang, (劉廷芳)** LIU T'ING-FANG, (劉廷芳).—Prefers English rendering: Timothy Tingfang Lew. Born 1891 in and native of Wenchow, Chekiang. 1914, B.A.; 1915, M.A. (Columbia). 1918, B.D. (Yale). 1920, Ph.D. (Columbia). 1920-6, dean, graduate school of education, National Normal University; professor of psychology, National University. Dean, school of theology, Yenching University, Peking, since 1924. Author of "Psychology of Learning Chinese," "China in American School Text-books"; joint author of "China Today Through Chinese Eyes," "Education in China." — p. 965

**LIU Tseh-jung, (劉澤榮)** LIU TSEH-JUNG, (劉澤榮).—Courtesy name: Shao-chou (紹周). Born 1892; native of Kaoyao, Kwangtung. 1914, graduate, St. Petersburg University. 1923, vice-director, bureaus of education and finance, Chinese Eastern Railway. Chief auditor, Board of Management, same Railway, since November 1927. — p. 965

**LIU Ts'un-hou, (劉存厚)** LIU TS'UN-HOU, (劉存厚).—Courtesy name: Chi-chih (積之). Born 1885 in and native of Chenyang, Szechwan. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. 1913-6, garrison commander, Chungking. 1917, commander-in-chief of southern Szechwan: military governor. 1924, military governor of Szechwan; frontier defence commissioner of Szechwan and Shensi. — p. 965

**LIU Tsung-yi, (劉宗翼)** LIU TSUNG-YI, (劉宗翼).—Courtesy name: Wen-sun (文孫). Born 1888; native of Hochien, Hopei. Studied at St. John's University. Connected with the salt revenue administration for the last fifteen years. Chief Inspector of Salt Revenue, since November 1928. — p. 965

**LIU Tzu-ju, (劉子如)** LIU TZU-JU, (劉子如).—Prefers to sign himself: Liu Dze Ru. Born 1870; native of Kikiang, Szechwan. 1896, joined London Mission Church. 1898, removed to Chungking, was employed by Brunner Mond Company. 1901, chief agent for Singer Sewing Machine Company in Szechwan. 1924, travelled around the world, lecturing in more than seventy places. Upon his return the next year, built a clock tower for Siao Shih Dze Church, Chungking, its bells being a farewell gift from Mission Board of Methodist Church when leaving Canada. 1926, sold all his property and gave everything but living expenses for his family to various Christian activities, including West China Union University, orphanages in Chungking and Chengtu, Y.M.C.A. and the self-supporting church in Chungking, the whole amounting to $130,000. Address: Singer Sewing Machine Co., Chungking. — p. 965-966

**LIU Wen-hui, (劉文輝)** LIU WEN-HUI, (劉文輝).—Courtesy name: Tzu-ch'ien (自乾). Born 1894 in and native of Tayi, Szechwan. Graduate, Paoting military academy. Sometime commander, Szechwan 9th and 31st divisions; commander, Nationalist 24th army; commander-in-chief for defence of Western Szechwan. Chairman, Szechwan Provincial Government, since 1928. — p. 966

**LIU Yu-lin, (劉玉麟)** LIU YU-LIN, (劉玉麟).—Prefers Cantonese rendering: Lew Yuk-lin. Born 1862; native of Kwangtung. Graduate, Philips Academy, Andover, U.S.A. 1914, honorary LL.D. (Cantab.). 1904-8, consul general to South Africa. 1910-4, minister to Great Britain. 1917-23, high adviser to Canton Military Government; salt commissioner for Kwangtung and Kwangsi. — p. 966

**LO Ch'ang, (羅昌)** LO CH'ANG, (羅昌).—Prefers anglicized Hakka rendering: Lo Chong. Born 1883; native of Paoan, Kwangtung. 1908, B.A. (Oxon). 1914-7, commissioner for foreign affairs in Tsinan and Amoy. 1919-24, consul-general at Singapore and Ottawa. 1927, councillor, Cabinet Office; adviser, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. — p. 966

**LO Chong** LO CHONG, see above. — p. 966

**LO Chung-yi, (羅忠詒)** LO CHUNG-YI, (羅忠詒).—Courtesy name: Yi-yuan (儀元). Prefers to sign himself: T. Y. Lo (Lo Tsung-yee). Born 1886; native of Fukien. 1908, B.A. in economics (Cantab.). Hanlin Compiler of imperial examinations. 1914-23, 1st secretary and chargé d'Affaires, London and Lima. 1924, secretary to the Cabinet. Minister to Denmark since 1926. — p. 966

**LO Hung-nien, (羅鴻年)** LO HUNG-NIEN, (羅鴻年).—Courtesy name: Yen-feng (雁峰). Born 1881; native of Kiangsu. 1911, B.Com. (Birmingham). 1912-20, director, national treasury department, Bank of China. 1921, vice-minister of finance. 1924, vice-minister of education. 1923-7, technical expert, commission for readjustment of finance; chief superintendent, Continental Bank, Peking. Manager, Chinese-American Bank of Commerce, Peiping, since 1928. — p. 966

**LO Pan-hui, (羅泮輝)** LO PAN-HUI, (羅泮輝). Courtesy name: Ch'in-san (芹三). Prefers English rendering: P. H. Lo. Native of Kwangtung. Graduate, Queen's College, Hongkong. 1908, B.A. (Harvard). 1909, M.A.; 1911, J. D. (Chicago). 1912-3, commissioner for foreign affairs, Canton. 1914-20, chief English secretary, Shanghai-Hangchow-Ningpo Railway. Chief English secretary, Shanghai-Nanking Railway, since 1920. Concurrently legal adviser, same Railway, since 1927. 1917-25, lecturer, Comparative Law School of China. — p. 966

**Lo, R. Y** LO, R. Y. see LO YUN-YEN. — p. 966

**Lo, T. Y** LO, T. Y., see LO CHUNG-YI. — p. 966

**LO Ting, (羅鼎)** LO TING, (羅鼎).—Courtesy name: Chung-min (重民). Born 1887; native of Yuhsien, Hunan. LL.B. (Tokyo Imperial). Sometime compiler, Law Codification Commission; judge, Peking High Court. Member, Legislative Yuan, since November 1928. — p. 966

**LO Wen-kan, (羅文幹)** LO WEN-KAN, (羅文幹).—Courtesy name: Chün-jen (鈞任). Born 1888; native of P'anyü, Kwangtung. M.A. (Oxon.) and Barrister-at-law (Inner Temple, London). 1912-5, procurator general, Peking. 1916-26, vice-chairman, Law Codification Commission: 1921 and 1926, minister of justice. 1922, chief justice, Supreme Court; minister of finance. 1926-7, delegate, Customs Conference; director general, customs administration. January-June 1928, minister for foreign affairs. Now in Mukden. — p. 966

**LO Yun-yen, (羅運炎)** LO YUN-YEN, (羅運炎).—Courtesy name: Yao-tung ([illegible]東). Prefers English rendering: R. Y. Lo. Born 1890 in and native of Kiukiang, Kiangsi. 1911, B.A. (Baldwin-Wallace). 1912, M.A.; 1914, Ph.D. (Syracuse). 1915-9, professor of economics and sociology, Kiukiang William Nast College. General editor, Methodist Episcopal Church, and vice-chairman, China Christian Literature Council, Shanghai, since 1921. Author of "The Social Teaching of Confucius" (doctoral dissertation) and New China's Problems." — p. 967

**LOE Meng-len** LOE MENG-LEN, see JAO MENG-JEN. — p. 967

**LOH, Tachuen S. K** LOH, TACHUEN S. K., see LU SHOW-CHING — p. 967

**LOO Hing-yun** LOO HING-YUN, see LU HSING-YUAN — p. 967

**LOU Tseng-tsiang** LOU TSENG-TSIANG, see LU CHENG-HSIANG. — p. 967

**LOU T'ung-sun, (樓桐孫)** LOU T'UNG-SUN, (樓桐孫).—Courtesy name: P'ei-lan (佩蘭). Prefers French rendering: Lau Tong Sun. Born 1896 in and native of Yungk'ang, Chekiang. Graduate, Chekiang Law School. 1925, M.A. (Paris). 1925-6, dean, Shanghai Law College. 1927, secretary, Chekiang Provincal Government, and president, Chekiang Law College. 1928, dean, Institute for the Training of Administrative Officers; professor of law, National Central University, Nanking. Member, Legislative Yuan, and adviser, Treaty Commission, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, since October 1928. Author of "A Study of Nationalism," "Modern Political History of England," "Interpretation of the General Principles of the Civil Code," and translator of "La Co-opération," "Les Sociétés co-opératives," etc. — p. 967

**LU Cheng-hsiang, (陸徵祥)** LU CHENG-HSIANG, (陸徵祥). Courtesy name: Tzu-hsing (子欣). Prefers French rendering: Lou Tseng Tsiang. Born 1870 in and native of Shanghai. 1905-11, minister to Holland; delegate to 2nd Hague Peace Conference. 1911, minister to Russia. 1912, minister for foreign affairs and premier. 1915, acting premier and minister for foreign affairs. 1919, chief delegate to Paris Peace Conference. 1922-7, minister to Switzerland; delegate to League of Nations Assembly and International Labour Conference. Summer of 1927, entered a monastery in Belgium (Abbaye de Saint André, Lophem-Lez-Bruges). — p. 967

**LU Chih-i, (呂志伊)** LU CHIH-I, (呂志伊).—Courtesy name: T'ien-min (天民). Born 1880; native of Szumao, Yunnan. Studied in Japan. 1912, vice-minister of justice, Nanking. 1913, senator. 1920-1, vice-minister of justice, and interior, Canton. 1924, chief justice, Supreme Court, Canton. Member, Legislative Yuan since December 1928. — p. 967

**LU Chin, (陸錦)** LU CHIN. (陸錦).—Courtesy name: Hsiu-shan (繡山). Born 1880 in and native of Tientsin. Graduate, Peiyang and Tokyo Military Colleges. 1913, defence commissioner of Tientsin. 1920, acting commander, 9th division. 1922, chief of staff to high inspecting commissioner of Chihli, Shantung and Honan. 1924, minister of war. — p. 967

**LU Ch'in, (盧靜)** LU CH'IN, (盧靜).—Courtesy name: Mu-chai (木齋). Born 1855; native of Hupeh. Mathematics instructor in Peiyang military academy under Li Hung-chang. 1911, retired. March 1927, donated one hundred thousand dollars to Nankai University, Tientsin, for construction of a new library; upon its completion he will donate thereto his own collection of 200,000 volumes. — p. 967

**LU Ch'uan, (陸權)** LU CH'UAN, (陸權).—Courtesy name: Mou-sun ([illegible]遜). Born 1891; native of Quinsan, Kiangsu. 1922, graduate, Peking Military College. Sometime director of training, Shanghai and Sungkiang garrison commander's headquarters. Mayor of Soochow since November 1928. — p. 967

**LU Hsien, (呂咸)** LU HSIEN, (呂咸).—Courtesy name: Chu-ch'ing (著青). Born 1891; native of Cholu, Hopei. Graduate, Peiyang University. LL.B. (Peking National). 1925. director, department of general affairs, Directorate General of Sino-Russian Negotiations. 1928, councillor, Ministry of Industry, Labour and Commerce. Commissioner of Labour and Commerce, Municipal Government of Greater Peiping, since August 1928. — p. 967

**LU Hsing-yuan, (盧興原)** LU HSING-YUAN, (盧興原)—Prefers anglicized Cantonese rendering: Loo Hing-yun or H. Y. Loo. Born 1885, at Macao. 1916, Barrister-at-Law, Inner Temple (London); M.A. (Oxford). 1919, chief of department, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Canton. 1921, chief of division, Supreme Court, Canton. June-October 1927, president of Shanghai Provisional Court — p. 968

**LU Kung-wang, (呂公[illegible])** LU KUNG-WANG, (呂公[illegible]).—Born 1880; native of Chekiang. Graduate, Paoting Military College. 1911, chief of staff, Chekiang revolutionary army. 1912, commander, 6th division. 1916 and 1925, civil and military governor of Chekiang. 1918, commander-in-chief, Chekiang provincial army. 1920, chief of staff, Canton Military Government. — p. 968

**LU Shou-ching, (陸守[illegible])** LU SHOU-CHING, (陸守[illegible]).—Courtesy name: Ta-ch'uan (達權). Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: Tachuen S. K. Loh. Born 1884 in and native of Tsingpu, Kiangsu. 1914, B.A. in political science (Wisconsin). 1916, magistrate, Kulangsu Mixed Court, Amoy. 1917, chief judge, Shanghai District Court. 1925, assistant councillor, Ministry of Communications. 1927, senior secretary, Ministry of Military Affairs. — p. 968

**LU Tsung-yu, (陸宗輿)** LU TSUNG-YU, (陸宗輿).—Courtesy name: Yün-sheng (潤生). Born 1875 in and native of Haining, Chekiang. Graduate, Waseda University. Chüjen of imperial examinations. 1912, vice-minister of finance. 1913, senator. 1913-6, minister to Japan. 1919, branded "national traitor" by Student Movement, together with Chang Tsung-hsiang and Ts'ao Yü-lin. 1924, member, Provisional Senate. Sometime managing director, Exchange Bank of China. — p. 968

**LU Yung-hsiang, (盧永祥)** LU YUNG-HSIANG, (盧永祥).—Courtesy name: Tzu-chia (子嘉). Born 1867 in and native of Tsiyang, Shantung. 1912, commander, 20th division. 1914, commander, 10th division. 1915-17, defence commissioner of Shanghai and Woosung. 1920, military governor of Chekiang. 1922, declared independence of Peking. 1924, fled to Japan. 1925, Tupan (military governor) of Kiangsu. 1926, retired. — p. 968

**LUNG Yun, (龍雲)** LUNG YUN, (龍雲).—Courtesy name: Chih-chou (志舟). Native of Chaot'ung, Yunnan. Graduate, Yunnan military institute. 1922, commander, Chinkuochun 3rd army. 1923, defence commissioner of Eastern Yunnan. 1924, commander, Chienkuochun 5th army. 1925, defence commissioner of Kwenming. 1927, commander, Nationalist 38th army. Chairman, Yunnan Provincial Government, and concurrently commander-in-chief, 13th Route Army, since 1928. — p. 968

**MA Chen-ch'ang, (馬振昌)** MA CHEN-CH'ANG, (馬振昌).—Courtesy name: Hsü-tung (旭東). Born 1896 in and native of Miyun, Chihli. Graduate, Nanyuan Aviation School. Sometime flight leader, Mukden Army aviation department. 1927, director. Aeronautical Department, Ministry of Military Affairs. — p. 968

**MA Fu-hsiang, (馬福祥)** MA FU-HSIANG, (馬福祥).—Courtesy name: Yün-t'ing (雲亭). Native of Taoho, Kansu. 1912, chief administrative officer, Kokonor. 1913, military commissioner, Ninghsia, Kansu. 1920, Tutung (civil and military governor) of Suiyuan. Member, Kansu Provincial Government, since September 1928. — p. 968

**MA Ho, (馬和)** MA HO, (馬和).—Courtesy name: Chün-wu (君武). Born 1887 in and native of Kweilin, Kwangsi. Graduate, Kyoto Imperial University. 1911, doktor ingenieur, (Berlin). 1912, vice-minister of agriculture and commerce. 1913 and 1916, senator. 1918, minister of communications, Canton Military Government. 1921-2, civil governor of Kwangsi. 1925-6, minister of justice, Canton. Chancellor, Kwangsi National University, since 1927. Author of textbooks and translator of scientific treatises. — p. 968

**MA Hsu-lun, (馬敘倫)** MA HSU-LUN, (馬敘倫).—Courtesy name: I-ch'u (夷初). Born 1885 in and native of Hangchow, Chekiang. Sometime vice-minister of education, Peking; commissioner of education, Chekiang Provincial Government. Political Vice-Minister of Education, Nanking, since October 192[illegible]. — p. 969

**MA Soo** MA SOO, see below. — p. 969

**MA Su, (馬素)** MA SU (馬素).—Courtesy name: Hui-chai (繪齋). Prefers to sign himself: Ma Soo. Born 1883 in and native of Shanghai. M.A. (Columbia). 1912, secretary, Nanking Provisional Government; founder and editor, China Republican (published in English), Shanghai. 1914-9, Kuomintang organizer in U.S.A., Canada and Mexico. 1921-2, Kuomintang representative in Washington; Customs Conference. — p. 969

**MA Yin-ch'u, (馬寅初)** MA YIN-CH'U, (馬寅初).—Born 1884 in and native of Ch'enghsien, Chekiang. 1910, B.A. (Yale). 1911, M.A.; 1913, Ph.D. (Columbia). Honorary LL.D. (Soochow). 1915, professor of economics and dean, Peking Government University. 1920-7, chief of note issue department, Bank of China, Peking Head Office. Member, Chekiang Provincial Government, and Legislative Yuan, since October 1928. — p. 969

**MA Yu-shan, (馬玉山)** MA YU-SHAN, (馬玉山).—Better known as: M. Y. San. Born 1878 in and native of Hsiangshan, Kwangtung. Founder and president, M. Y. San Confectionery and Biscuit Manufacturing Company, and of China National Sugar Refining Company, with factories in Manila, Hongkong, Shanghai and Woosung. — p. 969

**MAO I-shen, (茅以昇)** MAO I-SHEN, (茅以昇).—Courtesy name: T'ang-ch'en (唐臣). Prefers English rendering: T. E. Mao. Born 1896 in and native of Chinkiang, Kiangsu. 1917, Master, of Civil Engineering (Cornell). 1920, Doctor of Engineering, Carnegie Institute of Technology. 1921, awarded Fuertes Medal for Original Research (Cornell). 1921-2, vice-president, Tangshan College, Chiao Tung University. 1922-3, engineer, China Industry Development Corporation. 1923-4, dean, College of Engineering, Southeastern University, Nanking. 1924-5, president, Hohai Engineering College. 1925-7, technical expert, Ministry of Communications. 1926, president, Tangshan University. President, Pei Yang University, since September 1928. Author of "Secondary Stresses in Bridge Trusses." — p. 969

**MAO, T. E** MAO, T. E., see above. — p. 969

**MOH, H. Y** MOH, H. Y. see MU HSIANG-YUEH. — p. 969

**MEI Lan-fang, (梅蘭芳)** MEI LAN-FANG, (梅蘭芳).—Born 1893; native of Kiangsu. China's most noted female impersonator. Both father and grandfather were famous actors in Peking. First appeared on the stage, aged 13. Six years later, first appeared in Shanghai; won 270,000 votes in popularity contest as "King of Actors." 1918, engaged by Tokyo Imperial Theatre, and three years later played in Hongkong. Enthusiastically received in both places. Acts (reverse role of Sarah Bernhardt) in over four hundred plays, one-third being own productions. Wardrobes and ornaments valued at over one hundred thousand dollars. A philanthropist and painter. Spends leisure time in studying mechanics and science and collecting pet birds. — p. 969

**MEN Chih-chung, (門致[illegible])** MEN CHIH-CHUNG. (門致[illegible]).—Born 1888; native of Kirin. Graduate, Paoting Military College. Sometime chief of Suiyuan Constabulary and one of Feng Yü-hsiang's Kuominchun commanders. Chairman, Ninghsia Provincial Government, since October 1928. — p. 969

**MENG Hsien-ch'eng, (孟憲承)** MENG HSIEN-CH'ENG, (孟憲承).—Courtesy name: Po-ho (伯和). Prefers English rendering: H. C. Meng. Born 1894; native of Soochow. Kiangsu. 1916, B.A. (St. John's). 1916-8, instructor, Tsing Hua College. 1919-20, research scholar, George Washington University. 1921-3, attaché, Legation in London; lecturer, Southeastern University, Nanking. 1923-6, dean, Chinese department, St. John's University, and Tsing Hua College. 1927, dean, Kuang Hua University, Shanghai. Dean, department of education, Central University. since May 1928. Author and translator of several books. — p. 969

**MIAO Pin, (繆斌)** MIAO PIN, (繆斌).—Born 1899 in and native of Wusih, Kiangsu. Graduate, Nanyang University. 1924, radio instructor, Whampoa Military Academy, Canton. 1925-7. chief commissary officer, Nationalist 3rd division; chief of political bureau, Northern Expedition reserve regiments; member, Kiangsu Provincial Government. 1928, commissariat general, Commander-in-chief's headquarters. Director, department of Civil Affairs, Kiangsu Provincial Government, since November 1928. — p. 970

**MO Teh-hui, (莫德惠)** MO TEH-HUI, (莫德惠).—Native of Fengtien. 1924, vice-minister of agriculture and commerce. 1926, civil governor of Mukden. 1927-8. minister of agriculture and commerce. — p. 970

**MU Hsiang-yueh, (穆湘玥)** MU HSIANG-YUEH, (穆湘玥).—Courtesy name: Ou-ch'u ([illegible]初). Prefers to sign himself,: H. Y. Moh. Born 1877 in and native of Shanghai. 1913, B.S. in agriculture (Wisconsin). 1914, M.S. (Illinois). Managing director of Teh Dah Cotton Spinning Co. (Shanghai) and Hou Sung Cotton Spinning Co. (Chengchow, Honan) since 1916. 1920-6, chairman, cotton extension and improvement committee, Chinese Cotton Millowners' Association; president, Chinese Cotton Goods Exchange; member, Chinese Advisory Committee of Shanghai Municipal Council. Permanent Vice-Minister of Industry, Labour and Commerce, since October, 192[illegible]. — p. 970

**NA Yen-t'u, (那彥圖)** NA YEN-T'U, (那彥圖.—Mongol. Prince of 1st Order and Jassak of Khalka Mongols. 1896, superintendent of Peking Octroi. 1903, grand chamberlain. 1914, one of Mongol members in National Council and tartar general of Tarbagatai. 1922, pacification commissioner of Mongolia. — p. 970

**NAN Kuei-hsing, (南桂馨)** NAN KUEI-HSING, (南桂馨).—Courtesy name: P'ei-lan (佩[illegible]). Prefers English rendering: Q. S. Nan. Born 1884; native of Ningwu, Shansi. Graduate, Shansi University, and Tokyo Police Institute. 1916, acting defence commissioner of North Shansi. 1924-7, commissioner of police, Taiyuan. 1927, Governor Yen Hsi-shan's representative in Peking for special negotiations. 1928, mayor of Tientsin. — p. 970

**NAN, Q. S** NAN, Q. S., see above. — p. 970

**NEW, W. L** NEW, W. L., see NIU HUI-LIN. — p. 970

**NEW, W. S** NEW, W. S., see NIU HUI-SHENG. — p. 970

**NIEH Ch'i-chieh, (聶其杰)** NIEH CH'I-CHIEH. (聶其杰).—Courtesy name: Yün-t'ai (雲臺). Prefers English rendering: C. C. Nieh. Born 1880; native of Changsha (Hunan). At age of two began to reside at Shanghai. 1904, purchased the half-moribund Heng Foong Cotton Mill and, after six years, made it into a money-making concern; ten years later, it was giving employment to 1500 people and maintaining over a dozen scholarships in Nantungchow, France, England and U.S.A. 1918, organizer and president, Chinese Cotton Mill Owners' Association. General manager of Great China Cotton Mill since 1919. Co-founder, National Association for Vocational Education; and member, Chinese Advisory Committee of Shanghai Municipal Council. — p. 970

**NIEH Hsien-fan, (聶[illegible]藩)** NIEH HSIEN-FAN, (聶[illegible]藩).—Courtesy name: Wei-ch'eng (維城). Born 1880 in and native of Hofei, Anhwei. Studied in Tokyo Military College. 1913, defence commissioner, Chefoo. 1919, civil governor of Anhwei. 1922, commander-in-chief of Peking gendarmerie. — p. 970

**NIU Hui-lin, (牛惠霖)** NIU HUI-LIN, (牛惠霖).—Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: W. L. New (New Way-ling). Born 1887 in and native of Shanghai. Graduate, St. John's University, M.A., B.Ch. (Cantab.), M.R.C.S. (Eng.), L.R.C.P. (Lond.). 1917-27. medical superintendent, Chinese Red Cross General Hospital; consulting surgeon, Shantung Road Hospital; associate professor of operative surgery, St. John's University Medical School, Shanghai. In private practice and connected with many hospitals. Past president. National Medical Association of China and China Medical Association, Shanghai Branch. — p. 971

**NIU Hui-sheng, (牛惠生)** NIU HUI-SHENG, (牛惠生).—Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: W. S. New (New Way-sung). Born 1892 in and native of Shanghai. 1910, B.A. (St. John's). 1914, M.D. (Harvard). 1915-6, in charge of anatomy department. Harvard Medical School of China. 1918-20, assistant in orthopaedic surgery, Peking Union Medical College. Private practice in Shanghai since 1921, and connected with many hospitals. Instructor in orthopaedic surgery and applied anatomy. Women's Christian Union Medical College, and St. John's University Medical School. Past president, Shanghai Medical Society. — p. 971

**NIU Yung-chien, (鈕永建)** NIU YUNG-CHIEN, (鈕永建).—Courtesy name: T'i-sheng (惕生). Born 1887 in and native of Shanghai, Kiangsu. Studied in Wuchang military school. 1912, vice-minister, board of general staff, Nanking. 1917, chief of general staff, Canton. 1923, civil governor of Kiangsu, and commander-in-chief of Kiangsu revolutionary army. 1925, chief of staff, Nationalist 1st army, 1926, chief of staff, Commander-in-Chief's headquarters; chief secretary, Kuomintang Central Political Council. 1927, commander, Nationalist reorganized 7th army; chief secretary, Nationalist Government, Nanking; commissioner of civil affairs, Kiangsu. Chairman, Kiangsu Provincial Government, since October 1928; concurrently member, Legislative Yuan, and National Opium Suppression Commission. — p. 971

**Ouang, K. T** OUANG, K. T., see WANG CHI-TSENG. — p. 971

**OUYANG Keng, (歐陽庚)** OUYANG KENG, (歐陽庚).—Courtesy name: Shao-po (少伯). Prefers to sign himself: Owyang King. Native of Hsiangshan, Kwangtung. 1872, went with first batch of government students to U.S.A. 1881, Ph.B. (Yale). 1899-1910, consul in Vancouver, B.C. 1910-1917, consul general in Panama. 1919, first secretary, Legation in London. 1922-5, chargé d'Affaires in Santiago. — p. 971

**Owyang King** OWYANG KING, see above. — p. 971

**PAI Ch'ung-hsi, (白崇禧)** PAI CH'UNG-HSI, (白崇禧).—Courtesy name: Chien-sheng (健生). Born 1893; native of Kweilin, Kwangsi. Graduate, Paoting military academy. 1911, participated in revolutionary outbreak at Wuchang. 1926, commander, Nationalist 13th army; acting chief of general staff, Nationalist armies. 1927, defence commissioner of Shanghai and Woosung; commander, 2nd Route Army; assisted Li Tsung-jen and Ch'eng Ch'ien in ousting T'ang Sheng-chih from Hankow. 1928, second in command of 4th Group Army, resident in North China; member, Wuhan branch, Kuomintang Central Political Council. Generally regarded as the brain of the Kwangsi generals. March 1929, deprived of all posts upon defection of Kwangsi generals in Wuhan. — p. 971

**PAI Yuan-t'i, (白雲梯)** PAI YUAN-T'I, (白雲梯).—Courtesy name: Chü-ch'uan (巨川). Native of Kalasin, Inner Mongolia. Graduate, Peking College of Mongolian and Tibetan Languages. 1918, member, Canton Extraordinary Parliament. 1924, member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. 1927, Party organiser in Inner Mongolia. 1928, member, Government Council; member, Kuomintang Central Political Council. Permanent member, National Commission on Mongolian and Tibetan Affairs, since October 1928. — p. 971

**P'AN Ching-wu, (潘景武)** P'AN CHING-WU, (潘景武).—Courtesy name: Yen-sun (燕蓀). Prefers English rendering: T. W. Pan. Born 1898 in and native of Lintien, Heilungkiang. Graduate, Nankai University, Tientsin. 1926, English secretary to Ankuochun 3rd and 4th army corps. 1927, commissioner of education, Heilungkiang Province. Director, department of agriculture and mining, Heilungkiang Provincial Government, since February 1929. — p. 972

**P'AN Fu, (潘復)** P'AN FU, (潘復).—Courtesy name: Hsing-hang (馨航). Native of Tsining, Shantung. Chüjen of imperial examinations. 1913, industrial commissioner of Shantung. 1919-21, director general of Grand Canal Improvement Board; acting minister of finance. 1927-8, minister of finance and premier. — p. 972

**P'AN Lien-ju, (潘連茹)** P'AN LIEN-JU, (潘連茹).—Courtesy name: T'ai-ch'u. (泰初). Prefers English rendering: L. V. Pan. (Pan Lien-ven). Born 1887 in and native of Ping Yang, Shansi. 1910, Chinshih of imperial examinations. 1912-5, studied at Imperial College of Science, London. 1918-7, secretary, delegation at Paris Peace Conference. 1919-27, private secretary and delegate of Governor Yen Hsi-shan in Peking. Councellor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Nanking, and concurrently commissioner for foreign affairs, Kalgan, since September, 1928. — p. 972

**Pan, L. V** PAN, L. V., see above. — p. 972

**Pan, T. W** PAN, T. W., see PAN CHING-WU. — p. 972

**PAO Ch'i-yuan, (鮑啟元)** PAO CH'I-YUAN, (鮑啟元).—Courtesy name: Kuan-ch'un (冠[illegible]). Prefers to sign himself: Koschen C. Y. Bao. Born 1877; native of Chinkiang, Kiangsu.
