Graduate, Peiyang University. 1921, councillor, Aeronautical Department, Peking. 1925, managing director, Shanghai Telephone Company. — p. 972

**PAO Kuei-ch'ing, (鮑貴卿)** PAO KUEI-CH'ING, (鮑貴卿).—Courtesy name: T'ing-chiu (霆九). Native of Haich'eng, Fengtien. 1913, garrison commander, Wuhu (Anhwei). 1917, civil and military governor of Heilungkiang; director general, Chinese Eastern Railway. 1919, civil and military governor of Kirin. 1921 minister of war. 1924, reappointed director general, Chinese Eastern Railway. — p. 972

**PAO Ming-ch'ien, (鮑明鈐)** PAO MING-CH'IEN, (鮑明鈐).—Prefers English rendering: Mingchien Joshua Bau. Born 1894; native of Chinhsien, Chekiang. Graduate, Tsing Hua College. 1918, B.A. (Yale). 1919, M.A. (Columbia). 1921, Ph.D. (Johns Hopkins). 1922-8, head of English department, Peking National Teachers University; professor of political science in Peking Law College and National University; editor, Peking Daily News. Author of "The Foreign Relations of China" (doctoral dissertation); "The Open Door Doctrine;" "Modern Democracy in China;" "China and World Peace." — p. 972

**PAO Yu-ying, (包玉英)** PAO YU-YING, (包玉英).—Courtesy name: Tien-lun (殿綸). Native of Tant'u, Kiangsu. 1912, B.Sc. in chemistry (Toulouse). 1925, inspector of factories, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce. 1926, chief of labour section, same Ministry. 1927, director, department of labour, Ministry of Agriculture and Industry, Peiping. — p. 972

**Pei Tsuyee** PEI TSUYEE, see below. — p. 972

**PEI Tsu-yi, (貝祖詒)** PEI TSU-YI, (貝祖詒).—Courtesy name: Sung-sun (淞孫). Prefers English rendering: Tsuyee Pei. Born 1893; native of Soochow, Kiangsu. Connected with the Bank of China, for the last fifteen years. Shanghai Manager, same Bank, since 1927, committee member; 1928, councillor, Shanghai Municipal Council. — p. 972

**PIEN Shou-ch'ing, (邊守靖)** PIEN SHOU-CH'ING, (邊守靖).—Courtesy name: Chi-ch'ing (潔[illegible]). Born 1884 in and native of Chinghai, Chihli. Graduate, Tokyo Imperial University. 1912, speaker, Chihli provincial assembly. Director general, Ling Ching Mining Corporation. Proprietor, Ho Pei Jih Pao, Tientsin. — p. 972

**PIEN Shou-sun, (卞壽孫)** PIEN SHOU-SUN, (卞壽孫).—Courtesy name: Pai-mei (白眉). Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: Z. S. Bien (Bien Zue-sung). Born 1883; native of Icheng, Kiangsu. 1912, Ph.B. (Brown). 1914, chief auditor, Bank of China. 1916-20, chief secretary, Chung Foo Union Bank, Tientsin. 1922-24, manager, Bank of China, Peking. Tientsin Manager, same Bank, since 1925. — p. 973

**PIEN Yin-ch'ang, (卞蔭昌)** PIEN YIN-CH'ANG, (卞蔭昌).—Courtesy name: Yueh-t'ing (月庭). Born 1870 in and native of Tientsin. 1914, adviser to civil governor of Chihli. 1918, member of parliament. Sometime president, Tientsin Chinese general chamber of commerce. — p. 973

**PING K'e-chuang, (邴克莊)** PING K'E-CHUANG, (邴克莊).—Courtesy name: Ching-ju (敬如). Born 1885 in Mukden; native of P'anshan Liaoning (Fengtien). Graduate, Mukden police school. 1912, member of parliament. 1920, Taoyin of Ilan (Kirin) and commissioner for foreign affairs. 1924, commissioner for foreign affairs at Antung, and director-general, Yalu River Timber Co. Director, civil affairs department, Jehol Provincial Government, since December 1928. — p. 973

**PO Wen-wei, (柏文蔚)** PO WEN-WEI, (柏文蔚).—Courtesy name: Lieh-wu (烈武). Born 1875; native of Fengyang, Anhwei. Studied in Anhwei military academy. 1911 Revolution, elected regimental commander of Chinkiang army and played important part in capture of Nanking. 1912, Tutuh (military governor) of Anhwei. 1913, dismissed for participation in 2nd Revolution; elected Tutuh of Kiangsu. 1915, went to South Sea Islands to raise funds for Kuomintang Party. 1917, highest adviser to President Li Yuan-hung. 1921, councillor to Canton Government. 1927, member, Government Council, Nanking. Member, Huai River Improvement Commission, since 1928. — p. 973

**P'U Yi, (溥儀)** P'U YI, (溥儀).—Manchu ex-emperor Hsuan T'ung. Born 1906. Has adopted English name: Henry Pu Yi. Son of Prince Ch'un (Tsai Li) and nephew of emperor Kuang-hsü. November 14, 1908, succeeded to the Throne, under his father's regency. February 12, 1912, abdicated. July 1917, ascended the Throne upon Chang Hsun's attempt at coup d'état; but the Restoration lasted only a fortnight. Has been residing in Japanese Concession, Tientsin, since October 1924. — p. 973

**Quo Tai-chi** QUO TAI-CHI, see KUO T'AI-CH'I. — p. 973

**SAH Chen-ping, (薩鎮冰)** SAH CHEN-PING, (薩鎮冰).—Courtesy name: Ting-ming (鼎銘). Born 1858 in and native of Foochow, Fukien. Graduate, Foochow and Greenwich naval colleges. 1903-8, commander-in-chief of Peiyang and Kwangtung naval forces. 1910, accompanied Prince Tsai Hsun's naval mission to Europe and was knighted by King Edward VII. 1914-5, director general of arsenals. 1919, minister of navy. 1920, acting premier. 1921, made full admiral. 1922-6, special commissioner for suppression of opium and civil governor of Fukien. — p. 973

**SAH Fu-mou, (薩福楙)** SAH FU-MOU, (薩福楙).—Courtesy name: T'ung-sun (桐蓀). Born 1873 in and native of Foochow, Fukien. Graduate, Tientsin naval academy. 1906-7, and 1913, director, telegraph administration, and commissioner for foreign affairs, Canton. 1914, governor, Bank of China. 1917, commissioner for foreign affairs, Shanghai; custodian of enemy shipping. 1921, director, Shanghai Mint. — p. 973

**SHAO Li-tzu, (邵力子)** SHAO LI-TZU, (邵力子).—Courtesy name: Chung-hui (仲[illegible]). Native of Shaohsiang, Chekiang. Chüjen of imperial examinations. Member of Tung Meng Hui. Sometime editor-in-chief, Min Kuo Jih Pao (Shanghai). 1928, member, Kuomintang Central Political Council. Chief Secretary, Commander-in-Chief's headquarters since 1927. Member, Kuomintang Central Supervisory Committee, since 1926. — p. 973

**SHAO Yuan-ch'ung, (邵元冲)** SHAO YUAN-CH'UNG, (邵元冲).—Courtesy name: Yi-ju (翼如). Born 1890 in and native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. Studied in Chekiang provincial college, also Wisconsin, Columbia and London universities. 1916-8, private secretary to Dr. Sun Yat-sen. 1927, member, Shanghai provisional branch of Central Political Council; mayor of Hangchow. 1928, chief secretary, Kwangtung Provincial Government. Member, Legislative Yuan, since October 1928. March 1929, elected Member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. Author of several volumes in Chinese on labour conditions and Sun Yat-sen's principles. — p. 974

**SHEN Ch'i, (沈琪)** SHEN CH'I, (沈琪).—Courtesy name: Mu-han (穆涵). Born 1871 in and native of Chinghai, Chihli. Graduate of Peiyang Military College. 1912-27, chief technical expert, and director, railway department, Ministry of Communications; chairman, commission on railway technics. President, Chinese Institute of Engineers, for a number of years. — p. 974

**SHEN Chia-yi, (沈家彝)** SHEN CHIA-YI, (沈家彝).—Born 1881; native of Nanking, Kiangsu. Graduate, Tokyo Imperial University. 1913, chief judge, Peking Local Court. 1920, chief of division, Supreme Court. 1921-7, president, Peking High Court. Chief secretary, Municipal Government of Greater Peiping, since August 1928. — p. 974

**SHEN Jui-lin, (沈瑞麟)** SHEN JUI-LIN, (沈瑞麟).—Courtesy name: Yen-yi (硯裔). Prefers French rendering: Shen Soeu-ling. Born 1875 in and native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. 1910-7, minister to Austria-Hungary. 1922-4, vice-minister for foreign affairs. 1925-8, minister for foreign affairs; delegate and chairman, Customs Conference; minister of interior. — p. 974

**Shen Soeu-ling** SHEN SOEU-LING, see above. — p. 974

**SHEN Tzu-liang, (沈子良)** SHEN TZU-LIANG, (沈子良).—Courtesy name: Tzu-hsing (字行). Born 1884; native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. Graduate of a Swedish university. 1916, director, Canton-Samshui Railway. 1924, commissioner for foreign affairs, Hankow. 1925, director, Wuchang Mint. 1926, director, Hankow ex-German and ex-Russian concessions. — p. 974

**SHEN Yin-mo, (沈尹默)** SHEN YIN-MO, (沈尹默).—Born 1883; native of Wuhsing, Chekiang Studied in Tokyo Imperial University. 1913-27, professor of Chinese, Peking Government University . Member, Hopei Provincial Government, and principal, Conte College, Franco-Chinese University, since 1928. — p. 974

**SHENG Chien-mo, (盛建謨)** SHENG CHIEN-MO, (盛建謨).—Courtesy name: Chi-ying (季英). Prefers to sign himself: Sheng Kien-moo. Born 1896 in and native of Shanghai, Kiangsu. Graduate, Paoting Military College and Nanyuan Aviation School. 1926-8, flight commander, Chihli and Shantung armies' air service; councillor, aeronautical department, Ministry of Military Affairs. — p. 974

**SHIH Chao-chi, (施肇基)** SHIH CHAO-CHI, (施肇基).—Courtesy name: Chih-chih (植之). Prefers English rendering: Sao-ke Alfred Sze. Born 1876; native of Hangchow, Chekiang. 1901, B.A.; 1902, M.A. (Cornell). Honorary LL.D. (St. John's and Syracuse universities). Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. 1907-10, director, Peking-Hankow Railway; customs taotai, Harbin. 1912, minister of communications. 1913, grand master of ceremonies, President's Office. 1914-20, minister to Great Britain; delegate to Paris Peace Conference. 1921-8, minister to Washington; chief delegate, Washington Conference, delegate, Geneva Opium Conferences. Rappointed minister to Great Britain since February 1929. January—February 1923, minister of foreign affairs. Trustee, China Foundation for Promotion of Education and Culture, since 1925. Related by marriage to Mr. T'ang Shao-yi. — p. 974

**SHIH Chao-k'uei, (施肇夔)** SHIH CHAO-K'UEI, (施肇夔).—Courtesy name: Teh-ch'ien (德潛). Prefers to sign himself: Sze Chao-kuei. Born 1891; native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. LL.B. (Peiyang). M.A. (George Washington). Attorney-at-law. 1919-28, secretary, delegation to Washington Conference; secretary, Customs Conference and Ministry of Finance; senior clerk, department of political affairs, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Peiping. — p. 975

**SHIH Chao-tseng, (施肇曾)** SHIH CHAO-TSENG, (施肇曾).—Courtesy name: Sheng-chih (省之). Born 1868; native of Hangchow, Chekiang. 1897, superintendent, Hanyang Iron Works. 1907-9, managing director, Shanghai-Nanking Railway. 1910-1, director, Peking-Hankow Railway and China Merchants Steam Navigation Co. 1913-22, director-general, Lung-Hai and Tatung-Chengtu Railways. Promoter and patron, Central Hospital Peking. — p. 975

**SHIH Lu-pen, (施履本)** SHIH LU-PEN, (施履本).—Courtesy name: Ch'ang-ch'ing (長卿). Born 1880; native of Kiangyin, Kiangsu. 1909, Chüjen of imperial examinations. Studied in Japan. 1919-22, commissioner of foreign affairs for Shantung. 1923-4, chargé d'Affaires, Tokyo. 1924-8, assistant councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. — p. 975

**SHIH Shang-k'uan, (史尙寬)** SHIH SHANG-K'UAN, (史尙寬).—Courtesy name: Tsai-ch'in (在親). Born 1898 in and native of T'ungch'eng, Anhwei. 1922, LL.B. (Tokyo Imperial). Post-graduate studies in Germany and France. 1927, professor of law, Chungshan University, Canton. 1928, member, law drafting bureau, Canton branch of Kuomintang Central Political Council. Member, Legislative Yuan, since November 1928. — p. 975

**SHIH Shao-ch'ang, (施紹常)** SHIH SHAO-CH'ANG, (施紹常).—Courtesy name: Po-i (伯彝). Born 1872; native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. Chüjen of imperial examinations. 1915-9, consul general to Manila. 1920-5, director, department of political affairs. Ministry of Foreign Affairs. 1926-8, minister to Peru. Adviser, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, since March 1929. — p. 975

**SHIH Yu-ming, (史悠明)** SHIH YU-MING, (史悠明).—Courtesy name: Ai-shih (藹士). Prefers to sign himself: Iuming Suez. Born 1881; native of Ningpo, Chekiang. Graduate, St. John's University. 1911, superintendent of posts and telegraphs, Gyantze (Tibet). 1913-20, assistant director, department of political affairs, Ministry of Foreign Affairs; consul general in New York. 1921-8, chargé d'Affaires, Panama and Lima; assistant councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. — p. 975

**SHU Shuang-ch'uan, (舒雙全)** SHU SHUANG-CH'UAN, (舒雙全).—Courtesy name: Shou-ch'en (壽沈). Native of Tahsing, Hopei (Chihli). Graduate, Peking Military College. Sometime departmental chief, Ministry of Finance; superintendent, Shensi Arsenal; director of opium suppression, Honan; director-general of Peking-Octroi. Commissioner of Finance, Municipal Government of Greater Peiping, since July 1928. — p. 975

**SHUANG Ch'ing, (雙清)** SHUANG CH'ING, (雙清).—Courtesy name: Chih-ch'en (止澄). Born 1890; native of Kueiyang, Kweichow. Graduate, Kweichow Normal College. Participated in 1911 Revolution and 1916 campaign to oppose Yuan Shih-k'ai's monarchical scheme. 1920, councillor, Generalissimo's headquarters, Canton. 1927, councillor, Ministry of Communications, Nanking. Chief secretary and member, Chekiang Provincial Government, since October 1927. — p. 975

**SHUI Chun-shao, (水鈞韶)** SHUI CHUN-SHAO, (水鈞韶).—Courtesy name: Meng-keng (夢賡). Prefers English rendering: C. S. Shui. Born 1878 in and native of Funing, Kiangsi. Graduate, Paris Commercial College. Sometime director, Pien-Lo Railway, and associate managing director, Peking-Hankow, Peking-Suiyuan and Peking-Mukden Railways. 1925-6, 1st secretary, Embassy in Moscow. — p. 975

**SOONG, C. L** SOONG, C. L., see SUNG SHAN-LIANG. — p. 976

**SOONG, Mayleng** SOONG, MAYLENG, see SUNG MEI-LENG. — p. 976

**SOONG Tsung-faung** SOONG TSUNG-FAUNG, see SUNG CH'UN-FANG. — p. 976

**SOONG, T. V** SOONG, T. V., see SUNG TSU-WEN. — p. 976

**SU T'i-jen, (蘇體仁)** SU T'I-JEN, (蘇體仁).—Courtesy name: Hsiang-ch'ien (象乾). Born 1888; native of Sohsien, Shansi. Graduate, Shansi Normal College, and Tokyo Engineering College. Sometime councillor, Law Compilation Bureau; representative in Peiping of Governor Yen Hsi-shan. Commissioner for Foreign Affairs, Tientsin, since July 1928. — p. 976

**SUEZ, Iuming** SUEZ, IUMING, see SHIH YU-MING. — p. 976

**SUN Ching-ya, (孫鏡亞)** SUN CHING-YA, (孫鏡亞).—Courtesy name: Ch'ing-ch'en (靖塵). Prefers English rendering: K. A. Sun. Born 1886 in and native of Yungfeng, Kiangsi. Graduate, Fuhtan University, Shanghai. 1923, councillor, Generalissimo's headquarters, Canton; editor, Min Kuo Jih Pao, Canton. 1925-6, professor of literature, Chih Ta University, Shanghai. Member, Legislative Yuan, since December 1928. — p. 976

**SUN Ch'uan-fang, (孫傳芳)** SUN CH'UAN-FANG, (孫傳芳).—Courtesy name: Hsing-yuan ([illegible][illegible]). Born 1885; native of Linch'eng, Shantung. Graduate, Peiyang military academy; continued military education in Japan. 1923, Tuli (military governor) of Chekiang. 1925, Tupan (military governor) of Kiangsu. 1926-7, nominal overlord of Kiangsu, Kiangsi, Chekiang, Fukien and Anhwei. Now in Manchuria. — p. 976

**SUN, Clarence** SUN, CLARENCE. see SUN TO-YU. — p. 976

**SUN Feng-ts'ao, (孫鳳藻)** SUN FENG-TS'AO, (孫鳳藻).—Courtesy name: Tzu-wen (子文). Born [illegible] in and native of Tientsin, Chihli. Graduate, Peiyang University. Sometime adviser to the President and high inspecting commissioner of Chihli-Shantung-Honan. 1921-1922, commissioner of education for Shantung. 1923, director general, Tientsin-Pukow Railway. — p. 976

**SUN Fo** SUN FO, see SUN K'E. — p. 976

**SUN Huan-lun, (孫煥崙)** SUN HUAN-LUN, (孫煥崙).—Courtesy name: Yao-tz'u (要癡). Born 1885; native of Yut'ien. Sometime magistrate, Hungtung and Yangch'ü (Shansi), Taoyin of Chining, and chief of political affairs department, Nationalist Third Group Army. Director, department of civil affairs, Hopei Provincial Government, since August 1928. — p. 976

**SUN Hung-i, (孫洪伊)** SUN HUNG-I, (孫洪伊).—Courtesy name: Po-lan (伯蘭). Born 1870; native of Tientsin, Chihli. 1912, member of parliament. 1916, minister of education and of interior. Offered portfolio in Canton Military Government but did not accept. — p. 976

**SUN, K. A** SUN, K. A. see SUN CHING-YA. — p. 976

**SUN K'e, (孫科)** SUN K'E, (孫科).—Courtesy name: Cheh-sheng (哲生). Prefers anglicized Cantonese rendering: Sun Fo. Son of Dr. Sun Yat-sen. Born 1891 in and native of Chungshan, Kwangtung. 1916, B.A. (California). 1917, M.S. (Columbia). 1921-2, mayor of Canton and director-general of Kwangtung river conservancy. 1923-4, reappointed mayor of Canton. 1925-6, commissioner of reconstruction and chairman, Kwangtung Provincial Government. 1926-7, again concurrently mayor of Canton. Member of Nationalist Government Council from its organization in July 1925 to its reorganization, October 1928. Member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee and Central Political Council, since 1926. November 1926-August 1927, minister of communications, Canton and Hankow. October-December, 1927, minister of finance, Nanking. State Councillor, concurrently Vice-President of the Examination Yuan, and Minister of Railways, since October 1928. President, National Aviation Corporation, since January 1929. — p. 976

**SUN Pao-ch'i, (孫寶琦)** SUN PAO-CH'I, (孫寶琦).—Courtesy name: Mu-han (慕韓). Born 1867 in and native of Hangchow, Chekiang. 1902, minister to France. 1906, governor of Peking. 1907-8, minister to Germany. 1909-11, governor of Shantung. 1912, and 1923, director general of customs revenue administration. 1913-4, minister for foreign affairs. 1924, premier. 1925, appointed ambassador to Russia but did not proceed. President, Hanyehping Company; director, China Merchants Steam Navigation Co. — p. 977

**SUN To-yu, (孫多玉)** SUN TO-YU, (孫多玉).—Courtesy name: Chang-fu (章甫). Better known as: Clarence Sun. Born 1876 in and native of Shouhsien, Anhwei. 1909, C. E. (Cornell). Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. 1911-21, managing director of Kirin-Changchun, Nanking-Changsha, Shanghai-Nanking, Shanghai-Hangchow-Ningpo, Chu-Ching, Chow-hsiang and Hupeh-Hunan Railways. 1923-4, vice-minister of communications. Sometime president, Hui Tung Industrial Corporation. President, Chung Foo Union Bank, Tientsin, since 1924. — p. 977

**SUN Yat-sen, Mrs** SUN YAT-SEN, MRS., see SUNG CH'ING-LING. — p. 977

**SUN Yuan-fang, (孫元方)** SUN YUAN-FANG, (孫元方).—Courtesy name: Ching-hsi (景西). Prefers English rendering: Y. Ralph Sun. Born 1883 in Nanking; native of Shouhsien, Ahwei. 1912, Ph.B. (Brown). 1914-5, assistant manager, Bank of China, Hankow. Manager, Fou Feng Flour Mill, and managing director, T'ung Feng Flour Mill, Shanghai, since 1916. Manager, Chung Foo Union Bank, Shanghai, since 1917. Director, Shanghai Flour Mills Association. Committee member, Shanghai General Chamber of Commerce and Bankers Association as well as Chinese Ratepayers Association. — p. 977

**SUN, Y. Ralph** SUN, Y. RALPH, see above. — p. 977

**SUN Yun-yu, (孫潤宇)** SUN YUN-YU, (孫潤宇). Courtesy name: , Tzu-han (子涵). Born 1880 in and native of Soochow, Kiangsu. Chüjen of imperial examinations. Graduate, Tokyo, Imperial Law College. 1913-22, member of parliament. 1914, 1st secretary, Legation in Tokyo. 1924-7, chief, Law Compilation Bureau; chief secretary to the Cabinet. — p. 977

**SUNG Cheh-yuan, (宋哲元)** SUNG CHEH-YUAN, (宋哲元).—Courtesy name: Min-hsien (明軒). Born 1885. 1925, commander, 11th division; military governor of Jehol. 1926, commander, Kuominchun reorganized 1st division. 1927, commander, 4th army corps, Nationalist 2nd Group Army. Chairman, Shensi Provincial Government, since November 1927; concurrently commander, Nationalist 28th army, and commander-in-chief for the suppression of bandits in Shensi Province. — p. 977

**SUNG Ch'ing-ling, (宋慶齡)** SUNG CH'ING-LING, (宋慶齡). Born 1890; native of Shanghai, Kiangsu. Educated at McTyiere School, Shanghai. 1915, B.A. (Wesleyan). 1916, married to Dr. Sun Yat-sen. January 1927, followed the Nationalist Government to Hankow. September 1927-May 1929, resided in Europe. Elected member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee, since 1926. A bitter critic of the Nanking Government. — p. 977

**SUNG Ch'un-fang, (宋春舫)** SUNG CH'UN-FANG, (宋春舫).—Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: Soong Tsung-faung. Born 1891 in and native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. 1915, M.A. (Geneva). 1916-21, lecturer on modern languages, St. John's University; professor of French literature, Tsing Hua College and Peking National University. 1922-3, secretary, Ministry of Finance. Author of "Parcourant le Monde en Flammes," "La Literature Chinois Contemporaine," etc. — p. 977

**SUNG, Fartsan T** SUNG, FARTSAN T., see below. — p. 977

**SUNG Fa-hsiang, (宋發祥)** SUNG FA-HSIANG, (宋發祥).—Courtesy name: Chih-ch'ang (致長). Prefers English rendering: Fartsan T. Sung. Born 1883 in and native of Hsinghua, Fukien. 1911, B.Sc. (Michigan). 1913-20, inspector of mints, Ministry of Finance; director, Soochow and Nanking mints. 1925-8, managing director, Sino-Scandinavian Bank, Peking. Consul General to Australia since November 1928. Author of "Qualitative Analysis of Important Metals." — p. 977

**SUNG Han-chang, (宋漢章)** SUNG HAN-CHANG, (宋漢章).—Born 1872; native of Yüyao, Chekiang. Studied in Shanghai Anglo-Chinese College. 1911-27, manager, Bank of China, Shanghai. 1925-8, chairman, Shanghai Chinese general chamber of commerce, Chinese Bankers Association, and member, Chinese Advisory Committee of Shanghai Municipal Council. — p. 978

**SUNG Mei-ling, (宋美齡)** SUNG MEI-LING, (宋美齡).—Sister of Madame Sun Yat-sen. Born 1892. 1917, B.A. (Wellesley). 1927, married to General Chiang Kai-shek. Member, Legislative Yuan, since October, 1928. — p. 978

**SUNG Shan-liang, (宋善良)** SUNG SHAN-LIANG, (宋善良).—Courtesy name: Chiao-lin (椒臨). Prefers English rendering: C. L. Soong. Born 1882; native of Chekiang. 1920-8, secretary, Legation in Berne and Madrid. Chargé d'Affaires at Rio de Janiero, since September 1928. — p. 978

**SUNG Shou-heng, (宋壽恒)** SUNG SHOU-HENG, (宋壽恒).—Courtesy name: Tseh-chiu (則久). Born 1867 in and native of Tientsin, Chihli. Chairman, National Goods Maintenance Association, since 1912. Manager, National Goods Department Store, Tientsin, since 1913. Director, Tientsin Chinese general chamber of commerce, since 1924. Has established six schools in Tientsin at own expense. — p. 978

**SUNG Tsu-wen, (宋子文)** SUNG TSU-WEN, (宋子文).—Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: T. V. Soong. Brother of Madame Sun Yat-sen. Born 1891; native of Shanghai. Studied at St. John's University. 1915, A.B. in economics (Harvard). 1915-7, assistant in International Banking Corporation and Macdonell-Chow Corporation, New York. 1924, president, Central Bank, Canton. 1925-7, minister of finance, Canton Government. Reappointed Minister of Finance, since May 1928. Concurrently member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee and Central Political Council, and president, Central Bank, Shanghai. — p. 978

**SZE Chao-kuei** SZE CHAO-KUEI, see SHIH CHAO-K'UEI. — p. 978

**SZE, Sao Ke Alfred** SZE, SAO KE ALFRED, see SHIH CHAO-CHI. — p. 978

**TAI Ch'ao, (戴超)** TAI CH'AO, (戴超).—Courtesy name: Chih-ch'ien (志騫). Prefers to sign himself: T. C. Tai, (Tse-chien Tai). Born 1888 in and native of Tsingpu, Kiangsu. 1912, B.A. (St. John's). 1918, Bachelor of Library Science (New York State Library School). 1924, Ph.D. (Iowa). 1909-14, Librarian, St. John's University. 1918-9, assistant librarian, Camp Upton Library, New York. 1914-28, librarian, Tsing Hua College, Peiping. 1928, director, National Central University Library, Nanking. January-April 1929, director of higher education, Kiangsu University District, Nanking. Vice-Chancellor, National Central University, Nanking, since May, 1929. 1923, Order of Knighthood of Dannebroge conferred by the King of Denmark for special services. 1922-6, president, Peking Library Association. 1925-6, chairman, Library Association of China. 1926-9, member, board of management, Metropolitan Library, Peiping. 1928-9, member, Commission for editing the "Gazetteer of Kiangsu Province". Author of "Professional Education for Librarianship" (doctoral dissertation) and "Library Movement in China". — p. 978

**TAI Ch'en-lin, (戴陳霖)** TAI CH'EN-LIN, (戴陳霖).—Courtesy name: Yü-nung (雨農). Native of Chekiang. Graduate, Paris Ecole des sciences politiques. 1912, councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. 1916-20, minister to Spain. 1922-5, minister to Sweden, Norway and Denmark. — p. 978

**TAI Chi-t'ao** TAI CHI-T'AO, see below. — p. 978

**TAI Ch'uan-hsien, (戴傳賢)** TAI CH'UAN-HSIEN, (戴傳賢).—Courtesy name: Chi-t'ao (季陶) or T'ien-ch'o (天仇). Born 1882 in Szechwan; native of Chekiang. Studied in Japan. 1908, associate editor, Min Li Pao, Shanghai. 1912, editor, Min Ch'uan Pao. 1913, secretary to Dr. Sun Yat-sen in Japan. 1923, instructor, Whampoa Military Academy. State Councillor and President, Examination Yuan, since October 1928. Member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee and Central Political Council, since 1926. Concurrently chancellor, Kwangtung National University, Canton. — p. 979

**T'AN P'ing-shan, (譚平山)** T'AN P'ING-SHAN, (譚平山).—Born 1887; native of Kwangtung. 1920, graduate, Peking National University. Forms with late Li Ta-chao of Peking and Ch'en Tu-hsiu of Shanghai the notorious Communist Trio. 1924-5, active figure in general strike at Canton; member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee, and head of farmers and workers' bloc. March 1926, deprived of all power. Head of Chinese Communists and Canton representative on the council of Communist International. July 1927, resigned from Nationalist Government at Hankow. — p. 979

**T'AN Yen-k'ai, (譚延闓)** T'AN YEN-K'AI, (譚延闓).—Courtesy name: Tsu-yen (組庵). Born 1876; native of Chalin, Hunan. Chinshih and Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. 1910, chairman, Hunan provincial assembly. 1912 and 1916, military governor of Hunan. 1919, reappointed Tuchun and concurrently civil governor, same province. June 1922, appointed minister of interior, Peking, but declined. 1926, commander, Northern Expedition Second Army. 1927, chairman, Nationalist Government Council, Nanking. State Councillor and President, Executive Yuan, since October 1928. Concurrently member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee and Central Political Council. — p. 979

**T'ANG Sheng-chih, (唐生智)** T'ANG SHENG-CHIH, (唐生智).—Born 1885; native of Hunan. Grandfather served under Tseng Kuo-fan. Graduate, Paoting mlitary academy. 1920, divisional commander in Hunan. Adherent of Wu P'ei-fu until 1926, when he wrested Hunan from Chao Heng-t'i. 1927, driven out of Wuhan by Li Tsung-jen, Pai Ch'ung-hsi and Ch'eng Ch'ien. March 1929, appointed commander, 5th Route Army, in North China, upon defection of Kwangsi generals in Wuhan. May 1929, appointed chairman, National Military Council. — p. 979

**T'ANG Shao-yi, (唐紹儀)** T'ANG SHAO-YI, (唐紹儀).—Courtesy name: Shao-ch'uan (少川). Born 1860 in and native of Hsiangshan, Kwangtung. 1873, went to U.S.A. with first batch of government students. Attended Columbia University but was recalled before graduation. 1896, consul general in Korea. 1904-5, special envoy to India to negotiate Tibet Convention. 1906, director general, Shanghai-Nanking Railway and customs revenue administration. 1907, first governor of Fengtien. 1908, special envoy to thank Washington Government for remission of Boxer indemnity. 1910-1, president, board of communications; Yuan Shih-k'ai's delegate to negotiate with revolutionary leaders in Shanghai. 1912, first premier of of the Republic; superior adviser to the President. 1918, one of four administrative directors, Canton Military Government. 1919-22, chief delegate of Canton Government to Shanghai peace conference, minister of finance, Canton. Living in retirement in Shanghai or native district in Kwangtung since 1923. High Adviser of the National Government since January 1929. — p. 979

**T'ANG T'ieh-ch'iao, (湯鐵樵)** T'ANG T'IEH-CH'IAO, (湯鐵樵).—Courtesy name: Yün-shao (芸邵). Prefers to sign himself: Tong Tik-chaw. Born 1880 in and native of Liling, Hunan. Graduate, Waseda University (Tokyo). 1920 and 1925, acting vice-minister of justice. 1927, councillor, Ministry of Justice. — p. 979

**T'ANG Tsai-chang, (唐在章)** T'ANG TSAI-CHANG, (唐在章).—Courtesy name: Po-wen (伯文). Born 1884; native of Shanghai. Educated in Japan. 1912, secretary to the President and Cabinet. 1921-8, councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs; technical expert, Customs Conference; director, Peking Tramway Company. — p. 980

**T'ANG Tsai-fu, (唐在復)** T'ANG TSAI-FU, (唐在復).—Courtesy name: Hsin-yü (新畬). Born 1877; native of Shanghai. Graduate, Paris University. 1915-25, minister to Holland and Italy; delegate to League of Nations Assembly and representative on League of Nations Council. 1926-7, director, compilation bureau, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. — p. 980

**T'ANG Tsai-li, (唐在禮)** T'ANG TSAI-LI, (唐在禮).—Courtesy name: Chih-fu (質夫). Born 1882; native of Shanghai. Graduate, Tokyo Military Academy. 1914-6, chief of general staff. 1918-9, delegate to Allied Military Council, Paris; chief military delegate to Paris Conference. Knighted (K.B.E.) by H. M. King George. 1923-6, director general of railway guards, Ministry of Communications. — p. 980

**T'ANG Yu-lin, (湯玉麟)** T'ANG YU-LIN, (湯玉麟).—1926-8, Tutung (civil and military governor) of Charhar and Jehol. Chairman, Jehol Provincial Government, since January 1929. — p. 980

**T'ANG Yueh-liang, (唐悅[illegible])** T'ANG YUEH-LIANG, (唐悅[illegible]).—Born 1890 in Shanghai; native of Hsiangshan, Kwangtung. Studied at St. John's University. 1913, B.A. (Yale). 1915, M.A. (Princeton). 1915-8, lecturer, Tsing Hua College. 1918, secretary, Peking Y.M.C.A. 1921-2, secretary, delegation to Washington Conference; third secretary, Legation in Havana. 1924-6, assistant councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs; director, diplomatic bureau, headquarters of Northwestern defence. 1926-7, commissioner for foreign affairs, Chengchow (Honan). Permanent Vice-Minister for Foreign Affairs, since March 1928. — p. 980

**T'AO Chia-yao, (陶家瑤)** T'AO CHIA-YAO, (陶家瑤).—Born 1871; native of Kiangsi. Sometime member of parliament. 1923, civil governor of Kiangsi; director general of Kiukiang Port Construction. — p. 980

**T'AO, L. K** T'AO, L. K., see below. — p. 980

**T'AO Lu-kung, (陶履恭)** T'AO LU-KUNG, (陶履恭).—Courtesy name: Meng-ho (孟和).—Prefers English rendering: L. K. Tao. Born 1887; native of Tientsin. 1913, B.Sc. (London). 1914-25, professor of social and political sciences and dean of faculties, Peking Normal College; professor of sociology, Peking National University. Research secretary, China Foundation for Promotion of Education and Culture, Peking, since 1925. Joint author of "Village and Town Life in China." — p. 980

**TENG Chih-yi, (鄧植儀)** TENG CHIH-YI, (鄧植儀).—Born 1888 in and native of Shunteh, Kwangtung. 1914, M.S. in agriculture (Wisconsin). Sometime dean, College of Agriculture and Forestry, Chungshan University, Canton. Director of Kwangsi Institute for Development of Industries since 1927; concurrently associate director-general of Kwangsi Industrial Exposition to be held October 1929. — p. 980

**TENG Hsi-hou, (鄧錫侯)** TENG HSI-HOU, (鄧錫侯).—Courtesy name: Chin-k'ang (晋康). Prefers English rendering: S. H. Den. Born 1891 in and native of Yingshan, Szechwan. Graduate, Paoting military academy. 1920, commander, Szechwan 6th division. 1921, commander, Szechwan 4th army. 1923, commander, 30th division. 1924, civil governor of Szechwan. Commander, Nationalist 28th army, and commander-in-chief, 7th Route Army, since 1928. Concurrently member, Szechwan Provincial Government and director of civil affairs department; chairman, commission for the reorganization of troops in Western Szechwan. — p. 980

**TENG I-hua, (鄧[illegible]華)** TENG I-HUA, (鄧[illegible]華).—Courtesy name: Yü-hsi (宇熙). Born 1883 in and native of Chungking, Szechwan. Chüjen of imperial examinations. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. 1911, regimental commander, Chekiang revolutionary army, and staff officer to Tutuh (military governor) of Kiangsu. 1925-7, departmental chief, Ministry of War. Translator of a military manual, in two volumes. — p. 981

**TENG Shou-ch'uan, (鄧壽荃)** TENG SHOU-CH'UAN, (鄧壽荃).—Born 1888; native of Anhwei. Educated in Hunan Polytechnic Institute; went to Japan to specialize in mining and metallurgy. 1910, member, Hunan provincial assembly. 1912, manager, Hunan provincial lead-refining factory; superintendent, Shui-kou-shan Mining Corporation. 1926-8, commissioner of finance and construction, same Province. — p. 981

**TENG Tseh-ju, (鄧澤如)** TENG TSEH-JU, (鄧澤如).—Native of Kwangtung. 1923, salt commissioner for Kwangtung Province. Member, Kuomintang Central Supervisory Committee, since 1924. 1926, member, Kwangtung Provincal Government. 1928, member, Chinese Overseas Commission; member, Kuomintang Central Political Council. — p. 981

**TENG Ts'ui-ying (鄧萃英)** TENG TS'UI-YING (鄧萃英).—Courtesy name: Chih-yuan (芝園). Prefers English rendering: T. Y. Teng. Born 1886; native of Foochow, Fukien. Graduate, Columbia Teachers College. 1921-6, acting president, Amoy University; technical expert, delegation to Washington Conference, acting vice-minister of education. 1927-8, commissioner of education, Honan. Joint author of "Education in China." — p. 981

**TENG Yen-ta, (鄧演達)** TENG YEN-TA, (鄧演達).—Born 1891; native of Kwangtung. 1916, graduate, Paoting Military Academy. Spent two or three years in Russia and Germany. 1923, returned to Canton; appointed regimental commander. 1924, turned Communist. 1926, deprived of all military power. Subsequently chief, political bureau of Northern Expedition Army. A rabid Communist leader of Nationalist Government at Hankow. July, 1927, proscribed by National Government. — p. 981

**TENG Yen-ts'un, (鄧演存)** TENG YEN-TS'UN, (鄧演存).—Courtesy name: Chingsheng (競生). Born 1888; native of Weiyang, Kwangtung. Graduate, Peiyang military college. 1926, chief of staff, Northern Expedition 4th ("Iron") army. 1927-8, director-general, Hanyang Arsenal. — p. 981

**TIAO Hsin-teh, (刁信德)** TIAO HSIN-TEH, (刁信德).—Prefers English rendering: Edward Sintuk Tyau. Born 1879 in and native of Hsingning, Kwangtung. Graduate, St. John's University. M.D. (Pennsylvania). D.P.H. (Liverpool). 1905-8, medical officer to Chinese labour camps in Transvaal, South Africa. Senior medical officer, St. Luke's Hospital, Shanghai, with which he has been connected since 1902. Past president, National Medical Association, and editor, National Medical Journal. — p. 981

**TIAO Min-ch'ien, (刁敏謙)** TIAO MIN-CH'IEN, (刁敏謙).—Courtesy name: Teh-jen (德仁). Prefers English rendering: M. T. Z. Tyau. Born 1888, in and native of Hsingning, Kwangtung. Graduate, St. John's University. 1914, LL.B.; 1916, LL.D. (London). 1916-22, lecturer on international law, Tsing Hua College; founder and editor, Peking Leader; technical expert, delegation to League of Nations Assembly; secretary, delegation to Washington Conference. 1923-8, secretary, Ministry of Foreign Affairs; assistant director, drafting department, Customs Conference. Councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, since June 1929. Managing editor, The Chinese Social and Political Science Review, Peking, since October 1922. Author of "The Legal Obligations arising out of Treaty Relations between China and Other States" (doctoral dissertation), "London Through Chinese Eyes" (London) and "China Awakened" (New York), etc. — p. 981

**TIAO Tso-ch'ien. (刁作謙)** TIAO TSO-CH'IEN. (刁作謙).—Courtesy name: Ch'eng-chang (成章). Prefers English rendering: Philip K. C. Tyau. Born 1880 in and native of Hsingning, Kwangtung. Graduate of St. John's University. M.A.; LL.B. (Cantab.); barrister-at-law (Middle Temple, London). Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. 1909-10, director general of educational mission to Europe. 1912-4, 1st secretary and officiating consul general, in London. 1917-20, councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs; secretary, Cabinet Office. 1921-6, secretary general, delegation to Washington Conference; minister to Cuba and Panama. Adviser to Ministry of Foreign Affairs, since October 1928. Sometimes tennis champion of North China. — p. 982

**T'IEN Yin-huang, (田應璜)** T'IEN YIN-HUANG, (田應璜).—Courtesy name: Tzu-tsung (子琮). Born 1866; native of Hunyuan, Shansi. Sometime senator and chancellor of Shansi University. 1924, appointed minister of interior, but declined. — p. 982

**TING, Charles C** TING, CHARLES C., see TING HUNG-CH'UAN. — p. 982

**TING Ch'ao-wu, (丁超五)** TING CH'AO-WU, (丁超五).—Courtesy name: Li-fu (立夫). Born 1884; native of Shaowu, Fukien. 1912, member of parliament. 1927, councillor to Nationalist Government; member, Foochow branch of Kuomintang Central Political Council; director, reconstruction department, Fukien Provincial Government. 1928, member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee and Central Political Council. Member, Fukien Provincial Government, since 1928. March 1929, elected reserve member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. — p. 982

**TING Hung-ch'uan, (丁宏荃)** TING HUNG-CH'UAN, (丁宏荃).—Courtesy name: Chen-chih (振芝). Prefers English rendering: Charles C. Ting. Born 1874; native of Kiangsu. Graduate, Foochow naval college. 1915, chief of Tientsin police. 1922, director, ex-German Concession administration. 1926, commissioner of police for Chihli. — p. 982

**TING, S. Y. W** TING, S. Y. W., see below. — p. 982

**TING Shih-yuan, (丁士源)** TING SHIH-YUAN, (丁士源).—Courtesy name: Wen-ch'a ([illegible]槎). Prefers English rendering: S. Y. W. Ting. Born 1879; native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. Studied in Shanghai and England. 1914-6, commissioner for foreign affairs, Hankow. 1918-20, director general of Peking-Hankow and Peking-Suiyuan railways; took refuge in Japanese Legation upon collapse of Anfu Party. 1925-6, vice-chairman, commission for readjustment of finance, Peiping. — p. 982

**TING Wei-fen, (丁惟汾)** TING WEI-FEN, (丁惟汾).—Courtesy name: Ting-ch'en (鼎臣). Born 1874; native of Jihchao, Shantung. Graduate, Meiji University, Japan. 1912-3, member of parliament. Member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee, since 1924. 1928, director of training, Central Kuomintang Headquarters. Member, Shantung Provincial Government, since October 1928. — p. 982

**TING, V. K** TING, V. K., see below. — p. 982

**TING Wen-chiang, (丁文江)** TING WEN-CHIANG, (丁文江).—Prefers English rendering: V. K. Ting. Born 1887; native of Kiangsu. Studied in Cambridge. B.Sc. (Glasgow); D.Sc. (Freiburg). 1913-21, director, Geological Survey, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce. 1922-5, general manager, Peipiao Coal Mining Company, Tientsin. 1925-8, trustee, China Foundation for Promotion of Education and Culture; one of three Chinese members, British Boxer Indemnity Advisory Committee; managing director, Shanghai and Woosung Port Administration and mayor of Greater Shanghai. Author of "Geology of the Yangtze Delta," "Fifty Years of Mining in China," etc. — p. 982

**TONG, Hollington K** TONG, HOLLINGTON K., see TUNG HSIEN-KUANG. — p. 982

**TONG Shao-yi** TONG SHAO-YI, see T'ANG SHAO-YI. — p. 982

**TONG Tik-chaw** TONG TIK-CHAW, see T'ANG T'IEH-CH'IAO. — p. 982

**TS'AI Ch'eng-hsun, (蔡成勳)** TS'AI CH'ENG-HSUN, (蔡成勳).—Courtesy name: Hu-ch'en (虎臣). Born 1873; native of Tientsin. Graduate, Peiyang Military College. 1914-6, commander, 1st division. 1917, Tutung (civil and military governor) of Charhar. 1921, minister of war. 1922-5, civil and military governor of Kiangsi. — p. 983

**TSAI Hsun, (載洵)** TSAI HSUN, (載洵).—Manchu Prince. Brother of deceased emperor Kuang-hsü. 1909-10, high commissioner for naval reorganization and head of naval mission to Europe, U.S.A., and Japan. 1911, minister of navy. — p. 983

**TS'AI P'ei, (蔡培)** TS'AI P'EI, (蔡培).—Courtesy name: Tzu-p'ing (子平). Born 1884; native of Wusih, Kiangsu. LL.B. (Waseda). Sometime member of parliament; acting director navigation department, Ministry of Communications. Councillor, Ministry of Communications, since May 1928. — p. 983

**TSAI T'ao, (載濤)** TSAI T'AO, (載濤).—Manchu Prince. Brother of deceased emperor Kuang-hsü. 1909, chief of general staff. 1910, head of military mission to Japan, America and Europe. — p. 983

**TS'AI T'ing-kan, (蔡廷幹)** TS'AI T'ING-KAN, (蔡廷幹).—Courtesy name: Yao-t'ang ([illegible]堂). Born 1861 in and native of Hsiangshan, Kwangtung. 1873, one of first batch of government students to U.S.A.; recalled before completing his studies. Graduate, Taku Torpedo School. 1912-6, chief English secretary to President Yuan Shih-kai. 1918-22, master of ceremonies, President's Office; chairman, International Commission for Revision of Tariff, Shanghai; adviser, delegation to Washington Conference. 1924-6, promoted vice-admiral; director general, customs revenue administration; delegate, Customs Conference; chairman, commission to investigate Shanghai Incident of May 30, 1925. Summer of 1926, concurrently minister for foreign affairs. May 1927, retired. Past president, Peking Rotary Club. Res. Dairen. — p. 983

**TSAI Tseh, (載澤)** TSAI TSEH, (載澤).—Duke, Manchu. 1901, deputy lieutenant general, Plain Blue Banner. 1905, head of mission to study constitutional systems of government in Europe, U.S.A. and Japan. 1906, comptroller of imperial armoury. 1907, minister of the presence; minister of finance. 1908, promoted prince of 4th order. 1909, commissioner for naval reorganization. — p. 983

**TS'AI Yuan-p'ei, (蔡元培)** TS'AI YUAN-P'EI, (蔡元培).—Courtesy name: Chieh-min (孑民). Born 1867; native of Shanying, Chekiang. One of most influential Kuomintang "Elder Statesmen." Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. Studied in Germany and France. Founder and principal, Shanghai Aikuo Girls' School. 1912, minister of education, Nanking, and Peking. 1917-23, chancellor, Peking National University. 1923-5, travelled in Europe and America. 1927, minister of education, Nanking Nationalist Government. State Councillor and President, Control Yuan, since October 1928. President, National Research Institute, since autumn 1928. Member, Kuomintang Central Supervisory Committee, since 1924. Member, Kuomintang Central Political Council, since 1926. Author of three volumes on history of Chinese ethics, outline of philosophy, and historic reference in "Dream of the Red Chamber;" translator of several German treatises. — p. 983

**TS'AO Ching-yuan, (曹經沅)** TS'AO CHING-YUAN, (曹經沅). Courtesy name: Hsiang-heng (纕蘅). Born 1891 in and native of Mienchu, Szechwan. Graduate, Chung Hua University, Peking. Sometime secretary, Cabinet Office. 1927-8, councillor, Ministry of Interior. — p. 983

**TS'AO Ju-lin, (曹汝霖)** TS'AO JU-LIN, (曹汝霖).—Courtesy name: Yün-t'ien (潤田). Born 1875: native of Shanghai. Graduate, Tokyo Imperial University Chinshih of imperial examinations. 1913, senator. 1913-6, vice-minister for foreign affairs. 1917-9, minister of communications and acting minister of finance. Branded during Student Movement (May 1919) together with Chang Tsung-hsiang and Lu Tsung-yü, as "national traitors" for negotiation of Nishihara Loans. Past president, Bank of Communications, and managing director, Exchange Bank of China. — p. 983

**TS'AO K'un, (曹錕)** TS'AO K'UN, (曹錕).—Courtesy name: Chung-shan (仲珊). Born 1862; native of Tientsin, Chihli. Graduate, Peiyang Military College. 1912, commander, 3rd division. 1917-23, Tuchun (military governor) of Chihli; co-operated with Marshal Tuan Ch'i-jui to suppress attempted Manchu Restoration, and with Chang Tso-lin to dissolve Anfu Party; inspecting commissioner of Chihli, Shantung and Honan. October 1923—November 1924, president of the Republic. October 1924—April 1926, prisoner in the Presidency during Kuominchun occupation of Peking. Resigned on regaining freedom. — p. 984

**TS'AO Yu-lin** TS'AO YU-LIN, see TSAO JU-LIN. — p. 984

**TS'AO Yun-siang, (曹雲祥)** TS'AO YUN-SIANG, (曹雲祥).—Courtesy name: Ch'ing-wu (慶五). Prefers English rendering: Y. S. Tsao. Born 1881; native of Hsiushui, Chekiang. Graduate, St. John's University. 1911, B.A. (Yale); 1914, M.B.A. (Harvard). 1914-21, 2nd secretary, and officiating consul general in London chargé d'affaires, Copenhagen; associate secretary general, delegation to Washington Conference. 1922-7, councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs; president, Tsing Hua College. Chinese Representative, British-American Tobacco Company, since 1928. — p. 984

**TS'EN Ch'un-hsuan, (岑春煊)** TS'EN CH'UN-HSUAN, (岑春煊).—Courtesy name: Yun-chieh (雲階). Born 1859; native of Hsiling, Kwangsi. 1900-1, governor of Shensi and Shansi. 1902-6, governor of Kwangtung; acting viceroy of Szechwan, Kwangtung and Kwangsi 1907, president, Board of Communications. 1911, ordered to Szechwan to suppress agitation against nationalization of railways, but Revolution broke out while still in Hankow. 1913, director general of Canton Hankow Railway. 1915, elected commander-in-chief of Kwangtung and Kwangsi forces to oppose Yuan Shih-kai's monarchical scheme. 1918, presiding administrative director, and minister of interior, Canton Military Government. Living in retirement in Shanghai since 1920. — p. 984

**TSENG Kwang-hsiang, (曾廣勷)** TSENG KWANG-HSIANG, (曾廣勷).—Born 1885; native of Kwangtung. B.S. and M.A. (Pennsylvania). 1912, secretary to premier T'ang Shao-yi. 1915, chief of division, Ministry of Communications. 1920-6, rose to managing director, Peking-Mukden Railway. — p. 984

**TSENG Tsung-chien, (曾宗鑒)** TSENG TSUNG-CHIEN, (曾宗鑒).—Courtesy name: Yung-p'u (鎔[illegible]). Prefers English rendering: T. K. Tseng. Born 1882; native of Foochow, Fukien. Graduate, Nanyang College, Shanghai. B.A. in economics (Cantab.) 1914-9, consul general to Australia; salt commissioner for Kirin and Heilungkiang. 1920, director, cadastral administration. 1925, vice-minster for foreign affairs; delegate, Customs Conference. 1926, minister to Sweden and Norway. 1927-8, Chinese manager, Pekin Syndicate, Peking. Secretary Ceneral, Commission for the Readjustment of Finance, since January 1929. — p. 984

**Tseng, T. K** TSENG, T. K., see above. — p. 984

**TSENG Yang-feng, (曾仰豐)** TSENG YANG-FENG, (曾仰豐).—Courtesy name: Ching-nan (景南). Born 1886; native of Foochow, Fukien. Graduate, Peiyang University. 1917, M.A. (Illinois). Sometime engineer, Hankow-Szechwan Railway, Yellow River and Grand Canal conservancy boards. 1926-8, director, Huaipei salt revenue administration, Changlu. — p. 984

**TSENG Yang-p'u, (曾養甫)** TSENG YANG-P'U, (曾養甫).—Born 1892; native of P'ingyuen, Kwangtung. Graduate, Peiyang University. Studied in U.S.A. 1926, director of political training, Canton. 1927, director, reconstruction department, Kwangtung Provincial Government. Vice-Chairman, National Reconstruction Commission, and Vice-Minister of Agriculture and Mining, since October 1928. March 1929, elected member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. — p. 984

**TSENG Yu-chun, (曾毓雋)** TSENG YU-CHUN, (曾毓雋). Courtesy name: Yün-p'ei (雲沛). Born 1879; native of Foochow, Fukien. Chüjen of imperial examinations. 1916, managing director, Peking-Hankow Railway. 1918, senator; vice-minister of communications; proscribed and took refuge in Japanese Legation, upon collapse of Anfu Party. Res. Tientsin. — p. 985

**TSOU Ping-wen, (鄒秉文)** TSOU PING-WEN, (鄒秉文).—Born 1892; native of Socchow, Kiangsu. 1915, M.S. (Cornell). 1916-27, dean, school of agriculture, Southeastern University, Nanking. Director, Shanghaai Bureau for the testing and inspection of commercial commodities, Ministry of Industry, Labour and Commerce, since August, 1928. Author of a textbook of higher botany. Past president, Chinese Agricultural Society. — p. 985

**TS'UI Shih-chieh, (崔士傑)** TS'UI SHIH-CHIEH, (崔士傑).—Born 1887; native of Lintzu, Shantung. 1917, graduate, LL.B. (Tokyo Imperial). 1922, adviser, directorate-general for the rehabilitation of Shantung. 1923, adviser, directorate-general of Sino-Russian negotiations. 1927, secretary, headquarters of Nationalist 2nd army. Commissioner for Foreign Affairs, Tsinan, since 1928. — p. 985

**TS'UI T'ing-hsien, (崔廷獻)** TS'UI T'ING-HSIEN, (崔廷獻).—Courtesy name: Wen-chen (文徵). Born 1874 in and native of Shouhsien, Shansi. Graduate, Tokyo Law College. Sometime chairman, Shansi provincial assembly; principal, agricultural and industrial college, Taiyuan; commissioner of finance and salt, same Province. Mayor of Greater Tientsin, since September 1928. — p. 985

**TU Chu-hsuan, (杜竹宣)** TU CHU-HSUAN, (杜竹宣).—Born 1883; native of En-hsien, Shantung. Graduate, Tungchou College. 1905-7, instructor in history and mathematics, same institution. 1908-12, assistant to Mr. Thwing in anti-opium and anti-liquor campaign. 1912, editor, Ta Kung Pao ("L'Impartial"), Tientsin. 1916, founded Yi Shih Pao ("Social Welfare"), Tientsin and Peking, and editor since then. — p. 985

**TU Hsi-kuei, (杜錫珪)** TU HSI-KUEI, (杜錫珪).—Courtesy name: Shen-ch'en (慎丞). Born 1875: native of Foochow, Fukien. Graduate, Nanking naval college. 1912, commander of Hai Tung. 1917, commander, 2nd Squadron. 1921-2, commander-in-chief of the navy. 1926, minister of navy and acting premier. — p. 985

**TU Shu-ts'ai, (杜樹材)** TU SHU-TS'AI, (杜樹材).—Courtesy name: Tu-wei (度偉). Prefers English rendering: Doo Dewey or To Shue Tsoi. Native of Kwangtung. 1923, B.S. (Canton Christian College). Instructor in charge of animal husbandry, same institution, since 1924. 1926, concurrently acting dean. Author of a science reader for higher primary schools, etc. — p. 985

**TU Shun, (杜純)** TU SHUN, (杜純).—Courtesy name: Chieh-yuan (子遠). Born 1886; native of P'anyü, Kwangtung. 1920, superintendent of customs, Hangchow. 1922, salt transportation commissioner in Chekiang. 1925, director of salt office, Yangchow. — p. 985

**TUAN Ch'i-jui, (段祺瑞)** TUAN CH'I-JUI, (段祺瑞).—Courtesy name: Chih-ch'uan (芝泉). Born 1864; native of Hofei, Anhwei. Graduate, Peiyang Military College. 1906, commander, 3rd division. 1909, commander, 6th division. 1912, minister of war. 1914, military governor of Honan and minister of war. 1915, made field marshal. 1916, premier. 1917, suppressed Manchu Restoration; reappointed premier and minister of war; director general, war participation bureau. 1918, reappointed premier. 1919, director general of frontier defence. November 1924--April 1926, provisional chief executive in Peking. Res. Tientsin. — p. 985

**TUAN Mu-chieh, (端木傑)** TUAN MU-CHIEH, (端木傑).—Courtesy name: Wen-hsia (文俠). Sometime chief commissary officer, Kuominchun 3rd division. Departmental chief, Commissariate Administration, Ministry of Military Affairs, since December 1928. — p. 985

**T'UNG Hang-shih, (童杭時)** T'UNG HANG-SHIH, (童杭時).—Courtesy name: Chen-ch'i (枕谿). Prefers to sign himself: Dong Hang-zee. Born 1877 in and native of Ch'enghsien, Chekiang. Graduate, Chekiang Law College. LL.B., Tokyo Law College. 1913-7, senator. 1918, chief of division, Supreme Court; councillor, Generalissimo's headquarters, Canton. 1924, commissioner of industry, Hangchow. 1927-8, senior judge, Supreme Court. Chief of division, Supreme Court, since December 1928. — p. 986

**TUNG Hsien-kuang, (董顯光)** TUNG HSIEN-KUANG, (董顯光).—Prefers to sign himself: Hollington K. Tong. Born 1887; native of Ningpo, Chekiang. 1911, B.A. (Missouri). 1913-7, editor, Peking Daily News; secretary, national oil administration. 1918-27, secretary, Chihli river conservancy commission; councillor, Ministry of Communications. Editor, Yung Pao (Tientsin), since 1926. Contributing editor, China Weekly Review (Shanghai). — p. 986

**TUNG K'ang, (董康)** TUNG K'ANG, (董康).—Courtesy name: Shou-chin (綬金). Born 1869; native of Wuchin, Kiangsu. Chinshih of imperial examinations. 1914-8, also 1920 and 1922, chief justice, Supreme Court. 1918-20, president, law codification commission. 1921, minister of justice. 1922, acting minister of finance. 1923-6, vice-chairman, commission on extraterritoriality. Legal practitioner in Shanghai since 1927. — p. 986

**TUNG Shih-en, (董士恩)** TUNG SHIH-EN, (董士恩).—Courtesy name: Yu-ts'en (右岑). Born 1877; native of T'ungshan, Kiangsu. Graduate, Peiyang University. 1918, commissioner of finance, Kirin. 1919, commiissioner for foreign affairs, Harbin. 1922, senator. 1927, vice-minister of finance in charge of wine and tobacco matters. — p. 986

**Tyau, Edward Sintuk** TYAU, EDWARD SINTUK, see TIAO HSIN-TEH. — p. 986

**Tyau, M. T. Z** TYAU, M. T. Z., see TIAO MIN-CH'IEN. — p. 986

**Tyau, Phillip K. C** TYAU, PHILLIP K. C., see TIAO TSO-CH'IEN. — p. 986

**WANG Chan-yuan, (王占元)** WANG CHAN-YUAN, (王占元).—Courtesy name: Tzu-ch'un (子春). Born 1860; native of Kuanhsien, Shantung. Graduate, Peiyang Military College. 1912-3, commander, 2nd division. 1916-21, military and civil governor of Hupeh; high inspecting commissioner for Hupeh and Hunan. — p. 986

**WANG Chao-ming, (汪兆銘)** WANG CHAO-MING, (汪兆銘)—Courtesy name: Ching-wei (精衞). Sometime known as: Henri Waung. Born 1884 in and native of Panyü, Kwangtung. Graduate, Tokyo Law College. Member of Tung Meng Hui, became one of the most ardent revolutionists. 1909, participated in unsuccessful attempt to assassinate Prince Regent; condemned to imprisonment for life but released after outbreak of 1911 Revolution. Declined offers of premiership in Nanking provisional government and military governorship of Canton. 1919, promoter, "Work and Study Society" for financing Chinese labourers through French schools. 1921, member of Canton Military Government and Dr. Sun's representative to promote a triangular alliance with Chang Tso-lin and Tuan Ch'i-jui. 1924, chief adviser to Generalissimo Sun Yat-sen. 1925-6, forced to leave Canton. 1927, returned to resume chairmanship of Kuomintang Central Executive Committee; then tried to set up a Nationalist Government in Canton independent of Nanking and Hankow Has been residing in Europe since spring of 1928. Member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee, since 1924. Author of numerous books and essays, including "The World and China after the Paris Conference," in Chinese and English. — p. 986

**WANG Ch'eng-pin, (王承斌)** WANG CH'ENG-PIN, (王承斌).—Courtesy name: Hsiao-po (孝伯). Born 1874; native of Hsinch'eng, Fengtien. Graduate, Peiyang Military College. 1920, commander, 23rd division. 1922, civil governor of Chihli. 1923-4, military governor of Chihli and deputy high inspecting commissioner for Chihli, Honan and Shantung. — p. 986

**Wang, C. T** WANG, C. T., see below. — p. 986

**WANG Cheng-t'ing, (王正廷)** WANG CHENG-T'ING, (王正廷).—Courtesy name: Ju-t'ang (儒堂). Prefers English rendering: C. T. Wang or Chengting T. Wang. Born 1882; native of Ningpo, Chekiang. Studied in Peiyang University and Japan. 1911-2, B.A. (Yale); secretary, Chinese Y.M.C.A., Shanghai; actively identified with the Revolution and Nanking provisional government; acting minister of agriculture, industry and commerce; vice-speaker of Senate. 1914-6, general secretary, National Committee of Y.M.C.A. 1916-7, resumed seat as vice-speaker of Senate. 1917-8, joined Canton Military Government. 1918-9, envoy to U.S.A. to secure recognition of Canton Government; delegate to Paris Conference; advised against signing of Versailles Treaty because of Shantung clauses. 1920-22, interested in business in Shanghai. 1922, director general of Shantung rehabilitation commission and chief delegate to Sino-Japanese commission on Shantung matters. December 1922—January 1923, minister for foreign affairs and acting premier. March 1923—May 1924, director general of Sino-Russian negotiations. 1925-6, minister for foreign affairs; concurrently premier and acting minister of finance; director general of Sino-Russian Conference; chairman, Customs Conference. 1927, chairman, Far Eastern Olympics Meet and Chinese Ratepayers Association, Shanghai; managing director, Lung-Hai Railway. Minister for Foreign Affairs and member, Kuomintang Central Political Council, as well as other commissions, since June 1928. Known abroad as "China's fighting diplomat," has achieved unique diplomatic triumph by signing new tariff autonomy treaties with all Powers possessing treaty relations and prevailing upon treaty-expired Powers to renounce extraterritoriality* by 1930. March 1929, elected reserve member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. A forceful speaker, is in great demand at all public gatherings. A keen sportsman. President, National Good Roads Association of China; charter honorary member, Peiping Rotary Club. Managing director, Liuhokou Coal Mining Company. [Footnote: * Conditionally. Ed.] — p. 987

**WANG Chi-tseng, (王繼曾)** WANG CHI-TSENG, (王繼曾).—Courtesy name: Shu-ch'in (述勤). Prefers French rendering: K. T. Ouang (Ouang Ki-tseng). Born 1880; native of Foochow, Fukien. Graduate, Nanyang College, Paris Ecole des Hautes etudes commerciales and Ecole Libre des sciences politiques. 1909-11, secretary to viceroy Chang Chih-tung, Wuchang; superintendent of students in France. 1917, director, political affairs department, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. 1922-3, minister to Mexico. 1924, chief secretary to the Cabinet. — p. 987

**WANG Chia-jui, (王家瑞)** WANG CHIA-JUI, (王家瑞).—Courtesy name: Shao-p'u (少溥). Born 1895; native of Hsinmin, Fengtien. Graduate, Mukden Law College. Sometime councillor, Bureau of Printing; adviser, delegation to Washington Conference. — p. 987

**WANG Chien, (王鍵)** WANG CHIEN, (王鍵).—Courtesy name: Chin-sheng (晋生). Prefers English rendering: John Wong. Born 1885; native of Taiku, Shansi. Graduate, St. John's University. 1914, B.A. (Wisconsin). 1916, M.A. (Columbia) Specialized in leather chemistry. Promoter and manager of first leather tannery in Tientsin, since 1917. — p. 987

**WANG Chih-ch'ang, (王治昌)** WANG CHIH-CH'ANG, (王治昌).—Courtesy name: Huai-ch'ing (槐青). Born 1877; native of Tientsin. 1909, bachelor of commerce (Waseda). Chüjen of imperial examinations. 1912-27, councillor, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce; technical expert, delegation to Washington Conference. — p. 987

**WANG Chih-p'ing, (王治平)** WANG CHIH-P'ING, (王治平).—Courtesy name: Hua-ch'ing (化青). Native of Peking. Graduate, Peking Methodist University. 1915-7, M.A., Ph.D.) (Columbia). 1917-9, dean, Chinese department and professor of history, Peking Methodist University. 1924-8, general secretary, Chinese Y.M.C.A., Tientsin. — p. 987

**WANG Chih-t'ing, (王芝庭)** WANG CHIH-T'ING (王芝庭).—Born 1885 in and native of Yünhsien, Shensi. LL.B. (Meiji, Japan). 1914-28, rose to president, Kansu High Court. President, Ninghsia High Court, since January 1929. — p. 988

**WANG Chih-yu, (王之佑)** WANG CHIH-YU, (王之佑).—Courtesy name: Li-san (立三). Born 1893 in and native of Hsingch'eng, Liaoning (Fengtien). Graduate, Mukden military college. Sometime chief of staff, Ankuochun 5th, 6th and 7th army corps. 1928, chief of police, Kirin Province. Concurrently member, Kirin Provincial Government, since December 1928. — p. 988

**WANG, C. C** WANG, C. C., see WANG CHING-CH'UN. — p. 988

**WANG Ching-ch'i, (王景岐)** WANG CHING-CH'I, (王景岐).—Courtesy name: Shih-sun (石蓀). Prefers to sign himself: Wang King-ky. Born 1882 in and native of Foochow, Fukien. Graduate, Ecole Libre des sciences politiques, Paris. 1912-20, councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs; secretary, Russo-Chinese-Mongolian tripartite conference, Kiachta; technical expert, delegation to Paris Conference. 1915-21 Professor of History of the Treaties concluded between China and Foreign States, Peking University. 1920-21 Negotiator of Sino-German Agreement, Peking Minister in Belgium since 1921. Chinese Delegate to the Assembly of the League of Nations, 1926-1928. Representative to the Council of the League, 1928. Delegate to the Advisory Committee of Traffic in Opium. Author of "La Voix de la Chine", 1st. Edition 1927, 2nd. Edition 1929, published in Brussels. — p. 988

**WANG Ching-ch'un, (王景春)** WANG CHING-CH'UN, (王景春).—Courtesy name: Chao-hsi (兆熙). Born 188[illegible] in and native of Lanchow, Chihli. Prefers English rending: C. C. Wang. 1908, Ph.B. (Yale). 1909, M.A.; 1911, Ph.D. (Illinois). 1913-9, managing director, Peking-Mukden and Peking-Hankow Railways; vice-chairman, commission on unification of railway accounts and statistics; technical expert, delegation to Paris Conference. 1920-1, councillor, Ministry of Communications, and representative on Inter-Allied Technical Board; director general, Chinese Eastern Railway. 1925, delegate, International Electrical Communications and Postal conferences. 1926, one of three Chinese members on British Boxer Indemnity Advisory Committee. April-June 1927, director general of posts. Author of "Legislative regulation of railway finance in England" (doctoral dissertation). — p. 988

**WANG Ching-wei** WANG CHING-WEI, see WANG CHAO-MING. — p. 988

**WANG Ch'ung-hui, (王寵惠)** WANG CH'UNG-HUI, (王寵惠).—Courtesy name: Liang-ch'ou (亮疇). Born 1882 in and native of Tungkuan, Kwangtung. Studied in Peiyang University, also France and Germany. 1904, D. C. L. (Yale). 1907, barrister-at-law (Middle Temple, London); assistant delegate, 2nd Hague Peace Conference. 1912, minister for foreign affairs, Nanking; minister of justice, Peking. His views on constitutional law largely influenced drafting of Nanking Provisional Constitution. 1913-6, editor, Chung Hua Book Company; vice-president, Fuhtan University, Shanghai. 1917-27, chairman, law codification commission. 1920, chief justice, Supreme Court. 1921, delegate, League of Nations Assembly and Washington Conference; elected deputy judge, Permanent Court of International Justice, The Hague. July-December 1922, and January 1924, premier. 1925, nominated minister to England, but did not proceed; delegate, Customs Conference and International Commission on Extraterritorial Jurisdiction; chairman, commission for investigation of extraterritoriality. 1926, minister of education; director-general, general office for liquidation of branches in China of Russo-Asiatic Bank. 1927-8, minister of justice, Nanking; member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee and Central Political Council. State Councillor and President, Judicial Yuan, since October 1928. March 1929, elected member, Kuomintang Central Supervisory Committee. April 1929, re-elected member, Kuomintang Central Political Council. Author of English translation of German Civil Code (doctoral dissertation). — p. 988

**WANG Hsiao-ying, Miss, (王孝英女士)** WANG HSIAO-YING, MISS, (王孝英女士).—Graduate, Peking Girls' Normal College. Sometime principal, Foochow Girls' Normal College. Magistrate of P'ingnan district, Fukien, since 1927. First of her sex in China to be made a civil official. — p. 989

**WANG Hu, (王瑚)** WANG HU, (王瑚).—Native of Chihli. 1914, censor of the censorate. 1916, governor of Peking. 1920-3, civil governor of Kiangsu and Shantung. Member, National Famine Relief Commission, since January 1929. — p. 989

**WANG Huai-ch'ing, (王懷慶)** WANG HUAI-CH'ING, (王懷慶).—Courtesy name: Mou-hsuan (懋宣). Born 1866; native of Ningchin, Chihli. Graduate, Peiyang Military College. Displayed unusual bravery during Sino-Japanese war. 1912, garrison commissioner, Do'onor. 1914, garrison commander, Tsinan. 1919-22 and 1926, commander of Peking garrison. 1922, inspector general of Charhar, Jehol, and Suiyuan. — p. 989

**WANG I-chih, (王一之)** WANG I-CHIH, (王一之).—Prefers English name: Yates Wang. Born 1887; native of Hangchow, Chekiang. 1913, B.A. (George Washington); editor, Shun Pao (Shanghai). 1920-8, 2nd secretary, Legation in Vienna and permanent delegation to League of Nations. — p. 989

**WANG I-t'ang, (王揖堂)** WANG I-T'ANG, (王揖堂).—Courtesy name: I-t'ang (一堂). Born 1877 in and native of Hofei, Anhwei. Chinshih of imperial examinations. 1912-3, organized Kunghotang which amalgamated with Chinputang or Progressive Party; senator. 1915-6, civil governor of Kirin; minister of interior. 1918-23, speaker, House of Representatives; chief northern delegate, Shanghai peace conference; proscribed as leader of Anfu Party until pardoned (1923). — p. 989

**WANG Ju-ch'in, (王汝勤)** WANG JU-CH'IN, (王汝勤).—Courtesy name: Yu-fu (幼甫). Born 1877 in and native of Miyun, Chihli. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. 1919-25, commander, 8th division; assistant military governor of Hupeh. — p. 989

**WANG K'e-min, (王克敏)** WANG K'E-MIN, (王克敏).—Courtesy name: Shu-lu (叔魯). Born 1875 in and native of Hangchow, Chekiang. Chüjen of imperial examinations. 1902-11, councillor, Legation in Tokyo; commissioner for foreign affairs in Chihli. 1917 and 1923-4, minister of finance. 1922-3, re-elected president, Bank of China. — p. 989

**WANG King-ky** WANG KING-KY, see WANG CHING-CH'I. — p. 989

**WANG Kuang-ch'i, (王廣圻)** WANG KUANG-CH'I, (王廣圻).—Courtesy name: Chi-fu (劼甫). Born 1878; native of Wusih, Kiangsu. Graduate, Peking Language School. Sometime chief secretary to the Cabinet. 1912-4, minister to Belgium. 1915-20, minister to Italy. 1921-7, minister to Holland; delegate, Opium Conference, Geneva. Adviser, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, since January 1929. — p. 989

**WANG Lai-ch'i, (王賚祺)** WANG LAI-CH'I, (王賚祺).—Courtesy name: Yü-chou (予周). Born 1885; native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. Docteur en droit, University of Brussels. 1922-4, 2nd secretary, in charge of Legation in Sweden, Norway and Finland. 1925-7, secretary and chief of section, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. — p. 989

**WANG Lei, (王耒)** WANG LEI, (王耒). Born 1880; native of Chekiang. Educated in Japan. 1920-5, chief secretary to the Cabinet; vice-minister of interior. — p. 989

**WANG, Lingoh** WANG, LINGOH, see below. — p. 989

**WANG Lin-ke, (王麟閣)** WANG LIN-KE, (王麟閣).—Prefers English rendering: Lingoh Wang. Born 1880; native of Tientsin. B.A. (North Carolina). 1913-6, commissioner for foreign affairs, Chefoo and Tientsin. 1917-28, consul to Vancouver; second secretary, Legation in Washington; Peking manager, Commercial Guarantee Bank; consul general to Manila, P.I. Chargé d'Affaires at Madrid, since November, 1928. — p. 989

**WANG Lun, (王綸)** WANG LUN, (王綸).—Courtesy name: Chien-wai (劍外). Born 1892; native of Yenhai, Chekiang. Graduate, Peking Military College. Sometime chief of staff, 1st army corps, Nationalist 1st Group Army. Senior councillor, Board of General Staff, since November 1928. — p. 990

**WANG Nai-pin, (王迺斌)** WANG NAI-PIN, (王迺斌).—Courtesy name: En-fu (恩甫). Born 1870 in and native of Hsinmin, Fengtien. 1911, taotai of Jehol. 1918-22, adviser to President Hsu Shih-ch'ang. 1920, minister of agriculture and commerce. — p. 990

**WANG Po-ch'un, (王伯群)** WANG PO-CH'UN, (王伯群).—Born 1885; native of Hsingi, Kweichow. Studied in Japan. 1920, minister of communications, Canton Government. 1923, civil governor of Kweichow. Minister of Communications, since 1928; concurrently president, Communications University, Shanghai. 1928, member, Kuomintang Central Political Council. May 1929, elected reserve member, Kuomintang Central Political Council. June 1929, elected member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. — p. 990

**WANG Po-ling, (王伯齡)** WANG PO-LING, (王伯齡).—Courtesy name: Mou-ju (茂如) Native of Yangchow, Kiangsu. Studied in Peiyang military college and Japan. Participated in 1911 Revolution. 1923, high adviser, Generalissimo's headquarters, Canton. 1924, assisted General Chiang Kai-shek in establishing Whampoa Military Academy. 1926, commander, 1st division, Nationalist forces attacking Hunan and Kiangsi. 1927, commander, Yangtze defences; senior instructor, Central Military College, Nanking. Member, Kiangsu Provincial Government, and concurrently director, Reconstruction Department, since 1928. March 1929, elected member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. — p. 990

**WANG P'u, (王璞)** WANG P'U, (王璞).—Born 1875; native of Peking. Inventor and promoter, phonetic alphabet. Sometime professor, National Teachers' University; president, Peking Phonetic Alphabet Training School. Author of books on national pronunciation, spoken language, etc. — p. 990

**WANG Shih, (王時)** WANG SHIH, (王時).—Courtesy name: Yü-min (雨民). Prefers English rendering: S. Wang. Born 1889; native of Yungshun, Hunan. Graduate, Paoting military academy. Participated in 153 engagements (47 being major battles) for the revolutionary cause. Councillor, Ministry of Military Affairs, since November 1928. Author of a few military manuals. — p. 990

**WANG Shih-chen, (王士珍)** WANG SHIH-CHEN, (王士珍).—Courtesy name: P'ingch'ing (聘卿). Born 1864 in and native of Chengting, Chihli. Graduate, Peiyang Military College, in same class with Marshal Tuan Ch'i-jui. 1908-10, commander, Kiangpei garrison. minister of war and viceroy of Hunan-Hupeh and Kwangtung-Kwangsi. 1915 and 1917, minister of war. 1916, chief of general staff. 1917-8, acting premier and minister of war; promoted field marshal. 1925-6, chairman, military rehabilitation conference; chairman, committee of safety, during period between Kuominchun evacuation of and entry of Mukden troops into Peking. — p. 990

**WANG Shih-chen, (王世徵)** WANG SHIH-CHEN, (王世徵).—Courtesy name: O-sun (莪蓀). Prefers English rendering: S. C. Wang. Born 1873 in and native of Foochow, Fukien. Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. 1907, barrister-at-law, Lincoln's Inn, London. 1917, editor, Kung Yen Pao, and managing editor, Peking Daily News. 1918-20, senator; founder and editor, Hsin Pao, Peking. — p. 990

**WANG Shih-chieh, (王世杰)** WANG SHIH-CHIEH, (王世杰).—Courtesy name: Hsueh-t'ing (雪艇). Born 1891 in and native of Hupeh. Studied in Peiyang University. 1917, B.Sc. (London). 1920, docteur en droit (Paris). 1921-6, professor of comparative constitutional law, Peking National University. 1923-6, dean, faculty of law, same university. 1927-8, member, treaty committee, Hankow Nationalist Government; director, law codification bureau, Nanking. October 1928, appointed member, Permanent Court of Arbitration at the Hague. Member, Legislative Yuan, since December 1928. Author of "A Treatise on Comparative Constitutional Law," "The Problem of the International Settlement of Shanghai," "The Legal Aspects of Slavery in the History of China." — p. 990-991

**WANG Shih-t'ung, (王式通)** WANG SHIH-T'UNG, (王式通).—Courtesy name: Shu-heng (書衡). Born 1866; native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. Chinshih of imperial examinations. Sometime minister of justice. 1927, chief compiler, bureau of ceremonies, Peiping. — p. 991

**WANG Shih-yuan, (汪士元)** WANG SHIH-YUAN, (汪士元).—Native of Anhwei. Chinshih of imperial examinations. Sometime salt commissioner for Chihli and vice-minister of finance. 1921-2, director general of wine and tobacco administration. Res. Tientsin. — p. 991

**WANG Shu-fang, (王潄芳)** WANG SHU-FANG, (王潄芳).—Courtesy name: I-p'u (藝圃). Born 1901 in in and native of P'an-hsien, Kweichow. 1925, B.A. (Southeastern University). Sometime chief secretary, commander-in-chief, Northern Expedition First Route Army. Senior secretary, Ministry of Communications, since December 1928. — p. 991

**WANG T'an, (王坦)** WANG T'AN, (王坦).—Courtesy name: Yang-chih (養治). Born 1886; native of Mukden. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. 1913, chief staff officer, Charhar garrison. 1923-4, vice-minister of war. — p. 991

**WANG T'ang, (王棠)** WANG T'ANG, (王棠).—Courtesy name: Chao-nan (召南). Prefers to sign himself: Wong Tong. Native of Hsiangshan, Kwangtung. Sometime manager, Leemin Shinkuo Weaving Company; managing director, Canton-Hankow Railway. 1926, commissioner of finance, Canton Provincial Government. — p. 991

**WANG T'ing-chang, (王廷璋)** WANG T'ING-CHANG, (王廷璋).—Courtesy name: Tzu-ch'i (子琦).—Prefers French rendering: Ouang Ting-tchang. Born 1884; native of Chekiang. Studied in Peking, Paris and Liege. 1910, Chinshih of imperial examinations. 1917, chargé d'affaires in Mexico. 1922-5, councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs; director, protocol department, same Ministry and Customs Conference. Minister to Portugal since 1926. 1927, representative on League of Nations Council. — p. 991

**WANG T'ing-cheng, (王廷楨)** WANG T'ING-CHENG, (王廷楨).—Courtesy name: Tzu-ming (子眀). Born 1876 in and native of Tientsin. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. 1913, defence commissioner of Nanking. 1916, commander, 16th division. 1919-20, civil and military governor of Charhar. — p. 991

**WANG Tu, (王杜)** WANG TU, (王杜).—Courtesy name: Hui-ju (晦如). Born 1879; native of Hangchow, Chekiang. Sometime secretary, President's Office; councillor, Cabinet Office; judge, Administrative Court. 1927, councillor, Ministry of Communications. — p. 991

**WANG Wei-fan, (王維藩)** WANG WEI-FAN, (王維藩).—Born 1885; native of Hopei (Chihli). Graduate, Tokyo Law and Normal Colleges. Sometime commissioner of education, Charhar; principal, Peking Normal College. Chief secretary, National Opium Suppression Commission, since 1928. — p. 991

**WANG Wen-hsien, (王文顯)** WANG WEN-HSIEN, (王文顯).—Courtesy name: Li-shan (力山). Prefers English rendering: J. Wong-Quincey. Born 1887; native of Quinsan, Kiangsu. 1913, B.A. (London). 1914, secretary, financial mission to Europe. Professor of English literature, Tsing Hua College, since 1915; sometime dean and acting president. Author of "The World War from the Chinese Standpoint," "Educational Guide to the United States," "She Stoops to Compromise." — p. 991

**WANG Wen-pao, (王文豹)** WANG WEN-PAO, (王文豹).—Courtesy name: Shao-ch'uan (紹荃). Born 1879 in and native of Changsha, Hunan. 1914-27, director, department of prisons, Ministry of Justice. 1920, member, commission for reorganization of Russian courts along Chinese Eastern Railway. 1925-6, acting vice-minister of justice. — p. 991

**WANG Wen-tien, (王文典)** WANG WEN-TIEN, (王文典).—Native of Suian, Chekiang. Sometime chief technical expert, Sino-Russian Conference; chairman, Peking general chamber of commerce; vice-chairman, All China Union of Chambers of Commerce; North China general manager, Nanyang Brothers Tobacco Company. Manager, Tientsin branch, Chinese-American Bank of Commerce, since March 1929. — p. 992

**WANG Wen-wei, (王文[illegible])** WANG WEN-WEI, (王文[illegible]).—Courtesy name: Hsiang-hou (薌侯).—Prefers English rendering: Homer W. W. Wong. Born 1880 in and native of Chusan, Chekiang. Graduate, Shanghai language school. 1917-25, director general of posts; councillor, Ministry of Finance. — p. 992

**WANG, Yates** WANG, YATES see WANG I-CHIH. — p. 992

**WANG Yin-t'ai, (王蔭泰)** WANG YIN-T'AI, (王蔭泰).—Courtesy name: Meng-ch'ün (孟羣). Born 1886; native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. 1912, LL.B. (Berlin). 1914-20, councillor, law compilation bureau; director, general affairs department, office of high commissioner to Urga. 1922-5, councillor, civil service bureau. 1926-8, vice-minister and minister for foreign affairs; delegate, Customs Conference. Managing director, Exchange Bank of China, Peiping, since June 1928. — p. 992

**WANG Yu-chih, (王毓芝)** WANG YU-CHIH, (王毓芝).—Courtesy name: Lan-t'ing (蘭亭). Born 1878 in and native of Tsining, Shantung. 1922, director general, wine and tobacco administration. 1923-4, chief secretary to President Ts'ao K'un. — p. 992

**WANG Yu-kuei, (王毓桂)** WANG YU-KUEI, (王毓桂)—Courtesy name: T'ieh-san (鐵珊). Born 1891 in and native of Liaoyang, Liaoning (Fengtien). Graduate, Peking Government University. B. Com. (Manchester). M.S. (Columbia). 1927, secretary to Ankuochun 3rd and 4th army corps. 1928, dean, Min Kuo University (Peking); acting commissioner of education, Mukden. Commissioner of education, Liaoning (Fengtien) Provincial Government, since December 1928. — p. 992

**WANG Yu-ling, (汪有齡)** WANG YU-LING (汪有齡).—Courtesy name: Tzu-chien (子健). Born 1879 in and native of Hangchow, Chekiang. 1912, vice-minister of justice. 1914-20, senator; editor-in-chief, Kung Yen Pao. Legal practitioner in Peiping and Shanghai, since 1921. Founder and president, Ch'ao Yang University, Peiping. — p. 992

**WANG Yun-wu, (王雲五)** WANG YUN-WU, (王雲五). Courtesy name: Chih-jui or Yu-lu (之瑞 岫廬) Prefers English rendering: Y. W. Wang. Born 1887; native of Hsiangshan, Kwangtung. Sometime acting vice-minister of education; editor, Republican Daily, Peking. Editor-in-chief, Commercial Press, Shanghai, since 1920. Well-known author. Director, Oriental Library, and chairman, Library Association, Shanghai. — p. 992

**WAUNG, Henri** WAUNG, HENRI, see WANG CHAO-MING. — p. 992

**WEI Ch'en-tzu, (魏宸組)** WEI CH'EN-TZU (魏宸組).—Courtesy name: Chu-tung (注東). Native of Hupeh. 1912-5, chief secretary to the Cabinet; minister to Holland. 1919-21, minister to Belgium; delegate, Paris Peace Conference. 1922-8, minister to Germany. — p. 992

**WEI Ch'ueh, (韋愨)** WEI CH'UEH, (韋愨).—Courtesy name: P'eng-tan (捧丹). Prefers English rendering: Sidney K. Wei. Born 1896, in and native of Chungshan, Kwangtung. 1918, A.B. (Oberlin). 1920, Ph.D. (Chicago). 1921-5, assistant professor, Canton Christian College; commissioner of education, Canton. 1925-7. secretary, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Nationalist Government. 1928, commissioner of education for Greater Shanghai. Director of Secondary Education, Kiangsu University District, Nanking, since May 1929. Author of "International Relationship in China and its Ethical, Social, and Political Interpretations" (doctoral dissertation). — p. 992

**WEI Ch'ung-yuan, (魏崇元)** WEI CH'UNG-YUAN, (魏崇元).—Courtesy name: Ch'ien-ch'u (乾初). Native of Omei, Szechwan. Sometime principal, Chengtu Normal College. Investigation commissioner for organization of Hsikang Province, since October 1928. — p. 993

**WEI, E. F** WEI, E. F., see WEI I-FU. — p. 993

**WEI I, (魏易)** WEI I, (魏易).—Courtesy name: Ch'ung-shu (冲叔). Born 1880 in and native of Hangchow, Chekiang. 1914-7, secretary, national oil administration, and central salt administration. 1918-28, chief secretary, Chihli river improvement commission. Co-translator (with Lin Ch'in-nan) of 50 English classic novels. — p. 993

**WEI I-fu, (衞以黻)** WEI I-FU, (衞以黻).—Prefers English rendering: E. F. Wei. Born 1885; native of Wushing, Chekiang. Graduate, Nanyang College. 1912, M. E. (Cornell). Sometime locomotive superintendent, and acting managing director, Peking-Suiyuan Railway; chairman, technical committee, Ministry of Communications. Chief Technical Expert, and Permanent Vice-Minister of Communications, since October 1928. — p. 993

**WEI Po, (衞渤)** WEI PO, (衞渤).—Courtesy name: T'ing-t'ao (聽濤). Prefers English rendering: Tyndal Wei. Born 1885 in and native of Soochow, Kiangsu. 1914-28, director, department of loans, Ministry of Finance; technical expert, commission for readjustment of finance and Customs Conference; councillor, wine and tobacco administration. Assistant manager, Bank of Communications, Shanghai, since November 1928. — p. 993

**WEI, Sidney, Kok** WEI, SIDNEY, KOK, see WEI CH'UEH. — p. 993

**WEI, Tyndal** WEI, TYNDAL, see WEI P'O. — p. 993

**WEI Wen-pin, (魏文彬)** WEI WEN-PIN, (魏文彬).—Courtesy name: Ya-t'ing (雅庭). Born 1887 in and native of Miyun, Chihli. 1910, B.A.; 1911, M.A. (Amherst). 1914, Ph.D. (Columbia). 1915-21, secretary, Legation in Washington and London, also delegation to Paris and Washington Conferences. 1922-8, secretary and acting councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs; assistant director, conference department, Customs Conference. Councillor, Peiping and Tientsin garrison commander-in-chief's headquarters, since July 1928. Author of "The Currency Problem in China" (doctoral dissertation). — p. 993

**WEN Shih-chen, (溫世珍)** WEN SHIH-CHEN, (溫世珍). Courtesy name: P'ei-shan (佩珊). Prefers English rendering: S. T. Wen. Born 1878 in and native of Tientsin. Graduate, Peiyang naval college. 1906-10, secretary to viceroys of Nanking and Canton. 1913-24, commissioner for foreign affairs at Hangchow, Nanking and Shanghai; adviser, delegation to Washington Conference. — p. 993

**WEN Shih-lin, (溫世霖)** WEN SHIH-LIN, (溫世霖).—Courtesy name: Chih-ying (支英). Born 1871 in and native of Tientsin. 1911, banished to Chinese Turkestan for instigating a general strike against the government's refusal to convene a parliament immediately. 1913, 1916 and 1922, member of parliament. 1917, councillor, Canton Military Government. — p. 993

**WEN, S. T** WEN, S. T., see WEN SHIH-CHEN. — p. 993

**WEN Tsung-yao, (溫宗堯)** WEN TSUNG-YAO, (溫宗堯).—Courtesy name: Ch'ing-fu (欽甫). Born 1876; native of Kwangtung. 1908, assistant resident in Tibet. 1910, councillor, ministry of foreign affairs. 1912, delegate to Shanghai peace conference. 1917, associate director general of Pukow Port development. 1920, administrative director and minister for foreign affairs, Canton Government; chief delegate to Shanghai peace conference. Res. Shanghai. — p. 993

**WENG Wen-hao, (翁文灝)** WENG WEN-HAO, (翁文灝).—Courtesy name: Yung-i (詠霓). Born 1889; native of Chekiang. 1912, D.Sc.
