(Louvain). 1917, acting director, department of mines, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce. 1923-7, chief technical expert same ministry. Director of Geological Survey, Peking, since 1922. Trustee, China Foundation for Promotion of Education and Culture, since August 1928. Author of "Mineral Resources of China", "Earthquake Regions in China," etc. — p. 993

**WU, C. C** WU, C. C., see below. — p. 993

**WU Ch'ao-chu, (伍朝樞)** WU CH'AO-CHU, (伍朝樞).—Courtesy name: T'i-yün (梯雲). Better known as C. C. Wu. Son of late Wu T'ing-fang. Born 1886; native of Hsinhui, Kwangtung. 1911, LL.B. (London); barrister-at-law (Lincoln's Inn). 1912, commissioner of foreign affairs for Hupeh. 1913, member of parliament. 1915, councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs and the Cabinet. 1919, delegate for Canton Government, Paris Conference. 1920-4, vice and then minister for foreign affairs, Canton Government. 1925, mayor of Canton. 1927, minister for foreign affairs, Nanking Nationalist Government. Minister to United States since February 1929. Member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee, since 1926. Author of pamphlets entitled "Appeal for Recognition" and "China's position in Tibet," etc. — p. 994

**WU Ch'i-sun, (吳笈孫)** WU CH'I-SUN, (吳笈孫).—Courtesy name: Shih-hsiang (世緗). Born 1876; native of Kushih, Honan. 1916, director, bureau of printing and engraving. 1918-22, chief secretary to President Hsu Shih-ch'ang. — p. 994

**WU Chien-pang, (吳建邦)** WU CHIEN-PANG, (吳建邦).—Born 1897; native of T'ai-hu, Anhwei. 1926, docteur en droit, Ghent University; doctor in political and diplmatic sciences, Louvain University. Senior compiler, Legislative Yuan, since December 1928. Professor of political science, National Central University, since October 1927. Author of "La Chine et les Puissances" (doctoral dissertation). — p. 994

**WONG, Homer W** WONG, HOMER W., see WANG WEN-WEI — p. 994

**WONG, John** WONG, JOHN, see WANG CHIEN. — p. 994

**WONG, Quincey J** WONG, QUINCEY J., see WANG WEN-HSIEN. — p. 994

**WONG Tong** WONG TONG, see WANG T'ANG. — p. 994

**WOO, Y. L** WOO, Y. L., see WU JEN-LI. — p. 994

**WU Chih-hui** WU CHIH-HUI, see WU CHING-HENG. — p. 994

**WU Chin, (吳晋)** WU CHIN, (吳晋).—Courtesy name: Shao-yu (少佑). Prefers French rendering. Ou Tsing. Born 1889 in and native of Wuchin, Kiangsu. Graduate, Ecole de l'Artillerie, Versailles. 1917-21, military expert, delegation to Paris Conference and League of Nations Assembly. 1926-8, director, diplomatic bureau of Ankuochun; vice-minister for foreign affairs. — p. 994

**WU Ching-heng, (吳敬恒)** WU CHING-HENG, (吳敬恒).—Courtesy name: Chih-hui (稚輝). Born 1864; native of Wusih, Kiangsu. Member, Tung Meng Hui. Resided in England; France, Geermany and Japan. One of most influential Kuomintang "Elder Statesmen." Well known scholar: "style characterized by use of common language, slang, vulgar comparisons, keen humour, bold satire and audacity." Outspoken opponent of Tagore. Promoter, student-labourer movement to France, and Franco-Chinese University at Lyons. Member, Kuomintang Central Supervisory Committee, since 1924. Member, Kuomintang Central Political Council, since 1925. — p. 994

**WU Ching-hsiung, (吳經熊)** WU CHING-HSIUNG, (吳經熊).—Prefers English rendering: John C. H. Wu. Born 1899; native of Ningpo, Chekiang. 1920, LL.B. (Soochow). 1921, J.D. (Michigan). Postgraduate work in Berlin, Paris aand Harvard universities. 1924-7, professor, Comparative Law School of China; judge, Shanghai Provisional Court. Dean, Comparative Law School, and Chief Judge, Shanghai Provisional Court of Appeals (Criminal Division), since August 1928. Author of "Human Nature of Law" and "Juridical Essays." — p. 994

**WU Ching-lien, (吳景濂)** WU CHING-LIEN, (吳景濂).—Courtesy name: Lien-po (蓮伯). Born 1875 in and native of Hsingch'eng, Fengtien. Graduate, Peking National University. 1909, chairman, Fengtien provincial council. 1912, member, Provisional Council, Nanking. 1913, member of parliament. 1917 and 1922-3, speaker, House of Representatives. 1918, speaker, Extraordinary Parliament, Canton. — p. 994

**WU Hsien, (吳憲)** WU HSIEN, (吳憲).—Courtesy name: T'ao-ming (陶民). Born 1893 in and native of Foochow, Fukien. Studied in Fukien Provincial College and Tsing Hua College. 1916, S.B. (Mass. Inst. Tech.). 1916-7, assistant in organic chemistry, Mass. Inst. Tech. 1919, Ph.D. (Harvard). 1919-20, research fellow in biochemistry, Harvard Medical School, Boston. 1920-1, assistant in medicine; 1921-4, associate in biochemistry; since 1928, professor of biochemistry, Peking Union Medical College. Co-operating editor, Journal of Biochemistry (Japan). Editor, Chinese Journal of Physiology since 1926. — p. 995

**WU Hsing-han, (吳醒漢)** WU HSING-HAN, (吳醒漢). Courtesy name: Hou-ts'ai (厚栽). Born 1882; native of Huangp'i, Hupeh. 1917, councillor, Generalissimo's headquarters, Canton. 1923, pacification commissioner for Hupeh; commander, Chienkuochun 9th army. 1926, chief councillor, Nationalist reorganized 9th and 10th armies. 1927, councillor, Nationalist Government. Investigation commissioner for organization of Hsikang Province, since December 1928. — p. 995

**WU Jen-li, (吳紉禮)** WU JEN-LI, (吳紉禮).—Courtesy name: P'ei-chih (佩之). Prefers English rendering: Y. L. Woo. Born 1875; native of Hofei, Anhwei. Graduate, Weihaiwei naval school. 1895, captain of Fuchi and Haichi. 1902, director, Peiyang army schools. 1911, chief of ordnance department. 1925, officiating minister of navy. — p. 995

**WU, John C. H** WU, JOHN C. H., see WU CHING-HSIUNG. — p. 995

**WU Kuang-hsin, (吳光新)** WU KUANG-HSIN, (吳光新).—Native of Anhwei. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. Sometime commander-in-chief, Upper Yangtze Valley. 1925, minister of war. Related by marriage to Tuan Ch'i-jui. — p. 995

**WU K'un-wu, (吳昆吾)** WU K'UN-WU, (吳昆吾).—Prefers French rendering: Houx Koung-ou. Born 1886 in and native of Tungliang, Szechwan. Docteur en droit (Geneva). 1921, technical expert, delegation to Washington Conference. 1924, councillor, law compilation bureau. 1927, vice-chairman, commission on extraterritoriality. Councillor, Judicial Yuan, since March, 1928. — p. 995

**WU Lei-ch'uan, (吳雷川)** WU LEI-CH'UAN, (吳雷川).—Born 1870; native of Hangchow, Chekiang. Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. 1925, promoted councillor, Ministry of Education. Vice-president, Yenching University, Peking, since April 1926. Permanent Vice-Minister of Education, since October, 1928. — p. 995

**WU Lien-teh, (伍連德)** WU LIEN-TEH, (伍連德).—Courtesy name: Hsin-lien (星聯). Born 1880 in Singapore; native of Taishan, Kwangtung. B.A., M.B., Ch.B., M.D. (Cantab.) Honorary LL.D. (Hongkong), and D.Sc. (St. John's). 1911, distinguished himself during outbreak of pneumonic plague in North Manchuria. Director and chief medical officer, North Manchurian Plague Prevention Service at Harbin, since 1912. Delegate to International Opium Conferences (1912 and 1918). Past president, National Medical Association. Author of books on plague and other medical subjects. — p. 995

**WU Nai-sh'en, (吳乃琛)** WU NAI-SH'EN, (吳乃琛).—Born 1882; native of Chekiang. Graduate, California and Wisconsin universities. 1913, vice-governor, Bank of China. 1914-26, director, department of currency and banking, Ministry of Finance. — p. 995

**WU P'ei-fu, (吳佩孚)** WU P'EI-FU, (吳佩孚).—Courtesy name: Tzu-yü (子玉). Born 1873 in and native of P'englai, Shantung. Hsiuts'ai of imperial examinations. 1918, promoted commander, 3rd division. 1921, high inspecting commissioner for Hupeh and Hunan. June 1922, appointed minister of war, but did not assume office. 1923, made field marshal; high inspecting commissioner for Chihli, Shantung and Honan. 1924, commander-in-chief of Chihli force in Chihli-Fengtien war. 1926, assisted Mukden Party in defeating the Kuominchun armies. Residing in Szechwan since 1927. — p. 995

**WU Ping-hsiang, (吳炳湘)** WU PING-HSIANG, (吳炳湘).—Courtesy name: Ching-t'an (鏡潭). Born 1876 in and native of Hofei, Anhwei. 1913, chief of police, Peking. 1919-20, concurrently director of metropolitan municipal administration. — p. 996

**WU T'ieh-ch'eng, (吳鐵城)** WU T'IEH-CH'ENG, (吳鐵城).—Born 1888; native of Hsiangshan, Kwangtung. Studied in Japan. 1911 Revolution, assistant chief of staff, Kiangsi military government; concurrently commissioner of foreign affairs, Kiukiang. 1918, councillor, Canton Military Government. 1920, adjutant to President Sun Yat-sen. 1921, elected magistrate of native district. 1923, chief of police for Canton and also entire Province. 1924, commander, Kwangtung provincial gendarmerie, and 1st independent division; acting adjutant-general to Dr. Sun Yat-sen. 1925, garrison commander, Canton; commander of 17th division, Nationalist Army. 1928, member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee, and Central Political Council. Member, Legislative Yuan, since October 1928. March 1929, re-elected member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. — p. 996

**WU Ting-ch'ang, (吳鼎昌)** WU TING-CH'ANG, (吳鼎昌).—Native of Szechwan. Sometime president, Ta Ching Bank and Bank of China; director of Tientsin Mint. 1920, vice-minister of finance. President, Salt Bank, Peking, since 1925. — p. 996

**WU Tseng-yu, (吳曾愈)** WU TSENG-YU, (吳曾愈).—Courtesy name: Hsi-chih (希之). Born 1894 in and native of Foochow, Fukien. Graduate, Tsing Hua College. 1918, Litt.B. (Princeton). Manager, Chinese-American Bank of Commerce, in Tientsin, Tsinan, Shanghai and Harbin for last ten years. Reappointed manager of same Bank in Shanghai since December 1928. — p. 996

**WU Tsung-tz'u, (吳宗慈)** WU TSUNG-TZ'U, (吳宗慈).—Courtesy name: Ai-lin ([illegible]林). Born 1879 in and native of Nanfang, Kiangsi. Studied in Japan. 1911, founder, Hsing Hu[illegible] Pao, Peking. 1913, 1916 and 1922, member of parliament. 1914-5, acting civil governor of Szechwan. 1917-21, member, Canton Extraordinary Parliament and Military Government. Author of two bulky volumes on origin and development of 1923 Permanent Constitution. — p. 996

**WU Yu-jui, (伍毓瑞)** WU YU-JUI, (伍毓瑞).—Courtesy name: Hsiao-yen (肖巖). Born 1885 in and native of Nanchang, Kiangsi. Graduate, Tokyo Officers Training Academy. 1913, garrison commander, Nanchang. 1918, commander, Kiangsi 4th army. 1924, commander, Chienkuochun 2nd army. 1926, senior councilllor, Nationalist 3rd army headquarters. Mayor of Nanchang, since 1928. — p. 996

**WU Yu-lin, (吳毓麟)** WU YU-LIN, (吳毓麟).—Courtesy name: Ch'iu-fang (秋舫). Born 1869 in and native of Tientsin. Graduate, Peiyang naval college. 1913, director, Taku naval dockyard. 1922, managing director, Tientsin-Pukow and Pukow-Hsinyang railways. 1923-4, minister of communications. — p. 996

**YANG Ai-yuan, (楊愛源)** YANG AI-YUAN, (楊愛源).—Courtesy name: Hsin-ju (星如). Born 1886; native of Wut'ai, Shansi. Graduate, Paoting military academy. Sometime commander, 6th division; commander-in-chief of right wing, Nationalist Third Group Army. Chairman, Charhar Provincial Government, since November 1928. — p. 996

**YANG Ch'eng, (楊晟)** YANG CH'ENG, (楊晟).—Courtesy name: Shao-ch'uan (少川). Prefers to sign himself: Yang Tcheng. Born 1867; native of Tungkuan, Kwangtung. Graduate, Canton language school and Berlin University. Sometime minister to Austria, Germany and Holland. 1926-7, director, labour emigration bureau. — p. 996

**YANG Ch'eng-hsun, (楊承訓)** YANG CH'ENG-HSUN, (楊承訓).—Prefers English rendering: C. H. Young. Born 1892; native of Changsha, Hunan. Graduate, Tsing Hua College, and Massachusetts Institute of Technology. 1925, professor of natural sciences, Great China University, Shanghai. 1927-8, managing director, Peking-Hankow Railway. Managing director, Lung-Hai Railway, since June 1929. — p. 996

**YANG Chieh, (楊杰)** YANG CHIEH, (楊杰).—Courtesy name: Keng-kuang (耿光). Born 1889 in and native of Tali, Yunnan. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. 1913, chief of police, Chungking; commander, Yunnan 5th mixed brigade. 1919, adviser, headquarters of Szechwan-Yunnan-Kweichow combined forces. 1921, director of military students in Japan. 1924, chief of staff, Kuominchun 3rd Army. 1926, chief counsellor, Nationalist 6th army. 1927, commander, 18th army. 1928, principal, Central Military College, Nanking. Chief counsellor, 1st Group Army headquarters, and director of department, Commander-in-Chief's Headquarters, as well as principal, College of Military Police, since December 1928. Author of several treatises on military tactics during the Great War. — p. 997

**YANG I-teh, (楊以德)** YANG I-TEH, (楊以德).—Courtesy name: Ching-lin (敬林). Born 1871 in and native of Tientsin. Sometime police commissioner of Chihli and chief of Tientsin police. 1924, civil governor of Chihli. — p. 997

**YANG Ju-mei, (楊汝梅)** YANG JU-MEI, (楊汝梅).—Courtesy name: Yü-chieh (予戒). Born 1879 in and native of Suihsien, Hupeh. Graduate, Tokyo High Commercial College. 1917-27, presiding auditor, board of audit; technical expert, commission for readjustment of finance. — p. 997

**YANG Kuang-sheng, (楊光泩)** YANG KUANG-SHENG, (楊光泩).—Prefers English rendering: Clarence Kuangson Young. Born 1900 in and native of Shanghai. Graduate, Tsing Hua College. 1921, B.A. (Colorado). 1924, Ph.D. (Princeton). 1924-7, secretary, Legation in Washington and delegation to Geneva International Opium Conferences; professor of Chinese, Georgetown University; professor of political science, Tsing Hua College. Assistant director, publicity and intelligence department, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, since October 1928. Author of "Rights of Aliens in the United States" (doctoral dissertation.) — p. 997

**YANG Pao-ling, (楊豹靈)** YANG PAO-LING, (楊豹靈).—Born 1887 in and native of Soochow, Kiangsu. 1911, C. E. (Purdue). Sometime departmental chief, national conservancy bureau. 1917-27, technical expert, Chihli river improvement commission; co-director, Tientsin telegraph administration. Past vice-president, Association of Chinese and American Engineers. — p. 997

**YANG Sen** YANG SEN, see below. — p. 997

**YANG Shen, (楊森)** YANG SHEN, (楊森).—Courtesy name: Tzu-hui (子惠). Born 1883; native of Kuangan, Szechwan. Graduate, Chengtu military institute. 1916, chief of staff, Hukuochun 2nd army. 1920, commander, Szechwan 9th division. 1921, commander, Szechwan 2nd army. 1924, director-general of military affairs, Szechwan. Commander, Nationalist 20th army, since 1926. 1927, commander-in-chief, 5th Route Army. Member, Szechwan Provincial Government, since 1928. — p. 997

**YANG Shu-chuang, (楊樹[illegible])** YANG SHU-CHUANG, (楊樹[illegible]).—Courtesy name: Yu-ching (幼京). Born 1882; native of Foochow, Fukien. Graduate of Nanking naval school. 1914, commander of Fei Ying. 1921, commander of training squadron. 1922, acting commander-in-chief of navy. 1929, promoted full admiral and commander-in-chief of navy. 1926, co-operated with Northern Expedition along the Yangtze and Fukien waters. State Councillor of National Government and Chairman, Fukien Provincial Government, since October 1928. Concurrently member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee and Central Political Council. May 1929 appointed Minister of Navy. — p. 997

**YANG Tcheng** YANG TCHENG, see YANG CH'ENG. — p. 997

**YANG Tzu-i, (楊子毅)** YANG TZU-I, (楊子毅).—Prefers English rendering: T. Y. Young. Born 1883; native of Chungshan, Kwangtung. Studied in Breslau University. Sometime departmental chief, Ministry of Finance, Canton; magistrate of P'anyü (Kwangtung); secretary, Municipal Government of Greater Canton. 1928, officiatting director, department of civil affairs, Chekiang Provincial Government. — p. 997

**YANG Yung-ch'ing, (楊永淸)** YANG YUNG-CH'ING, (楊永淸).—Courtesy name: Hui-ch'ing (惠慶). Prefers English rendering: Y. C. Yang. Born 1892; native of Soochow, Kiangsu. M.A., LL.B. (George Washington). 1919-21, secretary, delegation to League of Nations Assembly and Washington Conference. 1923-7, secretary, commission for readjustment of finance; assistant director, conference department, Customs Conference; secretary, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. Elected President of Soochow University, Soochow, since October 1927. — p. 998

**YANG Yung-t'ai, (楊永泰)** YANG YUNG-T'AI, (楊永泰).—Courtesy name: Ch'ang-ch'ing (暢卿). Born 1880; native of Maoming, Kwangtung. 1913, 1916 and 1922, senator. 1917-8, member, Canton Extraordinary Parliament. 1920, commissioner of finance and civil governor, Canton. 1926, associate delegate, Customs Conference. — p. 998

**YAO Chen, (姚震)** YAO CHEN, (姚震).—Courtesy name: Tz'u-chih ([illegible]之). Born 1872; native of Anhwei. Law graduate, Waseda University. 1911 and 1918, chief justice, Supreme Court. 1917 and 1927, minister of justice. 1920, proscribed for membership of Anfu Party until pardoned 1923. — p. 998

**YAO Kuo-chen, (姚國楨)** YAO KUO-CHEN, (姚國楨).—Courtesy name: Yu-chih (幼枝). Born 1883; native of Kueichih, Anhwei. Chüjen of imperial examinations. 1919, officiating minister of communications. 1920, proscribed for membership of Anfu Party until pardoned 1923. — p. 998

**YAO Tsung, (姚琮)** YAO TSUNG, (姚琮).—Courtesy name: Wei-hsin (味辛). Native of Juian, Chekiang. Sometime chief instructor, Whampoa Military Academy; senior councillor, Nationalist Commander-in-Chief's headquarters. Commissioner of police, Nanking, since 1928. — p. 998

**YEH Ching-hsin, (葉景莘)** YEH CHING-HSIN, (葉景莘).—Courtesy name: Shu-heng (叔衡). Prefers English rendering: T. H. Yeh. Born 1882 in and native of Hangchow, Chekian[illegible]. 1911, B.Sc. (Birmingham). 1917-27, director, currency department, Minis[illegible]y of Finance; technical expert, commission for readjustment of finance. D[illegible]ector, public loans department, Ministry of Finance, since September, 1928. — p. 998

**YEH Ch'u-ts'ang, (葉楚傖)** YEH CH'U-TS'ANG, (葉楚傖).—Pen name: Hsiao-feng (小鳳). Born [illegible]1883; native of Soochow, Kiangsu. 1912, editor, Min Li Pao. 1916, editor-i[illegible]chief. Min Kuo Jih Pao (Shanghai). 1926, acting chief secretary, Kuomintang [illegible]entral Political Council; confidential secretary, Northern Expedition comma[illegible]der-in-chief's headquarters. 1927, member, Shanghai provisional branch politica[illegible]council. Member, Kiangsu Provincial Government, and concurrently dire[illegible]or of Reconstruction Department, as well as acting director of publicity dep[illegible]tment, Central Kuomintang Headquarters, since 1928. March 1929, elected [illegible]mber, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. May 1929, elected membe[illegible] Kuomintang Central Political Council. — p. 998

**YEH K'e-liang, (葉可樑)** YEH K'E-LIANG, (葉可樑).—Courtesy name: Hsiao-ho (肖鶴). Prefers [illegible] sign himself: Yih Ko-liang. Born 1881 in and native of Foochow, Fukien. [illegible]11-3, director, Peking Agricultural College. 1914-28, 2nd secretary, Leg[illegible]on in Washington; consul in Vancouver; consul general in San Francisco and [illegible]nila. — p. 998

**YEH Kung-cho, (葉恭綽)** YEH KUNG-CHO, (葉恭綽).—Courtesy name: Yü-hu (譽虎). Born [illegible]80 in and native of P'anyü, Kwangtung. 1919, special commissioner to stud[illegible]ndustries and communications in Europe. 1920, 1922 and 1924, minister [illegible]communications. 1921, high commissioner for promotion of industries. 1925[illegible]delegate, Customs Conference. — p. 998

**YEH Tsai-chun, (葉在均)** YEH TSAI-CHUN, (葉在均).—Courtesy name: Nai-ch'ung (乃崇). Born 1885 in and native of Foochow, Fukien. Graduate, Peking Law College. Connected with the judiciary since 191. December 1928, reappointed senior judge, Supreme Court. — p. 999

**YEH, T. H** YEH, T. H., see YEH CHING-HSIN. — p. 999

**YEN Chih-i, (嚴智怡)** YEN CHIH-I, (嚴智怡).—Courtesy name: Tz'u-yueh (慈約). Born 1882 in and native of Tientsin. Graduate, Tokyo Technical College. 1915-6, departmental chief, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce. 1917-22, industrial commissioner of Chihli. Commissioner of Agriculture and Mining, Hopei (Chihli) Provincial Government, since September 1928. — p. 999

**YEN Fu-ch'ing, (顏福慶)** YEN FU-CH'ING, (顏福慶).—Courtesy name: K'e-ch'ing (克卿).—Prefers English rendering: F. C. Yen. Born 1880; native of Shanghai. Graduate, St. John's University. 1909, M.D. (Yale). 1910, D.T.M. (Liverpool). 1921, honorary M.A. (Yale); C.P.H. (Harvard). 1903-4, medical officer, Chinese labour camps in South Africa. 1914-26, president, Yale Medical College, Changsha. 1927-8, vice-director, Peking Union Medical College. Dean, College of Medicine, National Central University, since July 1928; concurrently superintendent, Chinese Red Cross Hospital, Shanghai. First president, National Medical Association, and vice-president, China Medical Association. Sometime field director, International Health Board of Rockfeller Foundation, for eradication of hook-worm disease at Pinghsiang colliery. — p. 999

**YEN, Hawkling** YEN, HAWKLING, see YEN HUO-LING. — p. 999

**YEN Hsi-shan, (閻錫山)** YEN HSI-SHAN, (閻錫山).—Courtesy name: Po-ch'uan (百川). Better known as the "Model Governor." Born 1882 in and native of Wut'ai, Shansi. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. 1911 Revolution, elected Tutuh (military governor) of Shansi. Military governor of "Model Province" since 1912. Concurrently civil governor since 1917. 1923, made field marshal. June 1927, commander-in-chief of National Revolutionary Army in North China; engaged the Mukden forces single-handed until relieved in May 1928 by main Nationalist armies. State Councillor of the National Government since October 1928. Commander-in-chief, Peiping and Tientsin Garrison, since June 1928. Chairman, Commission on Mongolian and Tibetan Affairs, since November 1928. Member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee and Central Political Council, since September, 1928. — p. 999

**YEN Hui-ch'ing, (顏惠慶)** YEN HUI-CH'ING, (顏惠慶).—Courtesy name: Chün-jen (駿人). Prefers English rendering: W. W. Yen. Born 1877 in and native of Shanghai. 1900, B.A. (Virginia). 1906, Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. 1912, vice-minister for foreign affairs. 1913-20, minister to Germany and Denmark; minister for foreign affairs. December 1921, concurrently acting premier. June-July 1922, reappointed acting premier. 1923-7, chairman, commission for readjustment of finance. 1924, minister of agriculture and commerce; premier and concurrently minister of interior; delegate, Customs Conference; appointed minister to London. 1925-6, premier and acting minister for foreign affairs. 1924-8, trustee, China Foundation for Promotion of Education and Culture. Author of "Anglo-Chinese Dictionary" and translator of Walker's "Political Economy," etc. President, Chinese Social and Political Science Association, Peking. Past president, China Red Cross Society. — p. 999

**YEN Huo-ling, (嚴鶴齡)** YEN HUO-LING, (嚴鶴齡).—Courtesy name: Lü-ch'in (侶琴). Prefers English rendering: Hawkling Yen. Born 1879 in and native of Yüyao, Chekiang. Graduate, St. John's University. 1909, M.A.; 1911, Ph.D. (Columbia). 1919, councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, and acting president, Tsing Hua College. 1920-2, secretary, Opium Section, League of Nations Secretariat, Geneva; secretary general, delegation to Washington Conference. 1924-6, vice-minister of agriculture and commerce and of interior; secretary general, Customs Conference. Associate General Secretary, Honolulu Institute of Pacific Relations, since April 1929. Author of "A Survey of Constitutional Development in China" (doctoral dissertation). First editor (1916-8), Chinese Social and Political Science Review, Peking. — p. 999

**YEN, James Y. C** YEN, JAMES Y. C., see YEN YANG-CH'U. — p. 1000

**YEN Teh-ch'ing, (顏德慶)** YEN TEH-CH'ING, (顏德慶).—Courtesy name: Chi-yü (季餘). Born 1878 in and native of Shanghai. 1901, C.E. (Lehigh). 1906, Chinshih of imperial examinations. 1912-21, managing director, Canton-Hankow and Szechwan-Hankow railways; technical expert, delegation to Washington Conference. 1922-8, director, technical department, Ministry of Communications; commissioner, Sino-Japanese conference on Shantung affairs; technical expert, Customs Conference; councillor, Ministry of Railways. Managing director, Kiaochow-Tsinan Railway, since May 1929. Past president, Association of Chinese and American Engineers, Peking Rotary Club and American College Club of North China. — p. 1000

**YEN Tseh-p'u, (閻澤溥)** YEN TSEH-P'U, (閻澤溥).—Courtesy name: T'ing-jui (庭瑞). Sometime managing director of famine relief in Mukden and Shantung. 1927, minister of finance. — p. 1000

**YEN, W. W** YEN, W. W., see YEN HUI-CH'ING. — p. 1000

**YEN Yang-ch'u, (晏陽初)** YEN YANG-CH'U, (晏陽初).—Prefers English rendering: James Y. C. Yen. Born 1894; native of Hunan. B.A. (Yale). M.A. (Princeton). 1928, honorary M.A. (Yale). 1918-3, Y.M.C.A. secretary with Chinese labour corps in France; joint inventor of 1,000 simplest Chinese characters for teaching labourers. Founder and general secretary, National Association for Advancement of Mass Education, Peking, since 1925. Author of "Mass Education in China." — p. 1000

**YIH Ko-liang** YIH KO-LIANG, see YEH K'E-LIANG. — p. 1000

**YIN Ch'ang, (蔭昌)** YIN CH'ANG, (蔭昌).—Courtesy name: Wu-lou (午樓). Manchu, of Plain White Banner Division. Born 1860. 1901-5, and 1908-10, minister to Germany. 1911, minister of war. 1912, 1917 and 1920, chief of general staff. 1919-24, chief aide-de-camp, President's Office. 1923, made field marshal. — p. 1000

**YIN Ju-keng, ([illegible]汝耕)** YIN JU-KENG, ([illegible]汝耕).—Courtesy name: I-nung (亦農). Born 1889; native of P'ingyang, Chekiang. 1916, graduate in political science, Waseda University. 1916, secretary, House of Representatives. 1918, representative in Japan of Canton Military Government. 1920, financial adviser to civil governor of Yunnan. 1925, adviser, Customs Conference, Peking. 1927, adviser, Commander-in-Chief's headquarters, Nanking; secretary, Municipal Government of Greater Shanghai. Director, navigation department, Ministry of Communications, since 1928. — p. 1000

**YO Tsao-yeu** YO TSAO-YEU, see YUEH CHAO-I. — p. 1000

**YOUNG, C. H** YOUNG, C. H., see YANG CH'ENG-HSUN. — p. 1000

**YOUNG, Clarence Kuangson** YOUNG, CLARENCE KUANGSON, see YANG KUANG-SHENG. — p. 1000

**YOUNG, Samuel S** YOUNG, SAMUEL S., see HSIUNG CH'UNG-CHIH. — p. 1000

**YOUNG, T. Y** YOUNG, T. Y., see YANG TZU-I. — p. 1000

**YU Ch'i-ch'ang, (余棨昌)** YU CH'I-CH'ANG, (余棨昌).—Courtesy name: Chi-men (戟門). Born 1882; native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. 1911, LL.B. (Tokyo Imperial). 1917 and 1922, chief of division, Supreme Court. 1923-7, chief justice, Supreme Court. — p. 1000

**YU Chun-chi, (于焌吉)** YU CHUN-CHI, (于焌吉).—Courtesy name: Ch'ien-liu (謙六). Prefers English rendering: James T. C. Yü. Born 1899 in and native of Shengfang, Hopei. Graduate, Nankai Middle School. 1921, Ph.B., B.S. (Denison). 1922-23, A.B., M.A. in political science (Columbia). 1923-24, Sc.M., Sc.D. in economic geography (New York). 1927, Ph.D. in public law (Columbia). Senior member, Treaty Commission, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, since December 1928. Author of "The Interpretation of Treaties," and "Ecoomic Geographic Problems in Manchuria." — p. 1001

**YU Hsia-ch'ing, (虞洽卿)** YU HSIA-CH'ING, (虞洽卿).—Courtesy name: Ho-teh (和德). Better known as: Yu Ya-ching. Born 1865 in and native of Chenhai, Chekiang. One of most prominent merchants in Shanghai. 1925, chairman, Chinese General Chamber of Commerce; associate director, Shanghai-Woosung Administrative District. 1926, chairman, Chinese Ratepayers' Association. Managing director, San Peh Steam Navigation Company, Shanghai Chinese Produce Exchange, and a number of other companies. April 1929, elected councillor, Shanghai Municipal Council. — p. 1001

**YU, James T. C** YU, JAMES T. C., see YU CHUN-CHI. — p. 1001

**YU Jen-feng, (俞人鳳)** YU JEN-FENG, (俞人鳳).—Courtesy name: Hui-wu (翽梧). Born 1872; native of Tientsin. Graduate, Peiyang Military College. 1920, managing director, Peking-Hankow Railway. 1924-6, director general, Chinese Eastern Railway. — p. 1001

**YU Jih-chang, (余日章)** YU JIH-CHANG, (余日章).—Prefers English rendering: David Z. T. Yui. Born 1882 in and native of Wu-chang, Hupeh. Graduate, St. John's University. 1910, M.A. (Harvard). General secretary, National Committee of Y.M.C.A., Shanghai, since 1916. 1921, people's co-delegate to Washington Conference; honorary Litt. D. (St. John's University). 1923, chairman, National Christian Council. Member, Chinese Advisory Committee, Shanghai Municipal Council. — p. 1001

**YU Pao-hsien, (于寶軒)** YU PAO-HSIEN, (于寶軒).—Courtesy name: Chih-ang (志昂). Born 1875; native of Kiangtu, Kiangsu. 1916, senator. 1919, acting minister of interior. 1920-2, director, economic investigation bureau. — p. 1001

**YU T'ung-k'uai, (俞同奎)** YU T'UNG-K'UAI, (俞同奎).—Courtesy name: Hsin-ch'ü (星樞). Prefers English rendering: T. K. Yu. Native of Tehch'ing, Chekiang. 1910, B.Sc.; 1911, M.Sc. (Liverpool). 1912-20, dean, department of chemistry, Peking National University. 1920-3, principal, Engineering College, Peking. 1924, departmental chief, Ministry of Communications. 1925, technical expert, same ministry 1927, dean, departments of physics and chemistry, Peking National University. 1928, councillor, Ministry of Agriculture and Mining, Nanking. Principal, Engineering College, Peiping National University, since November 1928. — p. 1001

**YU Wen-ts'an, (俞文燦)** YU WEN-TS'AN, (俞文燦).—Courtesy name: Yü-san (育三). Prefers English rendering: W. T. Yu. Born 1892 in and native of T'aishan, Kwangtung. Graduate, Tsing Hua College. 1916, Ph.B. (Chicago). 1919, LL.B. (Harvard). Sometime acting chancellor, Peking National University; professor of economics, same University; departmental chief, Ministry of Education. Director, department of general affairs, Ministry of Education, since 1918. — p. 1001

**YU Ya-ching** YU YA-CHING, see YU HSIA-CH'ING. — p. 1001

**YU Yu-jen, (于右任)** YU YU-JEN, (于右任).—Courtesy name: Po-hsun (伯循). Born 1882; native of Sanyuan, Shensi. Chüjen of imperial examinations. 1908, established various daily journals in Shanghai to preach revolutionary ideas. 1912, vice-minister of communications, Nanking. 1915, elected commander of Shensi Army to oppose Yuan Shih-kai's monarchical scheme. 1925, appointed minister of interior, Peking, but did not assume office. Member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee, since 1924. Member, Kuomintang Central Political Council, since 1926. President, Board of Audit, since 1928. — p. 1001

**YUAN Chia-p'u, (袁家普)** YUAN CHIA-P'U, (袁家普).—Courtesy name: Hsueh-an (雪安). Born 1874; native of Liling, Hunan. Graduate, Waseda University. 1913, dean and acting president, Kuomin University, Peking. 1914, commissioner of finance, Yunnan. 1916-7, commissioner of finance and acting civil governor of Honan. Commissioner of Finance, Shantung Provincial Government, since May 1929. Concurrently member, Sino-Japanese Joint Commission for assessment of damages in connection with Tsinan Incident. — p. 1002

**YUAN Hsi-t'ao, (袁希濤)** YUAN HSI-T'AO, (袁希濤).—Courtesy name: Kuan-lan (觀瀾). Born 1865 in and native of Paoshan, Kiangsu. Chüjen of imperial examinations. 1904-8, founder and president, Lungmen Normal College, Shanghai. 1918-9, officiating minister of education. Resigned on account of inability to check Student Movement (May 1919). — p. 1002

**YUAN K'e-ting, (袁克定)** YUAN K'E-TING, (袁克定).—Native of Honan. Eldest son of Yuan Shih-k'ai. 1918-22, adviser, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. Formerly Director general of Kailan Mining Administration. Res. Tientsin. — p. 1002

**YUAN K'e-wen, (袁克文)** YUAN K'E-WEN, (袁克文).—Courtesy name: Pao-ts'en (豹岑). Born 1890; native of Honan. Second son of Yuan Shih-k'ai. 1911, influenced father to throw in his lot with the revolutionary leaders. 1914, co-director of Fu Chung Corporation and compiler in bureau of Ch'ing dynasty historiography. Considered one of most famous writers in Chinese light literature world to-day. Collector of coins, on which subject he has written several books. — p. 1002

**YUAN Li-tun, (袁禮敦)** YUAN LI-TUN, (袁禮敦).—Courtesy name: Lü-teng (履登). Prefers English rendering: L. T. Yuan. Born 1879 in and native of Ningpo, Chekiang. Graduate, St. John's University. 1904-12, principal, English Methodist College, Ningpo. General manager, Ningpo-Shaohsing Steam Navigation Company, Shanghai, since 1921. 1923, member, Chinese Advisory Committee, Shanghai Municipal Council. 1927, committee member, same Council. Councillor, same body, since 1928. — p. 1002

**YUAN, S. D** YUAN, S. D., see YUEN SHANG-CH'IEH. — p. 1002

**YUAN T'ung-li, (袁同禮)** YUAN T'UNG-LI, (袁同禮).—Prefers English rendering: T. L. Yuan. Born 1895; native of Tientsin, Hopei. Graduate, Peking National University. Bachelor of Library Science, New York State Library School, Albany. Postgraduate studies in London. Sometime librarian, Tsing Hua College; member, Palace Museum Commission; president, Peking Library Association. Director, Metropolitan Library, Peking, since 1927. Secretary, Library Association of China. — p. 1002

**YUEH Chao-i, (岳昭燏)** YUEH CHAO-I, (岳昭燏).—Prefers to sign himself: Yo Tsao-yeu. Born 1879; native of Kashing, Chekiang. 1919, first secretary, Legation in Paris. 1920. councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. 1924-8, minister to Mexico. — p. 1002

**YUEN Shang-chieh, (阮尚介)** YUEN SHANG-CHIEH, (阮尚介).—Courtesy name: Chieh-fan (介蕃). Prefers to sign himself: S. D. Yuan. 1914, doctor of medicine (Berlin). 1915, adviser, Ministry of War. 1917-27, president, Tung Chi University, Shanghai. — p. 1002

**YUI, David Z. T** YUI, DAVID Z. T., see YU JIH-CHANG. — p. 1002

**CHANG Carson** CHANG CARSON, see CHANG CHIA-SHAN. — p. 919

**CHANG, Cheuk K** CHANG, CHEUK K., see CHANG TSO-K'UN. — p. 919

**CHANG Chi, (張繼)** CHANG CHI, (張繼).—Courtesy name: P'u-ch'uan, (溥泉).—Born 1882 in and native of Ts'anghsien, Hopei (Chihli). Studied in Japan; joined Tung Meng Hui. Resided for some time also in France. 1920, publicity agent in Japan for Canton Government. 1924, member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. 1927, member, joint council of Kuomintang Central Executive Committee and Central Supervisory Committee. 1928, member, Kuomintang Central Politcal Council; chairman, Hopei branch of Kuomintang Central Political Council. State Councillor and Vice-President, Judicial Yuan, since October 1928. March 1929, elected member, Kuomintang Central Supervisory Committee. — p. 919

**CHANG Chia-ao, (張嘉璈)** CHANG CHIA-AO, (張嘉璈).—Courtesy name: Kung-ch'uan (公權). Prefers to sign himself: Chang Kia-ngau. Born 1888 in and native of Paoshan, Kiangsu. Graduate, Keio University, Tokyo. 1912, chief secretary of the Senate. Vice-Governor, Bank of China, virtually since 1917. — p. 919

**CHANG Chia-shen, (張嘉森)** CHANG CHIA-SHEN, (張嘉森).—Courtesy name: Chün-mai (君勵). Prefers English rendering: Carson Chang. Born 1886; native of Paoshan, Kiangsu. Graduate, Waseda University, Tokyo. Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. 1911-2, editor-in-chief, Peking-Tientsin Shih Pao; assistant editor, Justice. 1916, chief secretary, bureau of foreign affairs, Hangchow. A resident of Shanghai and professor in various colleges since 1917. — p. 919

**CHANG Chien, (張謙)** CHANG CHIEN, (張謙).—Courtesy name: Kunghui (公撝). Prefers English rendering: Henry K. Chang. Born 1888, in and native of Hsinhui, Kwangtung. Graduate, Wharton School and Law School of the University of Pennsylvania, LL.B. 1909. Passed Government Civil Service Examination and was given the degree of Chujen (舉人), 1909. Honorary Secretary of the Chinese Legation, Washington, 1910-1913. Chief Secretary to the Chinese Educational Mission to the United States, 1910. Manager, Oriental Savings Bank, 1924. Adviser to the former Provincial Government of Hopei, 1925. Deputy Councillor of the Ministry of Finance, 1927. Managing Director, China Investment Corporation, 1927. Elected Councillor of British Municipal Council of Tientsin, 1929. Appointed Consul-General at San Francisco by Mandate of the State Council, 1929. Contributor to such magazines as the Survey and Review of Reviews. — p. 919

**CHANG Chih-chiang, (張之江)** CHANG CHIH-CHIANG, (張之江).—Born 1881; native of Yenshan, Chihli. Sometime Tupan (military governor) of Northwest Frontier and Tutung of Charhar. 1926, took command of Kuominchun armies during Feng Yü-hsiang's sojourn in Moscow. 1927, member, State Council, Nanking. Chairman, National Opium Suppression Commission, since October 1928. — p. 919

**CHANG Chih-pen, (張知本)** CHANG CHIH-PEN, (張知本).—Courtesy name: Huai-chiu (懷九). Native of Chianglin, Hupeh. Graduate, Tokyo Law College. Sometime commissioner of justice, Hupeh Province; president, Hupeh Law College. 1924, member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. 1927-8, chairman, Hupeh Provincial Government; concurrently member, Wuhan branch, Kuomintang Central Political Council. — p. 920

**CH'ANG Chih-ying, (常之英)** CH'ANG CHIH-YING, (常之英).—Courtesy name: Ts'ai-yuen (采園). Prefers to sign himself: Chang Tse-ying. Native of Shantung. Graduate, Peiyang Police College. 1925, commissoner of police, Woosung and Shanghai. 1927, commissioner of police, Tientsin. — p. 920

**CHANG Chin-chiang** CHANG CHIN-CHIANG, see CHANG JEN-CHIEH. — p. 920

**CHANG Ching-hui, (張景惠)** CHANG CHING-HUI, (張景惠).—Courtesy name: Hsü-wu (叙五). Born 1873 in and native of Taian, Fengtien. 1919, commander, 27th division. 1926, minister of war. 1927, minister of industry. Chief Civil Administrator, Harbin, since October 1928. — p. 920

**CHANG Ching-yao, (張敬堯)** CHANG CHING-YAO, (張敬堯). Courtesy name: Hsün-ch'en (勳臣). Born 1881 in and native of Huoch'u, Anhwei. Attended Paoting Military Academy. 1914, commander, 7th division. 1918-20, military governor of Hunan. — p. 920

**CHANG Ch'un, (張羣)** CHANG CH'UN, (張羣). Courtesy name: Yueh-chün (岳軍). Born 1888; native of Szechwan. Graduate, Paoting military academy, and Tokyo Military College. 1912, served under General Ch'en Ch'i-mei. 1913, fled to Japan after failure of Second Revolution. 1920, chief of police, Chengtu (Szechwan). 1922, chief of police, Kaifeng (Honan). 1927, chief councillor, commander-in-chief's headquarters, Northern Expedition. 1928, director, Shanghai Arsenal; vice-minister of military affairs. Transferred Mayor of Shanghai since March 1929; at the same time elected member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. — p. 920

**CHANG Feng-chu, (張鳳舉)** CHANG FENG-CHU, (張鳳舉).—Prefers to sign himself: Tchang Fong-kiu. Pen name: Chang Ting-huang (張定璜). Born 1895 in and native of Nanchang, Kiangsi. 1921, Bungakushi, Imperal University of Kyoto. 1921-27, professor of literature, National University and Sino-French University, Peiping. Dean, College of Literature, National University of Peiping, since September 1928. — p. 920

**CHANG, Frank** CHANG, FRANK, see Chang Ying-fang. — p. 920

**CHANG Fu-yun, (張福運)** CHANG FU-YUN, (張福運).—Born 1890; native of Fushan, Shantung. 1914, B.A.; 1917, LL.B. (Harvard). 1918, lecturer on Roman Law, Peking Government University. 1919-2, secretary to Chinese High Commissioner in Siberia and delegation to Washington Conference. 1922, director, department of navigation, Ministry of Communications. 1923, president, Communications University, Peking. Director of Customs Administration, Ministry of Finance, and concurrently Chairman, National Tariff Commission, since 1928. — p. 920

**CHANG, Henry K** CHANG, HENRY K., see CHANG CHIEN. — p. 920

**CHANG Hsi-ch'ien, (張希[illegible])** CHANG HSI-CH'IEN, (張希[illegible]).—Born 1890; native of Chengtu, Szechwan. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. 1916, commander of military police, Szechwan Province. 1922, chief instructor, Chengtu military institute. 1927, chief of staff, Nationalist 24th army. 1928, director of communications, Commander-in-Chief's headquarters. Director, Bureau of Ceremonial, National Government Headquarters, since November 1928. Translator of military manuals. — p. 920

**CHANG Hsiang-lin, (張祥麟)** CHANG HSIANG-LIN, (張祥麟).—Prefers English rendering: Z. L. Chang. Born 1890; native of Shanghai. Studied in St. John's University, Shanghai, and Columbia University, New York. 1921, secretary, delegation to Washington Conference. 1922-7, consul general at New York; assistant commissioner to Philadelphia Exposition. — p. 920

**CHANG Hsiao-jo, (張孝若)** CHANG HSIAO-JO, (張孝若).—Courtesy name: Yi-tsu (詔祖). Son of late Chang Ch'ien. Born 1895 in and native of Nantungchow, Kiangsu. 1918, B.C.S. (Arnold Business College). 1919-21, president, administration of Nantungchow industrial enterprises; member, Kiangsu provincial assembly. 1923-24, industrial commissioner to Europe and U.S.A.; appointed minister to Chile, but did not proceed. — p. 921

**CHANG Hsin-hai, (張歆海)** CHANG HSIN-HAI, (張歆海).—Prefers English rendering: H. H. Chang. Born 1898 and native of Haiyien, Chekiang. 1918, graduate, Tsing Hua College. 1919. B.A. (John Hopkins). 1920, M.A. (Harvard). 1922, Ph.D. (Harvard). 1923, professor of English literature, Tsing Hua College and Peking National University. 1925, adviser, Customs Conference. 1926, professor of English, Southeastern University, Nanking. 1927, vice-President, Kwang Hua University. Councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affars, since June 1928. Member, Board of Directors, Tsing Hua University. Contributing editor, Chinese Social and Political Science Review. Contributor to the Yale Review, The Atlantic Monthly, The Edinburgh Review, The Hibbert Journal, etc. Author of "Matthew Arnold and His Humanistic View of Life" (doctoral dissertation). — p. 921

**CHANG, H. H** CHANG, H. H. see above. — p. 921

**CHANG Hsin-lang, (張星烺)** CHANG HSIN-LANG, (張星烺).—Courtesy name: Liang-ch'en (亮丞). Born 1887 in Ssu Yang Hsien, Kiangsu. 1909, B.Sc. (Harvard). 1909-12, research work in Berlin University; Berlin correspondent of "National Herald" (Shanghai). 1913-4, dean, chemistry department, Changsha Technical College. 1914-7, professor of chemistry, Peking Government University. 1917-8, senior compiler, Bureau for the Compilation of National History. 1926-7, dean, graduate school of Chinese studies, Amoy University. Professor of history, Yen-ching University and Catholic University, Peiping, since 1927. Author of "History of the Intercourse between China and the Western Countries" (中英交通史料), six volumes (Catholic University Press), and Chinese edition of "Marco Polo's Travels" (馬哥孛羅遊記) five volumes (Yenching University, three volumes still in press.) Editor and secretary, Chinese Geographical Society, Peiping. — p. 921

**CHANG Hsui-ching, (張修敬)** CHANG HSUI-CHING, (張修敬).—Courtesy name: Ching Ch'ien (競騫). Born 1888; native of Nanking. Graduate, Tokyo Artillery School. 1927, chief of staff, Nationalist 6th army. 1928, vice-dean, Whampoa Military Academy. Director of artillery training, Directorate-General of Military Training, since November 1928. — p. 921

**CHANG Hsueh-liang, (張學良)** CHANG HSUEH-LIANG, (張學良). Courtesy name: Hanch'ing (漢卿). Son of late Chang Tso-lin and better known as the "Young General." Born 1898 in and native of Haich'eng, Fengtien. Graduate, Mukden Military Academy. 1922, commander, Fengtien second army; principal, Mukden Military Academy. 1924, commander, Fengtien first army. 1926, commander, Fengtien third army. July 1928, elected to succeed his father as Commander-in-Chief for preserving the peace of Manchuria. State Councillor of the Naional Government since October 1928. January, 1929, Commander-in-Chief of Northeastern Frontier Defence. — p. 921

**CHANG Hsun-ch'in, (張訓欽)** CHANG HSUN-CH'IN, (張訓欽).—Native of Changsha, Hunan. Graduate, Tokyo Imperial University. 1924, vice-minister of finance. 1925, high adviser to Customs Conference; special commissioner for Hunan famine relief. — p. 921

**CHANG Hu, (張弧)** CHANG HU, (張弧).—Courtesy name: Tai-san (岱杉). Born 1876 in and native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. Chüjen of imperial examinations. 1913-5 and 1918, vice-minister of finance. 1917, director, labour emigration bureau. May 1922, ordered to be arrested. February 1923, pardoned. 1921-3 and 1925, minister of finance. — p. 921

**CHANG Hua-fu, (張華輔)** CHANG HUA-FU, (張華輔).—Courtesy name: Wei-sheng (慰生). Born 1887; native of Yinch'eng, Hupeh. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. 1916, chief of staff to defence commissioner of north Shansi. 1926, chief instructor, Whampoa Military Academy. 1928, member, Wuhan branch, Kuomintang Central Political Council; Chief of staff, Nationalist Fourth Group Army headquarters. — p. 922

**CHANG I-lin, (張一麐)** CHANG I-LIN, (張一麐).—Courtesy name: Chung-jen (仲仁). Born 1865 in and native of Soochow, Kiangsu. Chüjen of imperial examinations. Sometime secretary to Viceroy and President Yuan Shih-k'ai. 1915-22, minister of education; secretary to President Feng Kuo-chang; advisor to President Hsu Shih-ch'ang, member Kiangsu provincial assembly. — p. 922

**CHANG Jen-chieh, (張人傑)** CHANG JEN-CHIEH, (張人傑).—Courtesy name: Chin-chiang (靜江). Born 1873; native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. Towards the end of Manchu regime associated with Wu Chih-hui, Li Shih-tseng and other revolutionary leaders in Japan and France. One of the greatest admirers of Sun Yat-sen, sacrificed own wealth and property to further his cause. One of most influential Kuomintang "Elder Statesmen". 1924, member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. Member, Kuomintang Central Supervisory Committee and Central Political Council, since 1926. Chairman, Chekiang Provincial Government, since 1927. Chairman, National Reconstruction Commission, since 1928. — p. 922

**CHANG Kia-ngau** CHANG KIA-NGAU, see Chang Chia-ao. — p. 922

**CHANG Kuo-kan, (張國淦)** CHANG KUO-KAN, (張國淦).—Courtesy name: Kan-jo (乾若). Born 1873 in and native of P'uch'i, Hupeh. Chüjen of imperial examinations. 1914, vice-minister of interior. 1916 and 1924, minister of education; concurrently acting minister of justice. 1917, and 1922, minister of agriculture and commerce; concurrently acting minister of interior. 1920, director general of Hankow Port Construction; chief justice, administrative court. — p. 922

**CHANG Nai-yen, (張乃[illegible])** CHANG NAI-YEN, (張乃[illegible]).—Courtesy name: Chün-mou (君謀). Born 1894 in and native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. 1913-6, studied in Birmingham and London. 1919, D.Sc. (Geneva). 1920-22, professor of chemistry, Peking Government University. 1924, councellor, Generalissimo's headquarters, Canton. 1926, principal, engineering college, Canton University. 1927, commissioner of education, Kiangsu Province. Chancellor, National Central University, Nanking, and concurrently, member, Kiangsu Provincial Government, since May 1927. Author of "A la Connaissance des Acides Ortho-et para-nitro-alphabromocinnamiques" (doctoral thesis) and several scientific treatises, etc. — p. 922

**CHANG Ping-lin, (章炳麟)** CHANG PING-LIN, (章炳麟).—Courtesy name: T'ai-yen (太炎). Born 1867; native of Chekiang. Noted for his literary accomplishments and intense interest in politics. 1912, high adviser to the Cabinet, Nanking; political adviser to the President. 1917-8, participated in organization of Canton Military Government. Living in retirement, Shanghai. — p. 922

**CHANG Po-ling** CHANG PO-LING, see below. — p. 922

**CHANG Shou-ch'un, (張壽春)** CHANG SHOU-CH'UN, (張壽春).—Courtesy name: Po-ling (伯苓). Prefers to sign himself: Poling Chang. Born 1874 in and native of Tientsin. Graduate, Peiyang Naval College. 1917-8, attended Teachers' College, Columbia University. 1919, Litt.D. (St. John's) President, Nankai University, since 1904. 1924-6, trustee, China Foundation for Promotion of Education and Culture. Leader of Chinese Christian Church. — p. 922

**CHANG, Showbin W** CHANG, SHOWBIN W., see CHANG WEI. — p. 922

**CHANG Ta-i, (張大[illegible])** CHANG TA-I, (張大[illegible]).—Born 1883; native of Yunnan. Graduate of a law school in Japan. Member of Tung Meng Hui. 1912, chief secretary, ministry of interior, Nanking; technical expert, civil service bureau, Peking. 1913, member of parliament. Sometime chief judge, Yunnan High Court. — p. 923

**CHANG Tao-fan, (張道藩)** CHANG TAO-FAN, (張道藩).—Born 1897; native of P'anhsien, Kweichow. 1924, B.A. (London). 1926, secretary, department of agriculture and labour, Canton. Chief secretary, Municipal Government of Greater Nanking, and secretary, organization department, Central Kuomintang Headquarters, since September 1928. — p. 923

**CHANG Tse-ying** CHANG TSE-YING, see CH'ANG CHIH-YING. — p. 923

**CHANG Tso-hsiang, (張作相)** CHANG TSO-HSIANG, (張作相).—Courtesy name: Fu-ch'en (輔忱). Born 1882; native of Ihsien, Fengtien. 1918, chief of staff to inspector-general of Manchuria. 1919, concurrently commander, 27th division. 1924-8, military governor of Kirin. Chairman, Kirin Provincial Government, and Deputy Commander-in-Chief of North-eastern Frontier Defence, since December 1928. — p. 923

**CHANG Tso-k'un, (張焯堃)** CHANG TSO-K'UN, (張焯堃). Courtesy name: P'eng-nan (朋南). Prefers anglicized Cantonese rendering: Cheuk K. Chang. Born 1890; native of angllicized Cantonest rendering: Cheuk K. Chang. Born 1890; native of Kwangtung. 1916, B. S. (Cornell). 1921, agricultural expert to Canton Government. Director, Ling Nan Agricultural College, since July 1925. — p. 923

**CHANG Tsung-ch'ang, (張宗昌)** CHANG TSUNG-CH'ANG, (張宗昌). Courtesy name Hsiao-k'un (效坤). Born 1881; native of Shantung. Fought on Russia's side during Russo-Japanese war. 1917, superintendent of military education, Ministry of War. 1918-1922, division commander in Kiangsi; occupation commissioner, Suiling (Kirin). 1924, commander of Fengtien 1st Army. 1925, made field marshal. 1924-8, military governor of Shantung. Endeavoured to reconquer Shantung in March, 1929, but was defeated, fled and took refuge in Japan. — p. 923

**CHANG Tsung-hsiang, (章宗祥)** CHANG TSUNG-HSIANG, (章宗祥).—Courtesy name: Chung-ho (仲和). Born 1877 in and native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. 1903, LL.B. (Meiji, Tokyo). Sometime chief justice, Supreme Court; president, law codification commission; minister of justice. 1916-9, minister to Japan; branded "national traitor" by Student Movement together with Lu Tsung-yü and Ts'ao Yu-lin. — p. 923

**CHANG, V. H** CHANG, V. H., see Chang Wei-han. — p. 923

**CHANG Wei, (張瑋)** CHANG WEI, (張瑋).—Courtesy name: Hsiao-pin (傚彬). Prefers English rendering: Showbin W. Chang. Native of Kushih, Honan. 1910, B.A. (Cantab.) 1924-6, consul-general at Chita and Irkutsk. 1927, adviser, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. — p. 923

**CHANG Wei-han, (張維翰)** CHANG WEI-HAN, (張維翰).—Courtesy name: Tz'u-p'ing (次屏). Prefers English rendering: V. H. Chang. Born 1879; native of Fenghsiang, Shensi. 1913, member, Shensi provincial assembly. 1922-6, secretary, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce. Commissioner for Foreign Affairs, Yunnan, since 1927. — p. 923

**CHANG Wen-sheng, (張文生)** CHANG WEN-SHENG, (張文生)—Courtesy name: Hsing-wu ([illegible]五). Native of Kiangsu. Sometime defence commissioner of Suchow and Haichow, and military governor of Anhwei. — p. 923

**CHANG, Y. C** CHANG, Y. C., see CHANG YU-CH'UAN. — p. 923

**CHANG Yao-hsiang, (張耀翔)** CHANG YAO-HSIANG, (張耀翔).—Prefers to sign himself: Chang Yao-chiang. Born 1896 in and native of Hankow, Hupeh. Graduate, Tsing Hua College. 1918, B.A.; 1919, M.A. (Columbia). 1922-6, dean, department of education and psychology, Peking Normal University. Founder, Chinese Psychological Society: editor, Chinese Journal of Psychology. — p. 923

**CHANG Yao-tseng, (張耀曾)** CHANG YAO-TSENG, (張耀曾).—Courtesy name: Yung-hsi (容西). Born 18[illegible]4; native of Yunnan. LL. B., Waseda University, Tokyo. 1912, Yunnan delegate to organize Nanking Government. 1913 member of parliament. 1916, 1922, and 1925, minister of justice. 1923-7, chairman, commission on extraterritoriality. — p. 924

**CHANG Ying-fang, (陳膺方)** CHANG YING-FANG, (陳膺方).—Courtesy name: Ya-hsing (亞馨). Prefers English rendering: Frank Chang. Born 1889 in and native of Hengshui, Chihli. Attended Tientsin Ango-Chinese College. 1922-4, chief of military and naval audit bureau, Ministry of War. Author of a booklet in English and Chinese, "Why military expenditures should be independent." — p. 924

**CHANG Ying-hua, (張英華)** CHANG YING-HUA, (張英華).—Courtesy name: Yueh-sheng (月笙). Born 1886 in and native of Hengshui, Chihli. Attended Tientsin Anglo-Chinese College and Victoria University, Manchester. Sometime salt commissioner for Szechwan; commissioner of finance, Kansu; chairman, commission for the study of China's finances. 1926, for the third time acting minister of finance. — p. 924

**CHANG Yu-ch'uan, (張煜全)** CHANG YU-CH'UAN, (張煜全).—Courtesy name: Chiang-yün ([illegible]雲). Prefers English rendering: Y. C. Chang. Born 1880 in and native of Canton. Graduate, Peiyang University. 1903, LL.B., 1904, LL.M. (Yale). 1907, Chinshih of imperial examinations. Sometime commissioner for foregn affairs, Shanghai and Anking; president, Tsing Hua College. 1921-8, councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs; director, conference department, Customs Conference; technical expert, delegation to Washington Conference. Adviser, Headquarters of the Garrison Commander-in-Chief for Peiping and Tientsin, since July, 1928. — p. 924

**CHANG Yuan-chieh, (張元節)** CHANG YUAN-CHIEH, (張元節).—Courtesy name: Yen-nan (燕[illegible]). Native of Wusih, Kiangsu. Studied in Peking National University. 1921-7, 1st secretary, Legation in Tokyo, and chargé d'affaires for four times. — p. 924

**CHANG Yuan-shan, (章元善)** CHANG YUAN-SHAN, (章元善).—Courtesy name: Yen-hsün (彥訓). Prefers to sign himself: Y. S. Djang. Born 1892 in and native of Soochow, Kiangsu. Graduate, Tsing Hua College. 1915, B. A. (Cornell). Sometime chemist to Peiyang plague prevention bureau. Associate Executive Secretary, China International Famine Relief Commission, Peiping, since January, 1922. Honorary General Secretary, Peking Western Returned Students' Club, since 1924. — p. 924

**CHANG, Z. L** CHANG, Z. L., see CHANG HSIANG-LIN. — p. 924

**CHAO Cheng-p'ing, (趙正平)** CHAO CHENG-P'ING, (趙正平).—Courtesy name: Hou-sheng ([illegible][illegible]). Born 1888; native of Paoshan, Kiangsu. Graduate, Waseda University. Sometime president, Chi-Nan niversity, Shanghai; chief of staff to Governor of Nanking; editor of various papers. Director, social bureau, Municipal Government of Greater Peiping, since July 1928. — p. 924

**CHAO Ch'i, (趙琪)** CHAO CH'I, (趙琪).—Courtesy name: Jui-ch'uan (瑞泉). Native of Yehhsien, Shantung. Graduate, Tsingtao German-Chinese College. Studied in Germany. July 1925-April 1928, director of Kiaochow Port Administration. — p. 924

**CHAO Ch'ing-hua, (趙慶華)** CHAO CH'ING-HUA, (趙慶華).—Courtesy name: Sui-shan (燧山). Born 1879 in and native of Chinghua, Chekiang. Graduate, Hongkong Diocesan School and Canton Telegraph College. Sometime director, Canton telegraph administration; managing director, Canton-Kowloon and Tientsin-Pukow Railways; Shanghai manager, Bank of Communications; custodian of sequestrated Austrian and German shipping in Yangtze River. — p. 924

**CHAO Ch'uan, (趙泉)** CHAO CH'UAN, (趙泉).—Courtesy name: Chien-t'ang (鑑唐). Born 1895 in and native of Chihsien, Chihli. 1918, B. S. (Wesleyan). Sometime secretary delegation to Paris Conference, League of Nations. and Washington Conference; chief of conference department, directorate-general of Sino-Russian negotiations. 1926-8, secretary-general, permanent delegation to League of Nations. — p. 925

**CHAO Ch'un-nien, (趙椿年)** CHAO CH'UN-NIEN, (趙椿年).—Courtesy name: Chien-ch'iu (劍秋). Born 1867; native of Kiangsu. Chinshih of imperial examinations. 1912-3, vice-minister of finance. 1917-28, vice-president, Board of Audit. — p. 925

**CHAO Heng-t'i, (趙恒惕)** CHAO HENG-T'I, (趙恒惕).—Courtesy name: Yen-wu (炎午). Born 1880 in and native of Siangt'an, Hunan. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. 1916, commander-in-chief of Hunan forces. 1922, governor of "autonomous Hunan". 1925-26, civil governor of same province. — p. 925

**CHAO Hsi-en, (趙錫恩)** CHAO HSI-EN, (趙錫恩).—Courtesy name: Chin-ch'ing (晉卿). Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: S. U. Zau. Born 1883 in and native of Shanghai, Kiangsu. Studied at Nanyang College. Sometime adviser to civil governors of Chekiang and Shantung; director, Shanghai Chinese general chamber of commerce. 1928, departmental chief, Ministry of Industry, Labour and Commerce; councillor, Shanghai Municipal Council. Director, Shanghai Office, Ministry of Industry, Labour and Commerce, since October 1928. Managing director, Tung Yieh Land and Estate Co., and China Industrial Corporation. Director, Bank of Labour and Agriculture. A prominent leader of the Baptist Church. — p. 925

**CHAO Hsi-p'ing, (趙席聘)** CHAO HSI-P'ING, (趙席聘).—Courtesy name: Kuo-chen (國珍). Born 1878; native of Ch'ingwan, Hopei. 1925, commissioner of police, Kansu Province. 1927, defence commissioner, Hochou, Kansu; commander, 17th division. Member, Kansu Provincial Government, since November 1928. — p. 925

**CHAO Hsin-po, (趙欣伯)** CHAO HSIN-PO, (趙欣伯).—Born 1888. LL.D (Tokyo Imperial). 1922-8, legal adviser to late Chang Tso-lin. — p. 925

**CHAO I-k'uan, (趙以寬)** CHAO I-K'UAN, (趙以寬).—Courtesy name: Ching-hsi (警西). Born 1889; native of Tientsin. Graduate, Tokyo Officers Training Academy. Rose to chief artillery officer and chief of military affairs department. Commissioner of Police, Municipal Government of Greater Peiping, since July 1928. — p. 925

**CHAO Tai-wen, (趙戴文)** CHAO TAI-WEN, (趙戴文).—Courtesy name: Tz'u-lung (次隴). Born 1867; native of Wut'ai, Shansi. Senior licentiate of imperial examinations. Chief adviser to Governor Yen Hsi-shan, since 1912. 1916, defence commissioner for Northern Shansi. 1928, member Taiyuan branch of Kuomintang Central Political Council; chairman, Charhar Provincial Government; vice-minister of interior. Minister of Interior, and Vice-Chairman, Commission for Mongolian and Tibetan Affairs, since November 1928. March 1929, elected member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. Author of 周易[illegible]部 (Explanaton of Shao's Auguries of the Gods according to the Diagrams of the Science of Divination); 關於孟子學術之講演集 (Mencius' Doctrines), etc. — p. 925

**CHAO T'i, (趙倜)** CHAO T'I, (趙倜).—Courtesy name: Chou-jen ([illegible]人). Born 1870; native of Honan. Studied in Peiyang Military College. 1922, Tuchun (military governor) of Honan. 1926-8, adviser to late Chang Tso-lin. — p. 925

**CHAO Wen-jui, (趙文銳)** CHAO WEN-JUI, (趙文銳).—Born 1888; native of Ch'enghsien, Chekiang. Graduate, Tsing Hua College. 1911, B.A. (Harvard). 1917, M.A. (Columbia). Sometime lecturer in Wuchang Normal College. Peking College of Law and Chao Yang University. Commissioner for foreign affairs, Hangchow, since 1927. Translator of Bertrand Russell's "Problem of China" and Elderton's "Primer of Statistics." — p. 925

**CHAO Yen-hsu, (趙延緒)** CHAO YEN-HSU, (趙延緒).—Courtesy name: Shao-tsung (紹宗). Native of Fushun, Fengtien. Graduate, Paoting Military College and Nanyuan Aviation School. 1920, founded Mukden Aviation School. 1927-8, minister in charge of aviation matters, Ministry of Military Affairs. — p. 926

**CHAO Yi-t'ao, (趙詒璹)** CHAO YI-T'AO, (趙詒璹).—Courtesy name: Sung-nan (頌[illegible]). Born 1869; native of Quinsan, Kiangsu. Sometime 2nd secretary, Legation in Brussels, Rome, and The Hague. 1921-8, consul-general at Paris. — p. 926

**CHAO Yuan-jen, (趙元任)** CHAO YUAN-JEN, (趙元任).—Prefers English rendering: Y. R. Chao. Born 1892 in Tientsin; native of Changchow, Kiangsu. 1914, B.A. (Cornell); 1918, Ph.D. (Harvard). 1919-20, instructor in physics, Cornell University. 1920-1, interpreter for the Lecture Association (for Bertrand Russell). 1921-4, instructor in philosophy and Chinese, Harvard University. 1925-8, research professor, Tsing Hua University. Research fellow, Division of History and Language, National Research Institute, since January 1929. Trustee, China Founddation for Promoton of Education and Culture, since August 1928. Co-founder, The Science Society of China. Author of: Text-book of the National Language (國語留聲機片課本). 1922; A New Book of Rhymes (國音新詩韻). 1923; A Phonograph Course in the Chinese National Language, 1925. Translator of Lewis Carroll's "Alice's Adventures in Wonderland" 阿麗思漫遊奇境記) and A. A. Milne's "The Camberley Triangle" (最後五分鐘). Composer of "Songs of Contemporary Poets" (新詩歌集). Address: 40 Yang-i Hutung, East City, Peiping; or the Science Society of China, Nanking. — p. 926

**CHAO, Y. R** CHAO, Y. R., see above. — p. 926

**CHAR, George Y** CHAR, GEORGE Y., see HSIEH YUAN-FU. — p. 926

**CH'EN Ch'i-ts'ai, (陳其采)** CH'EN CH'I-TS'AI, (陳其采).—Courtesy name: Ai-shih (譪[illegible]). Born 1879; native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. Graduate, Nanking Language School, and Tokyo Officers Training Academy. 1903-26, held important posts in Kiangsu, Hunan, Peking, Chihli and Chekang. 1927-8, commissioner of finance, Chekiang Provincial Government. Superintendent of customs, Shanghai, since December 1928. — p. 926

**CH'EN Chi-yen, (陳繼淹)** CH'EN CHI-YEN, (陳繼淹).—Courtesy name: Hsi-wen (希文). Born 1898; native of Foup'ing, Hopei. Graduate, Peking Military College. 1925, chief councillor, Peking garrison commander's headquarters. 1926, chief of staff to military governor of Charhar. 1927, Party organizer in Urga. Member, Commission on Mongolian and Tibetan Affairs, since 1928. — p. 926

**CHEN, C. L** CHEN, C. L., see below. — p. 926

**CH'EN Chia-lin, (陳家麟)** CH'EN CHIA-LIN, (陳家麟).—Prefers English rendering: C. L. Chen. Born 188[illegible]; native of Chinhai, Hopei. Studied in Oxford, Cornell and Berlin universities. 1927, commissioner of foreign affairs, Tsinan. Author of "Comment on Shakespeare from Chinese Point of View." Translator of Charles Scott's "Quintinderward," Dickens' "Little Dorritt," Shakespeare's "Historical Plays," and Haggard's "She," etc. — p. 926

**CH'EN Chien-hsiu, (陳劍脩)** CH'EN CHIEN-HSIU, (陳劍脩).—Prefers English rendering: Pao-ao Ch'en. Born 1897 in and native of Suich'uan, Kiangsi. Graduate, Peking Government University. 1924, M.A. (London). 1926, taught in Peiping. 1927, director, bureau of education, Nanking. Director, department of social education, Ministry of Education, since December 1928. Author of "The Correllation between Character and Instinct" and "Children's Dream." — p. 926

**CH'EN Chin-t'ao, (陳錦濤)** CH'EN CHIN-T'AO, (陳錦濤).—Courtesy name: Lan-sheng (瀾生). Born 1870 in and native of Nanhai, Kwangtung. Graduate, Hongkong Queen's College. 1902, M.S. (Columbia). 1906, Ph.D. (Yale). Chin-shih of imperial examinations. Sometime vice-governor, Taching government bank; financial commissioner to Europe and U.S.A. 1916, minister of finance; concurrently minister of foreign affairs. 1917, victim of political plot and prosecuted for embezzlement. 1918, pardoned. 1926, again minister of finance. Recognized as one of China's best financial experts. Author of "Societary Circulation" (doctoral dissertation), as well as two volumes on distribution of wealth and public schools in four countries. — p. 926-927

**CH'EN CHIUN-MING, (陳烱明)** CH'EN CHIUN-MING, (陳烱明).—Courtesy name: Ching-ts'un (競存). Born 1875 in and native of Haifeng, Kwangtung. Chüjen of imperial examinations. Sometime Tutuh (military governor) of Canton. 1917, minister of war, Canton Military Government. 1921, civil governor of Canton, and commander-in-chief of Kwangtung forces. 1922, drove Dr. Sun Yat-sen out of Kwangtung. 1923, driven out of Kwangtung by Dr. Sun Yat-sen's forces. — p. 927

**CHEN DING-SAI** CHEN DING-SAI, see CH'EN T'ING-JUI. — p. 927

**CH'EN EN-HOU, (陳恩厚)** CH'EN EN-HOU, (陳恩厚).—Courtesy name; K'un-feng (昆峰). Born 1875 in and native of Wanp'ing, Chihli. Sometime councillor and director of protocol department, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. January 1928, appointed minister to Portugal, but did not proceed. — p. 927

**CH'EN, EUGENE** CH'EN, EUGENE, see CHEN YU-JEN. — p. 927

**CH'EN HENG-CHEH, (陳衡哲)** CH'EN HENG-CHEH, (陳衡哲).—Prefers English rendering: Sophia Ch'en. Wife of H. C. Zen (陳衡哲). Native of Changchow, Kiangsu. 1919, B.A. (Vassar College). 1920, M.A. (Chicago). Sometime professor of English literature, Peking National University; lecturer on Western history, Nanking Southeastern University. 1927, delegate to Institute of Pacific Relations, Honolulu. Author of two volumes on Western history. — p. 927

**CH'EN HSING, (陳行)** CH'EN HSING, (陳行).—Courtesy name: Chien-an (健菴). Prefers English rendering: Jian H. Chen. Born 1890 in and native of Shaoshing, Chekiang. 1917, B.Sc. (St. John's). 1918, M.A. (Ohio). 1921-2, chief of banking department, Chinese-American Bank of Commerce, Shanghai. 1922-6, manager, same Bank, Hankow. 1926-7, manager, Central Bank, Hankow, and director of Wuchang Mint; acting manager of Central Bank, Shanghai. Deputy Governor of same Bank since its reorganization in October 1928. — p. 927

**CH'EN HUAN-CHANG, (陳煥章)** CH'EN HUAN-CHANG, (陳煥章).—Courtesy name: Chung-yuen (重遠). Born 1881; native of Kwangtung. 1905, Chinshih of imperial examinations. 1911, Ph.D. (Columbia). Sometime editor, Chinese Reformer and Confucian Monthly; member of parliament. Founder and president, National Confucian Association, since 1911. Author of "Economic Principles of Confucius and His School" (doctoral dissertation) and several volumes on Confucianism. — p. 927

**CH'EN HUI-TEH, (陳[illegible]德)** CH'EN HUI-TEH, (陳[illegible]德).—Courtesy name: Kuang-fu (光甫). Prefers English rendering: K. P. Chen. Born 1880; native of Chinkiang, Kiangsu. 1909, B. C. (Pennsylvania). Sometime secretary, Nanking Exposition; organizer and manager, Kiangsu Provincial Bank; member, Chinese Advisory Committee of Shanghai Municipal Council; Founder and general manager, Shanghai Commercial and Savings Bank, since 1915. — p. 927

**CH'EN HUNG-HSIN, (陳鴻鑫)** CH'EN HUNG-HSIN, (陳鴻鑫).—Courtesy name: Ching-hou (敬候). Born 1883; native of Tientsin. 1914-17, instructor in Chinese, Tsing Hua College. 1919-27, second secretary, Legation in Rome; consul to Trieste and Rangoon. Councillor, Municipal Government of Greater Tientsin, since July 1928. — p. 927

**CHEN, JIAN H** CHEN, JIAN H., see CH'EN HSING. — p. 927

**CH'EN JU-HSUAN, (陳茹玄)** CH'EN JU-HSUAN, (陳茹玄).—Courtesy name: Yi-fan (逸凡). Prefers to sign himself: Chen Ye Young. Born 1894; native of Kwangtung. 1917, B.A. (Illinois). 1919, LL.B. (Columbia). Sometime editor, General Chamber of Commerce Monthly, and lecturer, Peking Law College; professor of law and economics, and concurrently vice-president, Southeastern University, Nanking. Author of "International Politics." — p. 928

**CHEN, K. P** CHEN, K. P., see CH'EN HUI-TEH. — p. 928

**CH'EN KUNG-PO, (陳公[illegible])** CH'EN KUNG-PO, (陳公[illegible]).—Born 1890; native of Kwangtung. B.A. (New York). One of the younger leaders of the Kuomintang left wing. 1927-8, editor, 革命評論 (The Revolutionary Critic) Weekly which, in three months, reached a circulaton of more than 60,000. Regarded as "a remarkable orator, a powerful writer, and an energetic party organizer." According to one foreign observer: "Students, workers, the younger officers in the army, those who were formerly Communists and are now leaderless, party workers, distressed Government officials who can no longer tolerate the confusion—all these classes turn (1928) to Mr. Chen Kung-po for leadership." — p. 928

**CH'EN KUO-FU, (陳果夫)** CH'EN KUO-FU, (陳果夫).—Native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. Nephew of General Chen Ch'i-mei, deceased. Studied in Nanking military academy, and Japan. 1926, elected member, Kuomintang Central Supervisory Committee. State Councillor of the National Government, and concurrently Vice-President, Control Yuan, since October 1928. March 1929, re-elected Member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. May 1929, re-elected Member, Kuomintang Central Political Council. — p. 928

**CH'EN LI-CHIANG, (陳[illegible]江)** CH'EN LI-CHIANG, (陳[illegible]江).—Courtesy name: Yi-min (逸民). Prefers English rendering: L. K. Chen. Born 1896 in and native of Kiukiang, Kiangsi. 1924, B.A. (De Pauw). 1925, M.A. (Chicago). Sometime dean and professor of education, Chungshan University, Wuchang. Commissioner of Education, Kiangsi Provincial Government, since September 1927. — p. 928

**CH'EN LI-FU, (陳立夫)** CH'EN LI-FU, (陳立夫).—Born 1899; native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. M.A. (Pittsburgh). 1927-8, secretary, commander-in-chief's headquarters; chief secretary, National Reconstruction Commission; director of political training, Directorate-General of Military Training. March 1929, elected member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. At the same time, Chief Secretary, Central Kuomintang Headquarters. — p. 928

**CHEN, L. K** CHEN, L. K., see CH'EN LI-CHIANG. — p. 928

**CH'EN LU (陳籙)** CH'EN LU (陳籙).—Courtesy name: Jen-hsien (任先). Prefers French rendering: Tcheng Loh. Born 1878 in and native of Foochow, Fukien. 1906, LL.B. (Paris). 1908, Hanlin Compiler of imperial examinations. Sometime delegate to Sino-Russian-Mongolian conference at Kiachta; resident-general at Urga; officiating minister of foreign affairs. 1920-8, minister to Paris. Author of "historical works on Mongolia". — p. 928

**CH'EN MING-CH'U, (陳銘樞)** CH'EN MING-CH'U, (陳銘樞). Courtesy name: Cheng-ju ([illegible]如). Born 1888 in and native of Hop'u, Kwangtung. Studied in Paoting Military Academy, and Japan. 1925, commander, 11th Nationalist army ("Iron Army). 1928, member, Canton branch of Central Political Council. Chairman, Kwangtung Provincial Government, since November, 1928. March 1929, re-elected member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. — p. 928

**CH'EN MOU-TING, (陳[illegible][illegible])** CH'EN MOU-TING, (陳[illegible][illegible]).—Native of Fukien. 1890, Chinshih of imperial examinations. Sometime senator; secretary to the Cabinet; and commissioner of foreign affairs at Nanking and Amoy. 1921-6, adviser, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. — p. 928

**CH'EN PAO-AO** CH'EN PAO-AO, see Ch'en Chin-hsiu. — p. 928

**CH'EN PAO-SHEN, (陳寶琛)** CH'EN PAO-SHEN, (陳寶琛). Born 1849; native of Fukien. 1868, Chinshih of imperial examinations. Sometime managing-director of Fukien Railway; governor of Shensi; adviser, Privy Council; member, Council of Regency during 1917 Manchu Restoration. Tutor of ex-Emperor P'u-yi since July 1911. Author of "Universal Rites of the Ch'ing Dynasty" approved by the board of ceremonies. — p. 929

**CH'EN PING-YIN, (陳賓寅)** CH'EN PING-YIN, (陳賓寅).—Courtesy name: Chin-ju (欽如). Born 1870 in and native of Kut-ien, Fukien. Graduate, Honan Law College. 1914-22, magistrate of Chungmou (Honan), Hoch'i (Kwangsi), Shanyin and Yangch'ü (Shansi). 1926, director, political affairs department, Suiyuan. Director, department of civil affairs, Suiyuan Provincial Government, since July 1928. — p. 929

**CH'EN SHAO-K'UAN, (陳紹寬)** CH'EN SHAO-K'UAN, (陳紹寬).—Prefers English rendering: S. K. Chen. Born in 1888; native of Foochow, Fukien. 1907, graduate, Naval College, Nanking. 1915, commander, cruisers Chao Ho and Hai Yung. 1916-9, senior naval attaché, Legation in London; naval delegate to Paris Peace Conference and International Hydrographic Conference, London. 1920, captain of training ship Tung Chi. 1923, captain of training cruiser Ying Swei. 1925, promoted Rear-Admiral. Commander, Second Squadron of the Chinese Navy, since 1926. Vice-Minister of Navy since April 1929. — p. 929

**CH'EN SHAO-YING, (陳肇英)** CH'EN SHAO-YING, (陳肇英).—Courtesy name: Hsiung-fu (雄夫). Native of Pu'chiang, Chekiang. 1918, commander, 1st division, Chekiang provincial army. 1919, joined Dr. Sun Yat-sen. 1922, commander, Bocca Tigris defences. Councillor, Commander-in-Chief's headquarters, since 1928. March 1929, elected member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. — p. 929

**CH'EN SHIH-CHANG, (陳世[illegible])** CH'EN SHIH-CHANG, (陳世[illegible]).—Prefers English rendering: Pinzen Cheng. Born 1886; native of Kiating, Kiangsu. B.Sc. (Birmingbam). Fellow, Institute of Chemistry (London) 1917-22, professor and dean of chemistry department, Peking National University. 1922-7, consulting engineer, Liuhokou Coal Mining Co. Commissioner of reconstruction, Kiangsu Provincial Government, since November 1928. — p. 929

**CH'EN SHIH-CHEN, (陳石珍)** CH'EN SHIH-CHEN, (陳石珍).—Born 1892; native of Kiangyin, Kiangsu. Graduate, Oberlin College (Ohio) and Columbia University. 1917-20, dean, middle school, Nanking Normal College. 1927-8, secretary, Chekiang University District. Chief secretary, Ministry of Education, since December 1928. — p. 929

**CH'EN SHIH-KUANG, (陳世光)** CH'EN SHIH-KUANG, (陳世光).—Courtesy name: Chen-tung (霆東). Better known as Chevalier S. K. Ch'en. Born 1879; native of Kwangtung. Sometime commissioner for foreign affairs and superintendent of customs, Shanghai. Director of Shanghai office, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, since 1928. — p. 929

**CH'EN SHU-JEN, (陳樹人)** CH'EN SHU-JEN, (陳樹人).—Native of Kwangtung. Born 1884. Graduate, Tokyo Imperial University. Sometime director, general affairs department, ministry of interior, Canton. 1927, acting head, ministry of labour, Canton. 1928-9, member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee and Central Political Council. A painter of some repute. — p. 929

**CH'EN, SOPHIA** CH'EN, SOPHIA, see CH'EN HENG-CHEH. — p. 929

**CH'EN SSU-PANG, (陳祀邦)** CH'EN SSU-PANG, (陳祀邦).—Courtesy name: Hsin-chou (新周). Prefers English rendering: S. P. Chen. Born 1884 in Singapore; native of Tsining, Kwangtung. 1904, B.A.; 1907, M.B., B.CH (Cantab. and St. Thomas's Hospital, London). Sometime commissioner for suppression of pneumonic plague in Shansi; chief medical officer, North Manchuria Plague Prevention Service, Harbin, and Peking Isolation and Central Hospitals; medical officer, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. 1928, president, Peking Rotary Club. — p. 929

**CH'EN TA, 陳達)** CH'EN TA, 陳達).—Courtesy name: T'ung-fu (通夫).—Prefers English rendering: Ta T. Chen. Born 1892, native of Yuhang, Chekiang. Graduate, Tsing Hua College. 1919, B.A. (Reed College). 1920, M.A.; 1923, Ph.D. (Columbia). Professor of Sociology, Tsing Hua College, since 1923. 1926, delegate to Institute of Pacific Relations, Honolulu. Author of "Chinese migrations, with special reference to labour conditions" (Bulletin No. 340, U.S. Bureau of Labour Statistics). — p. 930

**CH'EN TAO-YUAN, (陳道源)** CH'EN TAO-YUAN, (陳道源).—Courtesy name: Kuan-yi (貫一). Born 1888; native of Chihhsien, Chekiang. Graduate, Peiyang University. 1909, M. E. (Cornell). Associate member, Institute of Naval Architects, London. Sometime secretary, Salt Chief Inspectorate and delegation to Washington Conference; technical expert, Ministry of Navy. Chinese Deputy secretary, British Municipal Council, Tientsin, since 1928. — p. 930

**CH'EN T'ING-JUI, (陳震銳)** CH'EN T'ING-JUI, (陳震銳).—Prefers to sign himself: Chen Ding-sai. Born 1889: native of Soochow, Kiangsu. 1920, LL.B. (Soochow). 1921, J. D.; 1922, M.A. (Michigan). Legal practitioner in Shanghai since 1923. April 1929, re-elected committee member, Shanghai Municipal Council. Author of "The principles of state secession as revealed by the Versailles Treaty" (doctoral dissertation). — p. 930

**CH'EN TU-HSIU, (陳獨秀)** CH'EN TU-HSIU, (陳獨秀). Courtesy name: Chung-fu (仲甫). Native of Huaining, Anhwei. Born 1879. Studied in Japan and France. Sometime dean, department of literature. Peking National University; editor La Jeunesse. 1924. joined Kuomintang, but turned Communist shortly after. 1925, member of Kuomintang Central Executive Committee and delegate to Moscow Third International. — p. 930

**CH'EN WEN-HSUEH, (陳文學)** CH'EN WEN-HSUEH, (陳文學).—Courtesy name: Tzu-yang (紫[illegible]). Born 1884 in and native of Nanking. Sometime director of Peiping Octroi; chief secretary, Ankuochun 3rd and 4th army corps; director, political affairs department, Mukden. Director, civil affairs department, Liaoning (Fengtien) Provincial Government, since January 1929. — p. 930

**CHEN YE-YOUNG** CHEN YE-YOUNG, see CH'EN JU-HSUAN. — p. 930

**CH'EN YI, (陳儀)** CH'EN YI, (陳儀).—Courtesy name: Kung-hsia (公洽). Born 1883 in and native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. 1924-[illegible], commander, Chekiang 1st division. November 1926, civil governor of Chekiang. 1927, member, Nationalist Military Council. 1928, director, Shanghai arsenal. Officiating Minister of Military Affairs, since April 1929. — p. 930

**CH'EN YU-JEN, (陳友仁)** CH'EN YU-JEN, (陳友仁).—Better known as Eugene Ch'en. Native of Kwangtung. Born in Trinidad, 1878. Qualified as solicitor in London and practised in West Indies. 1912, returned to China 1914, editor and proprietor, Peking Gazette. 1916, imprisoned for alleged anti-Japanese preachings. 1917, pardoned. 1918-9, editor, Shanghai Gazette; attended Paris Peace Conference with Canton's representative. 1922-4, adviser to Canton Government. 1925, editor. Min Pao, Peking; kidnapped by Mukden soldiers and narrowly escaped being shot. 1926, acting minister of foreign affairs, Canton Government. 1927, minister for foreign affairs, Hankow Nationalist Government. Resident in Europe since September 1927. 1928-9, elected member of Kuomintang Central Executive Committee and Central Political Council. — p. 930

**CH'EN YU-KUANG, (陳裕光)** CH'EN YU-KUANG, (陳裕光).—Born 1893; native of Chekiang. Graduate, Nanking University. 1919, M.A.; 1922, Ph.D. (Columbia). 1922-5, dean, department of physics and chemistry, Peking Normal University. 1925, dean, college of arts and science, Nanking University. Elected president, same University, since November 1927. — p. 930

**CH'ENG Ch'ien, (程潛)** CH'ENG CH'IEN, (程潛).—Courtesy name: Sung-yün (頌雲). Born 1882; native of Fengling, Hunan. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. 1916, commander, Hunan "National Protection Army". 1923, secretary for military affairs, Generalissimo's headquarters, Canton. 1926, commander, Nationalist 6th Army; Garrison commander of Kiukiang. 1927, helped to defeat generals T'ang Sheng-chih and Sun Ch'uan-fang. 1928, member, Hunan Provincial Government, and Wuhan branch of Kuomintang Central Political Council. — p. 931

**CHENG, F. T** CHENG, F. T., see CHENG TIEN-HSI. — p. 931

**CH'ENG Hsi-keng, (程錫庚)** CH'ENG HSI-KENG, (程錫庚).—Courtesy name: Lien-shih (蓮士). Prefers English rendering: S. G. Cheng. Born 1894; native of Chinkiang, Kiangsu. 1917, B.Sc.; 1921, D.Sc. (London). Sometime secretary, delegation to League of Nations and Ministry of Finance; technical expert, commission for the study of national finance and Customs Conference. Author of "Modern China: A Political Study" (Oxford Press). — p. 931

**CHENG Hung-nien, (鄭洪年)** CHENG HUNG-NIEN, (鄭洪年).—Courtesy name: Shao-chiao (韶覺). Born 1874; native of P'anyü, Kwangtung. 1912, chief secretary, Ministry of Communications, Nanking. 1913, and 1920-2, vice-minister of communications. 1924-8, vice-minister of finance, Nationalist Government; commissioner of finance, Canton Provincial Government; vice-minister of reconstruction. Political Vice-Minister of Industry, Labour and Commerce, since November 1928. President of Ch'i Nan University, Chenju (near Shanghai), since 1908. — p. 931

**CH'ENG K'e, (程克)** CH'ENG K'E, (程克).—Courtesy name: Chung-yü (仲漁). Born 1878 in and native of Kaifeng, Honan. Graduate of Tokyo Imperial University. Sometime senator; high commissioner for Altai. 1923-4, minister of justice and chairman, law codification commission. — p. 931

**CHENG, Pinzen** CHENG, PINZEN, see CH'EN SHIH-CHANG. — p. 931

**CHENG Shih-ch'i, (鄭士琦)** CHENG SHIH-CH'I, (鄭士琦).—Born 1874; native of Anhwei. Sometime pacification commissioner of Kiangsi and Tupan (military governor) of Shantung. — p. 931

**CHENG, S. G** CHENG, S. G., see CH'ENG HSI-KENG. — p. 931

**CH'ENG Shu-teh, (程樹德)** CH'ENG SHU-TEH, (程樹德).—Courtesy name: Yu-t'ing (郁廷). Native of Foochow, Fukien. Graduate, Tokyo Law College. 1909, Hanlin Compiler of imperial examinations. Sometime senator. 1927, assistant director, Law Compilation Bureau. Author of twenty volumes on ancient Chinese Laws. — p. 931

**CH'ENG T'ien-fang, (程天放)** CH'ENG T'IEN-FANG, (程天放).—Born 1899 in Hangchow; native of Hsinchien, Kiangsi. 1920, B.A. (Fuhtan). 1923, M.A. (Illinois). 1925, Ph.D. (Toronto). 1919. chairman, Shanghai union of students associations. 1923, chief editor, Hsing Hua Jih Pao (Canada). 1926, professor of literature, Chih Ta University. 1927, commissioner of education, Kiangsi Province; professor of education, National Central University.
