Nanking 1928. councillor, Examination Yuan. Commissioner of education, Anhwei Provincial Government, since January 1929. Two months later, elected reserve member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. Author of "Oriental Immigration into Canada" (doctoral dissertation). — p. 931

**CHENG Tien-hsi, (鄭天錫)** CHENG TIEN-HSI, (鄭天錫). Courtesy name: Fu-t'ing ([illegible]廷). Prefers English rendering: F. T. Cheng. Born 1884 in and native of Hsiangshan, Kwangtung. 1912, LL.B. (London). 1913, barrister-at-law (Middle Temple). 1916, LL.D. (London). Sometime technical expert, delegation to Washington Conference and Customs Conference; deputy delegate to International Commission on Extraterritorial Jurisdiction; legal adviser, Trade Mark Bureau. Legal practitioner in Shanghai since 1928. Author of "Private International Law relating to Capacity to Contract" (doctoral dissertation), "Supreme Court Decisions," "Prize Court Judgments," etc. — p. 931

**CHENG Yen-hsi, (鄭延禧)** CHENG YEN-HSI, (鄭延禧). Courtesy name: Tzu-chün (子俊). Prefers to sign himself: Tcheng Yien-chi. Born 1879; native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. Studied at Foochow Anglo-Chinese College. Sometime commissioner of foreign affairs, Harbin. 1926-28, Chargé d'Affaires at Moscow. — p. 932

**CHENG Yu-hsiu, Miss (鄭毓秀女士)** CHENG YU-HSIU, MISS (鄭毓秀女士).—Prefers French rendering: Soumé Tcheng. Born at Canton, 1894. At age of 14, broke off engagement with the son of governor of Canton. 1911, carried bombs to Peking for assassination of Manchu leaders. 1921, persuaded Chinese Government to provide funds for educating 20 Chinese girls in France. 1926, docteur en droit, Paris. 1927, chief judge, Shanghai District Court. Member, National Reconstruction Commission, and Member, Legislative Yuan, since October 1928. Author of "The Model Chinese Family" in English, "Le Mouvement Constitutionelle en Chine" (doctoral dissertation) and "Souvenirs d'Enfance et de Revolution" in French. — p. 932

**CH'I Hsieh-yuan, (齊燮元)** CH'I HSIEH-YUAN, (齊燮元).—Courtesy name: Fu-wan (撫萬). Born 1879 in and native of Ningho, Chihli. Graduate, Peking Military College. Sometime commander, 6th division, and occupation commissioner, Nanking; Tuchun (military governor) of Kiangsu; high inspecting commissioner of Kiangsu, Kiangsi and Anhwei. — p. 932

**CHIA Shih-yi, (賈士毅)** CHIA SHIH-YI, (賈士毅).—Courtesy name: Kuo-po (果伯). Born 1888 in and native of Ihsing, Kiangsu. 1907, graduate, Meiji University, Tokyo. 1913, director, accounting department, Ministry of Finance. 1914, councillor, same Ministry. 1920, customs superintendent and commissioner of foreign affairs, Chinkiang. 1927, member, Kiangsu Provincial Government. Director, taxation department, Ministry of Finance, since 1928. Author of treatises on the financial history of the Chinese Republic (two volumes), tariff autonomy and national sovereignty, and the Washington Conference. — p. 932

**CHIANG Ch'ao-tsung, (江朝宗)** CHIANG CH'AO-TSUNG, (江朝宗).—Courtesy name: Yü-ch'en (宇澄). Born 1859 in and native of Chingte, Anhwei. Sometime commander of Hanchung garrison. 1912, commander, metropolitan gendarmerie; deputy lieutenant-general, Bordered Red Chinese Banner; acting premier. One of Peking's leading citizens and member of public safety committee in times of emergency. — p. 932

**CHIANG Chung-cheng, (蔣中正)** CHIANG CHUNG-CHENG, (蔣中正).—Courtesy name: Chieh-shih (介石). Better known as Chiang Kai-shek. Born 1886; native of Fenghua, Chekiang. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. Member, Tung Meng Hui (Revolutionary Party). 1911, secretary to Dr. Sun Yat-sen; participated in storming of Shanghai and served under General Ch'en Ch'i-mei. 1923, chief of staff to Generalissimo Sun Yat-sen and his representative to Soviet Russia to study its military system. 1924, principal of Whampoa Military Academy. 1926, commander-in-chief of Northern Expedition. April 1927, suppressed the Communists in Shanghai, etc., and formed a Nationalist Government at Nanking in opposition to that at Hankow. August, 1927, resigned all posts. January 1928, resumed command. State Councillor and concurrently President of the National Government, since October 1928. Member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee, since 1926. Chairman, Kuomintang Central Political Council, since 1928. Chairman, Military Disbandment and Reorganization Conference (January 1929). — p. 932

**CHIANG Kai-shek** CHIANG KAI-SHEK, see CHIANG CHUNG-CHENG. — p. 932

**CHIANG Meng-lin, (蔣夢[illegible])** CHIANG MENG-LIN, (蔣夢[illegible]).—Courtesy name: Chao-hsien (兆賢).—Prefers English rendering: Monlin Chiang. Born 1884 in and native of Yüyao, Chekiang. 1912, B.A. (California). 1913, M.A.; 1917, Ph.D. (Columbia). 1919-26, chairman, administration council, and acting chancellor, Peking National University. 1921, people's co-delegate to Washington Conference. 1927, member, Chekiang Provincial Government, and concurrently commissioner of education. Minister of Education since October 1928; concurrently chancellor, Chekiang University District. 1928, member, Kuomintang Central Political Council. Author of "A Study in Chinese Principle of Education" (doctoral dissertation). — p. 932

**CHIANG, Monlin** CHIANG, MONLIN, see above. — p. 933

**CHIANG T'ien-to, (江天鐸)** CHIANG T'IEN-TO, (江天鐸).—Courtesy name: Ching-an (競菴). Born 1879 in and native of Hua-hsien, Kwangtung. Graduate, Waseda University, Tokyo. 1913, member of parliament. 1917, vice-minister of agriculture and commerce. 1920 and 1922, minister of agriculture and commerce. — p. 933

**CHIANG Tso-pin, (蔣作賓)** CHIANG TSO-PIN, (蔣作賓).—Courtesy name: Yü-yen (雨巖). Prefers to sign himself: Tsiang Tso Ping. Born 1884; native of Yingcheng, Hupeh. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. Chüjen of imperial examinations. 1912, vice-minister of war, Nanking. 1916, vice-minister, Board of General Staff. 1927, chairman, political affairs commission, Anhwei Province. 1928, chairman, commission for political rehabilitation of war areas; member, Peiping branch, Kuomintang Central Political Council. Minister to Germany and Austria since October 1928. Author of "Travels Around the World," in Chinese, and translator of Japanese infantry drill manual. — p. 933

**CHIANG Tsun-kuei, (蔣尊簋)** CHIANG TSUN-KUEI, (蔣尊簋).—Courtesy name: Po-ch'i (伯器).—Native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. Born 1887. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. 1911, provisional Tutuh (military governor) of Canton. 1914, senator. 1920, vice-chief of staff, Canton Government. 1923, vice-minister, Ministry of Military Affairs, Canton Government. — p. 933

**CHIANG Tsun-wei, (蔣尊禕)** CHIANG TSUN-WEI, (蔣尊禕).—Courtesy name: Pin-hou (彬侯). Prefers English rendering: T. Y. Tsiang. Born 1877 in and native of Haining, Chekiang. Chinshih of imperial examinations. Rose to departmental chief and councillor, Ministry of Communications. 1924-1928, director-general of telegraphs. — p. 933

**CHIANG Wei-ch'iao, (蔣維喬)** CHIANG WEI-CH'IAO, (蔣維喬). Courtesy name: Chu-chuang (竹莊). Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: V. J. Tsiang. Born 1872; native of Changchow, Kiangsu. Sometime commissioner of education for Kiangsu province. Director, Shanghai Aikuo Girls' School, and editor, Shanghai Commercial Press. Author of several textbooks and books on Buddhism. — p. 933

**CHIANG Yung, (江庸)** CHIANG YUNG, (江庸).—Courtesy name: I-yün (翊雲). Born 1878 in and native of Changting, Fukien. Graduate, Waseda University, Tokyo. Chinshih of imperial examinations. 1913 and 1920-3, vice-president, and president, Law Codification Commission. 1916-8, vice-minister and minister of justice; director of Chinese students in Japan. 1924-6, president, Peking Law College. Founder and editor-in-chief, Chinese Law Review. — p. 933

**CH'IEN Ta-chun, (錢大鈞)** CH'IEN TA-CHUN, (錢大鈞).—Courtesy name: Mu-yin (慕尹). Born 1892; native of Soochow, Kiangsu. 1911 Revolution, participated in capture of Nanking. 1913, fled to Japan after failure of 2nd Revolution. 1919, graduate, Tokyo Officers Training Academy. 1923, chief of staff, 1st division, Canton army. 1924, training officer, Whampoa Military Academy. 1925, commander, 1st division, Nationalist army. 1928, defence commissioner of Shanghai and Woosung; member, Kiangsu Provincial Government. Commander, 2nd Nationalist army, since 1926. — p. 933

**CH'IEN T'ai, (錢泰)** CH'IEN T'AI, (錢泰).—Courtesy name: Chieh-p'ing (楷平). Prefers to sign himself: Tsien Tai. Born 1887 in and native of Chiashan, Chekiang. 1914, docteur en droit (Paris). 1915, councillor, Ministry of Justice. 1921-8, director, treaty department, Ministry of Foreign Affairs and drafting department, Customs Conference; acting vice-minister of foreign affairs. Councillor, Judicial Yuan, and adviser, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, since January 1929. — p. 933

**CH'IEN Wen-hsuan, (錢文選)** CH'IEN WEN-HSUAN, (錢文選).—Courtesy name: Chih-ch'ing (士青). Prefers English rendering W. H. Chien. Born 1874 in and native of Kuangteh, Anhwei. Graduate, Peking National University. Sometime director of students in England; and salt commissioner for Yunnan, Changlu, Chekiang, and Anhwei. Reappointed salt commissioner for Chekiang since 1926. — p. 934

**CH'IEN Yung-ming, (錢永銘)** CH'IEN YUNG-MING, (錢永銘).—Courtesy name: Hsin-chih (新之). Born 1885 in and native of Shanghai, Kiangsu. Graduate, Kobe Commercial College. 1922-7, vice-president, Bank of Communications; vice-minister of finance, Nanking. Commissioner of Finance, Chekiang Provincial Government, since 1928. — p. 934

**CHIN Chang, (金章)** CHIN CHANG, (金章).—Courtesy name: T'ao-t'ao (陶陶). Wife of Wang Chi-tseng (K. T. Ouang). Native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. Studied in London and Paris, and Chinese art under her brother (late Kungpah King). Foremost lady artist, in fish, flowers, birds and landscape figures. Chairman, executive committee, Chinese Home for Old Women. A Buddhist scholar and devotee to "Pure Land" sect. — p. 934

**CHIN Chih, (靳志)** CHIN CHIH, (靳志).—Courtesy name: Chung-yün (仲雲). Prefers English rendering: Thomas King. Born 1877 in and native of Kaifeng, Honan. 1897, Chinshih of imperial examinations. 1905-11, studied in Paris and London. 1919, Chargé d'Affaires in Brussels. 1921-3, secretary, delegation to Washington Conference, and Weihaiwei Retrocession Commission. Chief of Protocol Section, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, since September 1928. Author of six volumes of modern poems. — p. 934

**CH'IN Fen, (秦汾)** CH'IN FEN, (秦汾).—Courtesy name: Chin-yang (景陽). Born 1887 in and native of Chiating, Kiangsu. 1908, B.A.; 1909, M.A. (Harvard). Sometime professor of mathematics, Nanyang College and Peking National University; councillor, Ministry of Education. 1926, acting vice-minister of education. 1928, director, department of higher education, National Central University, Nanking. Author of a series of textbooks on mathematics and astronomy. — p. 934

**CH'IN Jun-ch'ing, (秦潤[illegible])** CH'IN JUN-CH'ING, (秦潤[illegible]).—Courtesy name: Tsu-tseh (祖澤). Better known as Zing Zung-ching (Shanghai rendering). Born 1878; native of Tz'u-chi, Chekiang. Manager, Foh Yu Native Bank, Shanghai, for the last quarter of a century. 1919, president, Shanghai Native Bankers Guild. Committee member, Shanghai Municipal Council, since 1928. — p. 934

**CHIN Pang-p'ing, (金邦平)** CHIN PANG-P'ING, (金邦平).—Native of Anhwei. 1915, minister of agriculture and commerce. 1916, director-general of national conservancy. Shanghai manager of Chee Hsin Cement Company since 1917. — p. 934

**CHIN Pao-shan, (金寶善)** CHIN PAO-SHAN, (金寶善).—Courtesy name: Ch'u-chen (楚珍). Prefers English rendering: P. Z. King. Born 1893 in and native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. 1918, M.D. (Chiba, Japan). 1920-8, medical officer, Isolation Hospital, Peiping; professor, National Medical College, Peiping, and Army Medical College, Tientsin; technical expert and director, National Epidemic Prevention Bureau; and chief Division of Medical Service, Public Health Demonstration Station, Peiping; health commissioner, Hangchow. Director, Department of Medical Service, Ministry of Health, since October 1928. Author of "Report on Public Health Administration in the United States," and chief editor (1927-8) of Chinese section, National Medical Journal of China. — p. 934

**CHIN Shao-chi, (金紹基)** CHIN SHAO-CHI, (金紹基).—Courtesy name: Shu-ch'u ([illegible]初).—Prefers English rendering: Sohtsu G. King. Born 1886 in and native of Nanhsiang, Kiangsu. 1902-5, studied in King's College, London. Sometime head of chemistry department, Nanyang College; managing director, Real Estate, and Trading Corporation, Hankow; Peking agent of Vicker's Limited; curator. Peking Historical Museum. — p. 934

**CHIN Ya-mei, (金雅妹)** CHIN YA-MEI, (金雅妹). Courtesy name: Yün-mei (韻梅). Prefers English rendering: Yamei Kin. Born 1864 in and native of Ningpo, Chekiang. At age of two, was adopted by Dr. & Mrs. McCartee and taken to U.S.A. 1885, M.D. (New York Infirmary)—being first Chinese woman to take a medical degree. 1894, married to H. L. Amador Eca da Silva. 1904, marriage annulled by Superior Court of California, with privilege of resuming maiden name and sole custody of only child (killed in France during the Great War). 1902-6, superintendent, Peiyang Women's Hospital (Tientsin), and sanitary supervisor, Changlu Foundling Asylum. 1915, delegate, Pan-Pacific Exposition. 1917, detailed by U. S. Department of Agriculture to make survey of culinary uses of soya bean in North China. Has been residing in Peking ever since (61 Tung Piao Pei Hutung, East City). — p. 935

**CHIN Yueh-lin, (金岳霖)** CHIN YUEH-LIN, (金岳霖).—Prefers English rendering: Y. L. Chin. Born 1895; native of Changsha, Hunan. Ph.D. (Berlin). Professor of philosophy, Tsing Hua University, since 1927. — p. 935

**CHIN Yun-ao, (靳雲鶚)** CHIN YUN-AO, (靳雲鶚).—Courtesy name: Chien-ch'ing (薦青). Born 1878 in and native of Chining, Shantung. Graduate, Peiyang military academy. 1923, commander, 14th division. 1926, vice-commander, Allied Northern Armies under Wu Pei-fu. — p. 935

**CHIN Yun-p'eng, (靳雲鵬)** CHIN YUN-P'ENG, (靳雲鵬).—Courtesy name: Yi-ch'ing (翼卿). Born 1877 in and native of Chining, Shantung. Graduate, Peiyang military academy. 1913, acting Tutuh (military governor) of Shantung. 1919, minister of war. 1920, premier and minister of war. — p. 935

**CHOU, C. C** CHOU, C. C., see CHOU TS'UNG-CHENG. — p. 935

**CHOU Ch'i-kang, (周啟剛)** CHOU CH'I-KANG, (周啟剛).—Courtesy name: Chiao-yung (覺庸). Born 1889; native of Nanhai, Kwangtung. Merchant in Cuba for a number of years. March 1929, re-elected member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. 1928, member, Kuomintang Central Political Council. Concurrently member, Chinese Overseas Commission, since October 1928. — p. 935

**CHOU Chung-liang, (周仲良)** CHOU CHUNG-LIANG, (周仲良).—Born 1886 in and native of Lip'ing, Kweichow. Postgraduate studies in Waseda University. Sometime confidential secretary, Generalissimo's headquarters, Canton, Director, Bureau of Printing and Engraving, National Government Headquarters, since 1928. — p. 935

**CHOU Hsin-t'ang, (周星棠)** CHOU HSIN-T'ANG, (周星棠).—Born 1877; native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. Sometime high adviser to military governor of Hupeh. 1923-8, chairman, Hankow Chinese general chamber of commerce. Owner of several banks and member, Board of Directors of Hankow Special Administrative Area No. 2. Most influential business leader in Wuhan. — p. 935

**CHOU Hsueh-hsi, (周學熙)** CHOU HSUEH-HSI, (周學熙).—Native of Anhwei. 1908, president, Anhwei Railway Co. 1912, minister of finance. 1911-2, arranged amalgamation of Kaiping and Lanchow mines. Actively interested in cotton industry since 1919. Promoter and director, Peking Waterworks Co. — p. 935

**CHOU Lung-kuang, (周龍光)** CHOU LUNG-KUANG, (周龍光).—Courtesy name: Erh-wei (二爲). Born 1885; native of Tingyuan, Anhwei. LL.B. (Tokyo Imperial). 1925-8, councillor, Ministry of Justice. Director, Asiatic Department, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, since July 1928. — p. 935

**CHOU Shou-kuang, (朱綬光)** CHOU SHOU-KUANG, (朱綬光).—Courtesy name: Lan-sun ([illegible]孫). Born 1886 in Foochow; native of Siang-yang, Hupeh. 1919, graduate, Tokyo Military College. 1920, councillor, Ministry of War. 1924, director of ordnance department, same Ministry. Adviser and chief of staff to Governor Yen Hsi-shan, since 1925. Member, Hopei Provincial Government, and chief of staff, Peiping and Tientsin Garrison Commander-in-chief's headquarters, since July 1928. — p. 935

**CHOU Shu-jen, (周樹人)** CHOU SHU-JEN, (周樹人).—Pen name: Lu-hsin (魯迅). Born 1881 in and native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. 1902, studied in Sendai School of Medicine, Tokyo. 1917, taught biology and chemistry in Hangchow; principal, Shaohsing Middle School. 1926, dean, Chinese Department, Amoy University. China's most eminent fiction writer. 1918, blazed the short story movement with "A Madman's Diary" (狂人日記)—translation from the Japanese). Fifteen of his stories have been collected and published under the name of "Nei Han," of which "The Biography of Ah Q" is available in French, Russian, German and English translations. Author of 中國小說史畧 (History of Chinese Novels). Editor of 語絲 Weekly. — p. 936

**CHOU Shu-mo, (周樹模)** CHOU SHU-MO, (周樹模).—Courtesy name: Shao-p'u (少樸). Born 1868 in and native of T'ienmen, Hupeh. 1889, Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. Sometime imperial censor and commisssioner of public instruction. 1915, president, administrative court. — p. 936

**CHOU Tso-jen, (周作人)** CHOU TSO-JEN, (周作人).—Born 1887; native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. Professor of Chinese, Peiping National University, for the last ten years. Frequent contributor to La Jeunesse (新青年). Acknowledged by many as a real master of prose essay. Together with brother (Chou Shu-jen), has collected and translated a number of stories from Japan, Russia, Poland and Southern Europe. — p. 936

**CHOU Tso-min, (周作民)** CHOU TSO-MIN, (周作民).—Prefers to sign himself Chow Tso-ming. Born 1882 in and native of Huaian, Kiangsu. Studied in Nanyang College and Japan. Managing director of Kincheng Banking Corporation since 1918. 1923, organized the powerful combine of Kincheng-Salt Industry-Continental-China and South Seas Banks. — p. 936

**CHOU Ts'ung-cheng, (周從政)** CHOU TS'UNG-CHENG, (周從政).—Courtesy name: Ta-fu (達夫). Prefers English rendering: C. C. Chou. Born 1894 in and native of Kaip'ing, Fengtien. Graduate, Peking National University. Sometime member of Fengtien provincial assembly; magistrate of Kuantien (Fengtien). 1927-8, councillor, Ministry of Education. Translator of "The Philosophy of Education." — p. 936

**CHOU Ya-wei, (周亞衛)** CHOU YA-WEI, (周亞衛).—Courtesy name: P'u-wen (普文). Prefers French rendering: Tcheou Yaoué. Born 1889; native of Ch'enghsien, Chekiang. 1916, graduate, Ecole superieur de guerre, Paris. Sometime chief of staff and director of training, Nationalist armies. Assistant Director-General of Military Training, since 1928. — p. 936

**CHOU Yi-ch'un, (周詒春)** CHOU YI-CH'UN, (周詒春).—Courtesy name: Chi-mei (寄梅). Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: Y. T. Tsur. Born 1883 at Hankow; native of Anhwei. Graduate, St. John's University. 1909, B.A. (Yale). 1910, M.A. (Wisconsin). 1911, Chinshih of imperial examinations. Hon. D. Litt. (St. John's and Yenching). Sometime president, Tsing Hua College, Peking; senator; manager, Chung Foo Union Bank, Peking; secretary general, commission for the readjustment of finance; technical expert, Customs Conference. 1924-1928, trustee and director, China Foundation for Promotion of Education and Culture. — p. 936

**CHOU Yin-jen, (周陰人)** CHOU YIN-JEN, (周陰人).—Courtesy name: Yueh-en (樾恩). Born 1885; native of Chihli. Graduate of military schools in Japan. 1923, defence commissioner, Hsinghua and Ch'uanchow, Fukien. 1924, Tupan (military governor) of Fukien; commander, 12th division. — p. 936

**CHOW Tso-meng** CHOW TSO-MENG, see CHOU TSO-MIN. — p. 936

**CHU Chao-hsin, (朱兆莘)** CHU CHAO-HSIN, (朱兆莘).—Courtesy name: Ting-ch'ing (鼎青). Born 1879 in anad native of Huahsien, Kwangtung. Graduate, Peking National University. 1911, B.Sc. (New York). 1912, M.A. (Columbia). 1913, senator. 1914, magistrate, Kulangsu Mixed Court, Amoy. 1917, commissioner for foreign affairs, Shanghai. 1918, consul-general at San Francisco. 1922-7, chargé d'affaires in London; minister to Italy; delegate to League of Nations Assembly and Council. August 1927—May 1929 vice-minister for foreign affairs, Nanking; concurrently commissioner for foreign affairs, Canton. — p. 937

**CHU Ch'i, (朱淇)** CHU CH'I, (朱淇).—Courtesy name: Chi-chen (季箴). Born 1858 in and native of Nanhai, Kwangtung. Chüjen of imperial examinations. 1904, founded Peking Jih Pao and editor ever since. 1907, founded Peking Daily News, which he sold to Anfu Party ten years later. Founder and first president, Peking Press Association. Doyen of Chinese newspapermen in Peking. — p. 937

**CHU Ch'i-ch'ien, (朱啟鈐)** CHU CH'I-CH'IEN, (朱啟鈐).—Courtesy name: Kuei-hsin (桂莘). Born 1872; native of Kweichow. Chüjen of imperial examinations. 1912, minister of communications. 1913, appointed acting premier but did not accept. 1915, minister of interior; played prominent part in Yuan Shih-kai's scheme to make himself emperior. 1919, chief delegate to Shanghai peace conference. 1921, special envoy to France to receive honorary doctor's degree conferred upon President Hsu Shih-ch'ang by Paris University. President of Chung Hsing Mining Co., Tientsin. — p. 937

**CHU Chia-hua, (朱家驊)** CHU CHIA-HUA, (朱家驊).—Courtesy name: Liu-hsien (騮先). Born 1893; native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. Ph.D. (Berlin). 1917-26, professor of physics, Peking Government University. 1926, acting chairman, Kwangtung Provincial Government. Director, Geological Survey of Kwangtung and Kwangsi since July 1927. Director, department of civil affairs, Chekiang Provincial Government, since December 1927; concurrently Vice-Chancellor, Kwangtung National University, since May 1927, March 1929, elected member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. — p. 937

**CHU Ching-nung, (朱經農)** CHU CHING-NUNG, (朱經農).—Prefers to sign himself: King Chu. Born 1887; native of Puchiang, Chekiang. Graduate, China Institute, Woosung. 1918, B.A.; 1920, M.A. (George Washington). Postgraduate work in Columbia University. 1916-20, secretary, Chinese Educational Mission, Washington, D.C. 1921-2, professor of education, Peiping National University. 1924-5, head, department of Chinese, Shanghai Baptist College. 1925-7, dean and professor of education, Kwang Hua University, Shanghai. 1928, vice-president, same University, and commissioner of education, Municipal Government of Greater Shanghai. Director of primary and secondary education, Ministry of Education, since October 1928. Editor and author of a series of textbooks on education. — p. 937

**CHU Chun-yi, (朱君毅)** CHU CHUN-YI, (朱君毅).—Prefers English rendering: Jennings P. Chu Born 1895; native of Chekiang. Graduate, Tsing Hua College. 1918. B.A. (John Hopkins). 1920, M.A.; 1922, Ph.D. (Columbia). Sometime professor of education, Southeastern University, Nanking; head, department of education and psychology, Tsing Hua College. Professor of educational psychology, Amoy University, since September 1928. Author of "Chinese Students in America: Qualities associated with their success" (doctoral dissertation), "Some statistical and measurement terms standardized in Chinese," etc. Translator of Thurstone's "Fundamentals of Statistics" and Garrett's "Statistics in Psychology and Education." Founder and editor, Journal of Education and Psychology (in Chinese). — p. 937

**CHU Ho-hsiang, (朱鶴翔)** CHU HO-HSIANG, (朱鶴翔).—Courtesy name: Feng-ch'ien (鳳千). Prefers French rendering: Louis Ngaosiang Tchou. Born 1888 in and native of Paoshan, Kiangsu. 1912, B.A.; 1913, LL.B.; 1915, doctor in politics and diplomacy (Louvain). Sometime councillor and director, department of political affairs, Ministry of Foreign Affairs; director, department of general affairs, Customs Conference. June 1928, acting vice-minister for foreign affairs. Adviser, Peiping and Tientsin garrison commander-in-chief's headquarters, since August, 1928. Author of "Le regime des Capitulations et la regime constitutionelle en Chine" (doctoral dissertation). — p. 937

**CHU Hsi-tsu, (朱希祖)** CHU HSI-TSU, (朱希祖).—Courtesy name: T'i-hsien (先逷). Born 1879 in and native of Haiying, Chekiang. 1908, graduate, Waseda University. 1912, magistrate of native district. 1913, professor of Chinese, Peking Government University. 1915, assistant compiler, Bureau of National Historiography. 1916-26 dean, Chinese department, Peking National University. Professor of history, Tsing Hua University, since 1927. Research fellow on national language, National Research Institute, since 1928. Author of six treatises on ancient Chinese history. — p. 938

**CHU Hsing-yuan, (祝惺元)** CHU HSING-YUAN, (祝惺元).—Courtesy name: Yen-ch'i (硯溪). Born 1880 in and native of Tahsing, Chihli. Graduate, Peking National University. Sometime secretary and chief of section, Ministry of Foreign Affaris. 1920, also 1925-26, commissioner of foreign affairs, Tientsin. — p. 938

**CHU, Jennings P** CHU, JENNINGS P., see CHU CHUN-YI. — p. 938

**CHU King** CHU KING, see CHU CHING-NUNG. — p. 938

**CHU Lu-ho, (朱履龢)** CHU LU-HO, (朱履龢).—Courtesy name: Hsiao-shan (笑山). Native of Kashing, Chekiang. Studied in London. Sometime secretary, Extraterritoriality Commission, and Customs Conference, Peiping. Political Vice-Minister, Ministry of Judicial Administration, since December 1928. — p. 938

**CHU P'ei-teh, (朱培德)** CHU P'EI-TEH, (朱培德).—Courtesy name: Yi-chih (益之). Born 1890; native of Yenhsing, Yunnan. Graduated from a military academy in Yunnan. 1923, chief aide-de camp to Generalissimo's headquarters, Canton. 1924-6, Northern Expedition 1st and 3rd armies. Chairman, Kiangsi Provincial Government, since 1927; concurrently member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. 1928, Member, Kuomintang Central Political Council. — p. 938

**CHU Shen, (朱深)** CHU SHEN, (朱深).—Courtesy name: Po-yuen (愽淵). Born 1880 in and native of Yungching, Chihli. LL.B. (Tokyo Imperial). 1915, chief procurator, Supreme Court. 1917, minister of justice. 1919, concurrently minister of interior. — p. 938

**CHU T'ing-ch'i, (朱庭祺)** CHU T'ING-CH'I, (朱庭祺).—Courtesy name: T'i-jen (體仁). Prefers English rendering: T. C. Chu. Born 1886 in and native of Wusih, Kiangsu. 1909, B.S. (Harvard). Sometime councillor, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce; English secretary, Shanghai-Nanking Railway. 1922-27, co-director general Kiaochow-Tsinan Railway; chief of through tariffic department, Ministry of Communications. Director, Department of Accounting, Ministry of Finance, since September 1928. — p. 938

**CHU Wen-fu, (朱文甫)** CHU WEN-FU, (朱文甫).—Courtesy name: Fu-ting (佛定). Born 1888; native of Kiangyin, Kiangsu. 1918, docteur en droit, Paris. 1921-8, 2nd secretary, Legation in Washington; councillor and vice-director, Intelligence Bureau, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. — p. 938

**CH'U Yang-kuang, (屈仰光)** CH'U YANG-KUANG, (屈仰光).—Courtesy name: Wen-liu (文六). Born 1881 in and native of Linghai, Chekiang. 1913-20, civil governor of Chekiang and Shantung; adviser to the President. 1926, minister of interior. — p. 938

**CHU Yu-chi, (朱有濟)** CHU YU-CHI, (朱有濟).—Courtesy name: Tso-chou (作舟). Born 1886 in and native of Paoshan, Kiangsu. Sometime director, Chuang Ho salt taxation bureau, Fengtien; director, Shanghai Mint. 1927, vice-minister of finance. — p. 938

**CHU YU-TS'ANG, (朱育滄)** CHU YU-TS'ANG, (朱育滄).—Courtesy name: Mou-ch'en (懋澄). Born 1895; native of Szechwan. B.Sc. (Glasgow). 1918, professor of mechanical engineering, Changsha polytechnic institute. 1921-6, industrial secretary, National Committee of Y.M.C.A., Shanghai. Director, department of labour, Ministry of Industry, Labour and Commerce, since September 1928. — p. 939

**CHU YU-YU, (朱友漁)** CHU YU-YU, (朱友漁).—Prefers English rendering: Y. Y. Tsu. Born 1887 in and native of Shanghai, Kiangsu. 1907, B.A. (St. John's). 1911, M.A.; 1912, Ph.D. (Columbia). B.D. (New York Gen. Theol. Seminary). 1912-20, professor of sociology, St. John's University. 1921-4, in charge of student work of Y.M.C.A. International Committee, U.S.A. Secretary, department of religious and social work, Peking Union Medical College, since 1924. Author of "The Spirit of Chinese Philanthropy" (doctoral dissertation) and co-author of "China through Chinese Eyes." — p. 939

**CH'UAN SHAO-CH'ING, (全紹淸)** CH'UAN SHAO-CH'ING, (全紹淸).—Courtesy name: Hsi-po (希伯). Born 1884 in and native of Wanp'ing, Chihli. Graduate, Peiyang Medical College. M.D. (Johns Hopkins). Sometime professor of anatomy and medicine, also director, Peiyang Medical College; surgeon-general of Chinese Army; commissioner for suppression of pneumonic plague in Shansi. 1924, vice-minister of education; director of public health, Tsingtao. Director, Chinese Red Cross Hospital, Peking, since 1926. Director of Public Health, Tientsin, 1929. — p. 939

**CHUANG CHIH-HUAN, (莊智煥)** CHUANG CHIH-HUAN, (莊智煥).—Courtesy name: Chung-wen (仲文). Prefers English rendering: Joonvin Tzehoan Chwang. Born 1901; native of Ch'inhsien, Chekiang. B.Sc. (Nanyang). Engineer, Ecole superieure d'electricité (Paris). Sometime chief of radio administration, Nanking; superintendent of customs and commissioner of foreign affairs, Wenchow; chief, bureau of rolled tobacco tax, Chekiang. Director, department of telegraphs, Ministry of Communications, since October 1928. — p. 939

**CH'UN, PRINCE (TSAI LI), (醇親王載禮)** CH'UN, PRINCE (TSAI LI), (醇親王載禮).—Brother of emperor Kuang-hsü. 1891, succeeded to father's title. 1901, lieutenant-general of Plain White Banner. 1908, appointed regent on death of Kuang-hsü. December 1911, resigned from the regency. — p. 939

**CHUNG JUNG-KUANG, (鍾榮光)** CHUNG JUNG-KUANG, (鍾榮光).—Prefers Cantonese rendering: Chung Wing-kwong. Chüjen of imperial examinatins. Sometime commissioner of education, Canton; dean, Canton Christian College. President, same institution, since April 1927. Member, National Opium Suppression Commission, since October, 1928. — p. 939

**CHUNG SHIH-MING, (鍾世銘)** CHUNG SHIH-MING, (鍾世銘).—Courtesy name: Hui-sheng (蕙生). Born 1880 in and native of Tientsin. 1908, B.A.; 1909, M.A. (Harvard). 1910-1911, Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. 1921 and 1924, vice-minister of finance. 1922, acting minister of finance. 1927, Commssioner of foreign affairs for Fengtien province. — p. 939

**CHUNG WING-KWONG** CHUNG WING-KWONG, see CHUNG JUNG-KUANG. — p. 939

**CHWANG, JOONVIN TZEHOAN** CHWANG, JOONVIN TZEHOAN, see CHUNG CHIH-HUAN. — p. 939

**DJANG, Y. S** DJANG, Y. S., see CHANG YUAN-SHAN. — p. 939

**EN-K'E-PA-T'U, (恩克巴圖)** EN-K'E-PA-T'U, (恩克巴圖). Courtesy name: Tzu-jung (子榮). Born 1889; native of Charhar. Graduate, College of Mongolian and Tibetan Languages. 1912, member of parliament. 1919, resumed seat in Canton Extraordinary Parliament. 1922, high adviser to President Li Yuan-hung. 1924-7, member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. 1928, member, Kuomintang Central Political Council. Member, Legislative Yuan, since October 1928; concurrently member, Commission on Mongolian and Tibetan Affairs, and trustee, Palace Museum, Peiping. March 1929, elected member, Kuomintang Central Supervisory Committee. — p. 939

**FANG CHEN-WU, (方振武)** FANG CHEN-WU, (方振武).—Courtesy name: Shu-p'ing (叔平). Native of Shouhsien, Anhwei. 1925, commander, Kuominchun 5th army. 1927, commander-in-chief, 11th Route Army, Northern Expedition. 1928, commander, 4th army, Nationalist 1st Group Army. March 1929, elected member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. May 1929, appointed chairman, Anhwei Provincial Government. — p. 940

**FANG CHI-FAN, (方積蕃)** FANG CHI-FAN, (方積蕃).—Courtesy name: Chiao-po (椒伯). Born 1884 in and native of Chenhai, Chekiang. Graduate, Chenchow Law School, Shanghai. Organizer and principal, Pei Yu School, Chenhai, and Chihai School, Ningpo. 1918, chief arbitrator, Chinese general chamber of commerce. 1920, director, Shanghai Mint. 1922, vice-chairman, Chinese General Chamber of Commerce and Chinese Ratepayers' Association. Director of many industrial and shipping concerns in Shanghai and Chenhai. — p. 940

**FENG CHIAO-HUI, (方覺慧)** FENG CHIAO-HUI, (方覺慧).—Courtesy name: Tzu-ch'iao (子樵). Native of Chinch'un, Hupeh. Member of Tung Meng Hui. 1911, editor of Ta Kiang Pao, Hankow. 1913, editor, Chen Tan Pao. Political refugee in foreign countries for ten years. 1926, vice-director of political training, Commander-in-Chief's headquarters, Canton. 1928, same post in Directorate-General of Military Training. Member, Legislative Yuan, since January 1929. Two months later elected member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. — p. 940

**FANG PEN-JEN, (方本仁)** FANG PEN-JEN, (方本仁).—Courtesy name: Yueh-t'ing ([illegible]庭). Born 1880; native of Huangkang, Hupeh. 1908, Graduate, Peking Military College. Sometime military governor of Kiangsi; commander, 11th army, Northern Expedition; adviser, Commander-in-Chief's Headquarters. 1928, member, Taiyuan branch of Kuomintang Central Political Council; member, military council. Member, Hupeh Provincial Government, and concurrently director, civil affairs department, since April 1929. — p. 940

**FENG CH'ING-KUEI, (馮慶桂)** FENG CH'ING-KUEI, (馮慶桂).—Prefers to sign himself: H. K. Fung. Native of Kwangtung. 1908, B.S.; 1910, M.A.; 1911, Ph.D. (Cornell). Sometime expert, Bureau of Plant Industry, U. S. Department of Agriculture, and bureau of cotton investigation, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce. 1922, assistant councillor, Ministry of Finance. Author of "A Study of the American Cotton Plant with special reference to its introduction into China" (doctoral dissertation) and "Classification of Chinese Literature". — p. 940

**FENG YU-HSIANG, (馮玉祥)** FENG YU-HSIANG, (馮玉祥).—Courtesy name: Huan-chang (煥章). Born 1880 in and native of Ch'ao-hsien, Anhwei. Attended Peiyang Miltary College, and Paoting Military Academy. 1913, commander, 16th mixed brigade. 1920, played important part in Chihli-Anfu war. 1921, acting military governor of Shensi; troops later reorganized into 11th division. 1922, played decisive part in Chihli-Fengtien war; Tuchun (military governor) of Honan; inspector-general of the national army, with headquarters in Peking. 1923, director-general of Northwestern defence; promoted field marshal. 1924, made President Ts'ao K'un prisoner; ejected Manchu emperor and, with Chang Tso-lin's support, installed Marshal Tuan Ch'i-jui as provisional chief executive. 1925, defence commissioner of Northwest territory. 1926, retired to sojourn in Moscow. 1927, returned from Russia. 1928, joined forces with the Nationalists and co-operated in unifying China Proper. October 1928, appointed State Councillor and Vice-President, Executive Yuan, as well as minister for military affairs. March 1929, re-elected member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. May 1929, re-elected member, Kuomintang Central Political Council. June 1929, expelled from Kuomintang and dismissed from all posts for destroying railway bridges and committing other acts of defiance against National Government. — p. 940

**FENG YU-LAN, (馮玉闌)** FENG YU-LAN, (馮玉闌).—Prefers to sign himself: Fung Yu-lan. Born 1890; native of Honan. 1923, Ph.D. (Columbia). Sometime, dean, department of liberal arts, Chung Chow University, Kaifeng (Honan). Professor of philosophy, Yenching University, since 1927. Author of "A comparative study of life ideals" (doctoral dissertation), also Chinese translation of same. Editor, Mind's Echo (Kaifeng). — p. 941

**FOO PING-SHEUNG** FOO PING-SHEUNG, see FU PIN-CH'ANG. — p. 941

**FU LIANG-TSO, (傅良佐)** FU LIANG-TSO, (傅良佐).—Courtesy name: Chieh-ch'ing (節清). Native of Kanch'eng, Hunan. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. 1912, civil and military governor of Charhar. 1916, vice-minister of war. 1917, Tuchun (military governor) of Hunan. — p. 941

**FU, PEICHING TUNG** FU, PEICHING TUNG, see FU T'UNG. — p. 941

**FU PIN-CH'ANG, 傅秉常)** FU PIN-CH'ANG, 傅秉常).—Prefers anglicized Cantonese rendering: Foo Ping-sheung. Born 1896; native of Nanhai. Kwangtung. 1916, graduate, Hongkong University. 1918-20, secretary to Dr. Wu T'ing-fang. 1920-2, superintendent of customs and commissioner of foreign affairs, Kiungchow. 1922-6, superintendent of customs and commissioner of foreign affairs, Canton. 1927, director of Customs Administration, and chairman, Tariff Commission, Nanking. 1928, member, Legislative Yuan. Minister to Belgium since March 1929. — p. 941

**FU SIAO-EN** FU SIAO-EN, see FU TSUNG-YAO. — p. 941

**FU TING-SHEN, (符鼎升)** FU TING-SHEN, (符鼎升).—Courtesy name: Chiu-ming (九銘). Prefers to sign himself: Ting Seng Foo. Born 1879; native of Ihuang, Kiangsi. Studied in Tokyo Normal College. 1912, commissioner of education, Kiangsi. 1913-7, senator, 1918, commissioner of education, Kiangsu. 1927-8, secretary and councillor, Ministry of Communications, Nanking. Director, department of general affairs, same Ministry, since November 1928. — p. 941

**FU TSENG-HSIANG, (傅增湘)** FU TSENG-HSIANG, (傅增湘).—Courtesy name: Yuan-shu (沅叔). Born 1872 in and native of Chiangan, Szechwan. Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. Sometime secretary to viceroy Yuan Shih-k'ai; commissioner of education for Chihli province. 1914-6, minister of education; superintendent of students in Japan. 1922, director, financial liquidation office. — p. 941

**FU TSUNG-YAO, (傅宗耀)** FU TSUNG-YAO, (傅宗耀). Courtesy name: Hsiao-an (筱菴). Better known as: Fu Siao-en. Born 1871 in and native of Chenhai, Chekiang. 1921, managing director, China Merchants Steam Navigaton Company. One of Shanghai's most prominent merchants; general manager, Commercial Bank of China; director of Chinese general chamber of commerce and numerous banking and industrial concerns. — p. 941

**FU T'UNG, (傅銅)** FU T'UNG, (傅銅).—Courtesy name: P'ei-ch'ing (佩青). Prefers English rendering: Peiching Tung Fu. Born 1887 in and native of Lanfang, Honan. Studied in Tokyo and Oxford. M.A. (Birmingham). Professor of philosophy, Peking National University, since 1926. — p. 941

**FUNG, H. K** FUNG, H. K., see FENG CH'ING-KUEI. — p. 941

**FUNG YU-LAN** FUNG YU-LAN, see FENG YU-LAN. — p. 941

**HAN AN, (韓安)** HAN AN, (韓安).—Courtesy name: Chu-p'ing (竹坪). Prefers to sign himself: N. Han. Born 1885 in and native of Chaohsien, Anhwei. 1905, B.A. (Nanking). 1909, B.A. (Cornell). 1911, M.Sc. in forestry (Michigan). Post-graduate work in Wisconsin University. Sometime director, department of forestry, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce; editor-in-chief, Agriculture and Forestry Review. 1928, commissioner of education for Anhwei province. — p. 941

**HAN KUO-CHUN, (韓國鈞)** HAN KUO-CHUN, (韓國鈞).—Born 1858; native of Kiangsu. Sometime commissioner of foreign affairs, Mukden; civil governor of Anhwei and Kiangsu. — p. 942

**HAN LIN-CH'UN, (韓麟春)** HAN LIN-CH'UN, (韓麟春).—1926, commander, Mukden 4th army corps. Mukden armies. Director-General, Mukden Arsenal, since 1928. — p. 942

**HAN, N** HAN, N., see HAN AN. — p. 942

**HO CH'ENG-CHUN, (何成濬)** HO CH'ENG-CHUN, (何成濬).—Courtesy name: Hsueh-chu (雪竹). Born 1882; native of Suihsien, Hupeh. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. 1912, deputy aide-de-camp, Ministry of War, Nanking. 1923, commander-in-chief, Nationalist forces in Fukien. 1924, commander-in-chief, left wing, Northern Expedition. 1925-7, chief councillor, Commander-in-Chief's headquarters. 1928, member, Hupeh Provincial Government; acting mayor of Peiping; chief aide-de-camp, National Government Headquarters. March 1929, elected member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee. April 1929, appointed chairman, Hupeh Provincial Government. — p. 942

**HO CH'I-KUNG, (何其鞏)** HO CH'I-KUNG, (何其鞏). Courtesy name: K'e-chih (克之). Born 1899; native of T'ungch'eng, Anhwei. Sometime chief secretary to General Feng Yu-hsiang. 1924 and 1926, went to Moscow to study political conditions. 1927-8, director, department of civil affairs, South Honan; member, Kaifeng branch, Kuomintang Central Political Council. July 1928-May 1929, Mayor of Greater Peiping. — p. 942

**HO CHIEH-TS'AI, (何傑才)** HO CHIEH-TS'AI, (何傑才).—Courtesy name: Ch'i-wei (其偉). Prefers English rendering: G. Zay Wood. Born 1894; native of Shanghai. Graduate, Nanyang and Tsing Hua College. 1917, B.A. (Yale). 1918, M.A. (Harvard). Sometime editor, Peking Daily News; secretary, Cabinet Office and Customs Conference. 1927-8, acting commissioner for foreign affairs; director, Land Office, Shanghai; departmental chief, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Nanking. Author of "The Anglo-Japanese Alliance," "Twenty-one Demands," "A Study in Diplomacy and World Politics," "China, Japan, and 1915 Treaties." — p. 942

**HO CHIEN, (何健)** HO CHIEN, (何健).—Courtesy name: Yün-ch'iao (芸樵). Born 1887 in and native of Liling, Hunan. Graduate, Paoting military academy. 1920, rose to commander, 9th brigade. 1926, commander, 2nd division, Nationalist 8th army. 1927, commander, Nationalist 35th army; acting chairman, Anhwei Provincial Government; chairman, commission for rehabilitation of western Hunan. 1928, associate director-general for the pacification of Hunan Province; commander, reorganized 6th division, 4th Group Army. Chairman, Hunan Provincial Government, since April 1929. — p. 942

**HO FENG-LIN, (何豐[illegible])** HO FENG-LIN, (何豐[illegible]).—Courtesy name: Mou-ju (茂如). Born 1873 in and native of Pingyin, Shantung. Graduate, Peiyang military college. Sometime defence commissioner, Shanghai and Sungkiang; presiding judge of special court for trying Communists arrested in raid of Soviet embassy. 1927, minister of military affairs. — p. 942

**HO JUI-CHANG, (何瑞章)** HO JUI-CHANG, (何瑞章).—Courtesy name: Tz'u-heng (次衡). Prefers English rendering: J. C. Ho. Born 1887 in and native of Nanlin, Anhwei. Graduate, Kiangnan High School, Nanking. Chüjen of imperial examinations. Sometime associate director, Chu-Ching and Peking-Suiyuan Railways; acting councillor, Ministry of Communications. — p. 942

**HO WEI, (何蔚)** HO WEI, (何蔚).—Courtesy name: Shen-ch'i (愼其). Born 1883; native of Hsingning, Kwangtung. LL.B. (Tokyo Imperial). Sometime chief procurator, Canton High Court. Senior judge, Supreme Court, since December 1928. — p. 942

**HO Ying-ch'in, (何應欽)** HO YING-CH'IN, (何應欽). Courtesy name: Ching-chih (敬之). Born 1889 in and native of Hsingi, Kweichow. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. Member of Tung Meng Hui. Participated in 1911 and 1913 revolutions. 1920-3, principal, Yunnan military institute. 1924, chief instructor, Whampoa Military Academy. 1926, acting chairman, Fukien Provincial Government. 1927, member, Kiangsu and Kweichow Provincial Governments. 1928, chairman, Chekiang Provincial Government; dean, Central Military College. State Councillor and chief of staff, Commander-in-Chief's Headquarters, since October 1928. Member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee, since 1926. Member, Kuomintang Central Political Council, since 1927. Director-General of Military Training since December 1928. Has been specially commended by the National Government for his meritorious services as fighter and tactician. — p. 943

**HOA, T. T** HOA, T. T., see HU NAN-KUAI. — p. 943

**HOO, Victor Chi-tsai** HOO, VICTOR CHI-TSAI, see HU SHIH-TSEH. — p. 943

**HOO Wei-teh** HOO WEI-TEH, see HU WEI-TEH. — p. 943

**HSIA Ch'i-feng, (夏奇峯)** HSIA CH'I-FENG, (夏奇峯).—Born 1889; native of Kiangsu. Sometime assistant editor, Shanghai Eastern Times, and its correspondent in Paris and Geneva. 1923-8, member of Information Section, Secretariat of League of Nations, Geneva. — p. 943

**HSIA Chin-lin, (夏晉麟)** HSIA CHIN-LIN, (夏晉麟).—Prefers English rendering: C. L. Hsia. Born 1894; native of Ningpo, Chekiang. Graduate, Tientsin Anglo-Chinese College. 1919, B.Sc. (Glasgow). 1920, M.A.; 1922, Ph.D. (Edinburgh). Sometime secretary, Weihaiwei Retrocession Commission; dean of arts faculty, Southern University, Shanghai; secretary, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Nanking. President, Medhurst College, Shanghai, since July 1927. Author of "Treaty Relations between China and Great Britain" (doctoral dissertation). — p. 943

**HSIA Ch'ing-yi, (夏清貽)** HSIA CH'ING-YI, (夏清貽).—Courtesy name: Sung-lai (来頌). Prefers English rendering: C. Y. Hsia. Born 1877 in and native of Chiating, Kiangsu. Studied in Waseda University, Tokyo. Sometime secretary, Cabinet Office; councillor, Bureau of Printing, Peking. — p. 943

**HSIA Jen-hu, (夏仁虎)** HSIA JEN-HU, (夏仁虎).—Courtesy name: Wei-ju (如蔚). 1926-7, vice-minister of finance 1927-8, chief secretary to the Cabinet. — p. 943

**HSIA Yen-ts'ao, (夏彥藻)** HSIA YEN-TS'AO, (夏彥藻).—Courtesy name: Ch'in-hsi (琴西). Born 1890; native of Tientsin, Chihli. Graduate, Chihli Law College. Chief secretary, Tientsin chamber of commerce; editor, Hua Pei Shih Pao; and legal adviser to Tientsin flour association and bankers' association. — p. 943

**HSIA Yi-t'ing, (夏貽霆)** HSIA YI-T'ING, (夏貽霆).—Courtesy name: T'ing-chai (梃齋).—Born 1878 in and native of Kiangyin, Kiangsu. Sometime vice-minister of foreign affairs. 1917-8, minister to Brazil and Peru. — p. 943

**HSIANG Hsiang, (項驤)** HSIANG HSIANG, (項驤).—Courtesy name: Wei-ch'en (微塵). Prefers to sign himself: Witson H. Shan. Born 1880 in and native of Juian, Chekiang. 1910, M.A. (Columbia); Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. Sometime acting superintendent, Bank of China; vice-minister of finance. — p. 943

**HSIAO Chi-jung, (蕭繼榮)** HSIAO CHI-JUNG, (蕭繼榮).—Courtesy name: Liang-kung (亮功). Born 1888; native of Kwangsi. Chüjen of imperial examinations. Graduate of Berlin University. Second secretary and charge d'affaires at Berne, since 1926. — p. 943

**HSIAO Fu-ch'eng, (蕭佛成)** HSIAO FU-CH'ENG, (蕭佛成).—Courtesy name: T'ieh-ch'iao (鐵橋). Born 1862 in Singapore; native of Fukien. Proprietor, and editor of a Chinese daily in Siam. Chairman, Siam branch of Kuomintang. 1926, member, Kuomintang Central Supervisory Committee. March 1929, re-elected to same post. 1928, member, Kuomintang Central Political Council, and member, Chinese Overseas Commission. — p. 943

**HSIAO Kung-chuan, (蕭公權)** HSIAO KUNG-CHUAN, (蕭公權).—Prefers English rendering: K. C. Hsiao. Born 1897; native of Kiangsi. 1920, graduate, Tsing Hua College. 1922, B.A.; 1923, M.A. (Missouri). 1926, Ph.D. (Cornell). Professor of Political Science, Nankai University, Tientsin, since 1927. Author of "Political Pluralism: a Study in Contemporary Political Theory." — p. 944

**HSIAO Yu-mei, (蕭友梅)** HSIAO YU-MEI, (蕭友梅).—Born 1884 in and native of Chungshan-hsien, Kwangtung. 1909, B.A. (Tokyo Imperial). 1916, Ph.D. (Leipzig). 1912, secretary, President's Office, Nanking. 1921-7, lecturer, Peking Government University; dean, department of music, College of Fine Arts, Peking. Dean and acting principal, National Conservatory of Music, Shanghai, since October 1927. Author of several volumes on the general theory of music, outline of harmony, etc. — p. 944

**HSIAO Yung-hsi, (蕭永熙)** HSIAO YUNG-HSI, (蕭永熙).—Courtesy name: Tz'u-hsiu (次修). Born 1878 in and native of Huayang, Szechwan. Sometime co-director, Peking Octroi; chargé d'Affaires in Stockholm and Christiania; director, department of navigation, Ministry of Communications. — p. 944

**HSIEH En-lung, (謝恩隆)** HSIEH EN-LUNG, (謝恩隆).—Courtesy name: Meng-po (孟博). Born 1884 in and native of Canton, Kwangtung. Graduate, Hongkong Queen's College and Peiyang University. 1909, B.Sc. (Boston). 1910, M.Sc. (Cornell). Sometime technical expert, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce, and Ministry of Communications. — p. 944

**HSIEH Shou-ch'ang, (謝壽昌)** HSIEH SHOU-CH'ANG, (謝壽昌).—Courtesy name: Kuan-sheng (冠生). Prefers French rendering: Sie Cheou-tchang. Born 1897 in and native of Chenghsien, Chekiang. 1915-8, ed,itor, Commercial Press. 1921, bachelor of laws, Shanghai Aurora University. 1924, docteur en droit, Paris University. 1924-6, lecturer in various universities in Shanghai. Secretary, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Nanking, and dean, department of law, Central University, since Septemberr 1927. Author of "Esquisse d'une Histoire du Droit Chinois" (doctoral dissertation) and co-editor of several dictionaries. — p. 944

**HSIEH Tso-chieh, (謝作楷)** HSIEH TSO-CHIEH, (謝作楷).—Prefers anglicized Cantonese rendering: Tse Tsok Kai. Born 1886; native of Hsinhui, Kwangtung. 1908, B.S. (Mass. Inst. Tech.). 1909, M.S. Sometime agricultural and forestry inspector, Kwangtung Province; member, Canton Municipal Government; director, rolled tobacco tax bureau, Canton. At present chairman, president, Canton Municipality's electric light company. — p. 944

**HSIEH Wan-ying, Miss, (謝婉瑩女士)** HSIEH WAN-YING, MISS, (謝婉瑩女士).—Native of Fukien. Born in Foochow, 1902. Began to write verse when twelve years old. 1923, B.A. (Yenching Women's College). 1926, M.A. (Wellesley College). Writes under nom de plume of Ping-hsin (冰心), and is recognized as forerunner of modern verse. Author of "The Stars" (繁星); "The Spring Water" (春水); "Superman and Other Stories" (超人); and "The Letters" (寄小讀者). Instructor in Chinese, Yenching University, Peking, since September 1926. — p. 944

**HSIEH Wei-lin, (謝維麟)** HSIEH WEI-LIN, (謝維麟).—Courtesy name: Chen-shu (振叔). Prefers English rendering: William Hsieh. Born 1893 in Tientsin; native of Sungkiang, Kiangsu. Studied in Soochow University and Université libre de Bruxelles (Brussels). 1918, Licencié en Droit, Paris University. 1920-7, secretary, Legations in Rome and The Hague; technical expert, delegation to the Geneva Opium Conference; secretary, Customs Conference. Senior Secretary, Ministry of Judicial Administration, since September 1928. — p. 944

**HSIEH, William** HSIEH, WILLIAM, see above. — p. 944

**HSIEH Ying-chou, (謝瀛洲)** HSIEH YING-CHOU, (謝瀛洲).—Courtesy name: Hsien-t'ing (仙庭). Prefers to sign as: Sié Ying Chou. Born 1894 in and native of Ts'unghua, Kwangtung. 1924, docteur en droit (Paris). 1925, professor of law, Chungshan University, Canton. 1926, principal, Canton Law College. Principal, College of Law, Peiping National University, since November 1928; concurrently, Vice-Minister, Ministry of Judicial Administration. Author of "Federalisme en chine" (doctoral dissertation). — p. 945

**HSIEH Yuan-fu, (謝元甫)** HSIEH YUAN-FU, (謝元甫).—Prefers English rendering: George Y. Char. Born in Honolulu, 1887; native of Hsiangshan, Kwangtung. 1910, B.S. (Boone). 1914, M.D. (St. John's and Harvard Medical School, Shanghai). 1917, postgraduate studies in Harvard Postgraduate School of Medicine. 1918-9, resident urologist, Long Island College Hospital, Brooklyn; house surgeon, Bellevue Hospital, New York. 1920-7, associate in surgery, Peking Union Medical College; medical adviser, Ministry of War. Medical director, Peking Central Hospital, and attending urologist, Peking Union Medical College, since May 1927. — p. 945

**HSING Shih-lien, (邢士廉)** HSING SHIH-LIEN, (邢士廉).—Courtesy name: Ou-san (隅三). Born 1885; native of Tungkuang, Hopei (Chihli). Graduate, Tokyo Officers Training Academy. 1922, commander, 24th brigade. 1925, commander, Northeastern 20th division; defence commissioner of Shanghai and Woosung. 1926, commander, Peking garrison. Member, Liaoning (Fengtien) Provincial Government, since January 1929. — p. 945

**HSIUNG Ch'ung-chih, (熊崇志)** HSIUNG CH'UNG-CHIH, (熊崇志).—Prefers English rendering: Samuel S. Young. Born 1884; native of Kwangtung. 1904, B.A. (California). 1905, M.A. (Columbia). 1907, Chinshih of imperial examinations. Sometime president, Tangshan Engineering College; deparmental chief, Grand Canal improvement board; secretary, Customs Conference and Ministry for Foreign Affairs, Peiping. Consul-General to New York since June 1927. — p. 945

**HSIUNG Hsi-ling, (熊希齡)** HSIUNG HSI-LING, (熊希齡).—Courtesy name: Pin-san (秉三). Born 1867 in and native of Fenghuang, Hunan. Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. Sometime Tutung (civil and military governor) of Jehol; premier and minister of finance; director-general, national oil administration, flood relief and Grand Canal improvement board. Founder of Orphanage at Hsiangshan, Western Hills, Peking. Promoter of charitable institutions and director of philanthropic societies. — p. 945

**HSIUNG K'e-wu, (熊克武)** HSIUNG K'E-WU, (熊克武).—Born 1882; native of Szechwan. Graduate, Tokyo Military College. Sometime defence commissioner, Chungking, and civil governor of Szechwan. — p. 945

**HSIUNG Pin, (熊斌)** HSIUNG PIN, (熊斌).—Courtesy name: Che-ming (哲明). Born 1893 in and native of Huangan, Hupeh. Graduate, Peking Military College. Sometime vice-minister of war; chief counsellor, General Feng Yu-hsiang's headquarters. 1928, member, Hupeh Provincial Government; senior counsellor, Commander-in-Chief's Headquarters, Nanking. Director, Aviation Administration, Ministry of Miilitary Affairs, November 1928. — p. 945

**HSU Ch'ien, (徐謙)** HSU CH'IEN, (徐謙).—Courtesy name: Chi-lung (季龍). Prefers English rendering: George Chien Hsu. Born 1872. Native of Hsihsien, Anhwei. Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. Sometime vice-minister of justice; minister of justice, Canton; editor-in-chief, Tientsin and Peking Yi Shih Pao ("Social Welfare"). 1925-6, head, Canton diplomatic mission to Peking; member, Kuomistang Central Executive Committee. Regarded as a Communist leader. Proscribed by Nanking Government since July 1927 and repudiated by Hankow Government since August 1927. Author of several books on literature and Christianity. — p. 945

**HSU Chien-p'ing, (許建屏)** HSU CHIEN-P'ING, (許建屏).—Courtesy name: Yen-fan (彥藩). Prefers English rendering: Jabin Hsu. Born 1889 in Shanghai; native of Chekiang. Graduate, Tsing Hua College. 1914, B.A. (Michigan). Sometime chief sub-editor, China Press, Shanghai, and managing director, Shanghai Tribune. Secretary, Ministry of Industry, Labour and Commerce, since May 1928. Author of "My Own Report of the Washington Conference." — p. 946

**HSU Ch'ih, (徐[illegible])** HSU CH'IH, (徐[illegible]).—Courtesy name: Hsuan-yen (巽言). Prefers English rendering: Charles S. Y. Shu-tze. Born 1886; native of Hangchow, Chekiang. Graduate, Christ College (Cambridge), and Université Libre (Brussels). Sometime councillor, Ministry of Interior, and substitute delegate, International Labour Conference, Geneva. — p. 946

**HSU Chih-mo, (徐志摩)** HSU CHIH-MO, (徐志摩).—Prefers to sign himself: Hsu Tse-mou. Born 1897; native of Chekiang. Studied in Shanghai, Tientsin and Peking before spending two years in America and another two years in London and Cambridge (King's College). 1923-6, professor of literature, Peking National University; literary editor, Peking Morning Post. Professor of Literature, Kwang Hua University, Shanghai, since 1927. Editor of New Moon Monhly (新月月刊). Author of (1) "Poems" (志摩的詩), 1925; "A Florentine Night" (翡冷翠的一[illegible]), 1927; "Those Fallen Leaves" (落葉散文集), 1925; "Souvenirs of Paris" (巴黎的鱗爪), 1927; "Self-Mutilation and Other Essays (自剖文集), 1928; "Bien-Kuen-Kwan" (卞昆岡), a tragedy in five acts, 1928. Translator of Voltaire, Thomas Hardy, Tagore, Katherine Mansfield, etc. Address: 613 Avenue Foch, Shanghai. — p. 946

**HSU Fou, (徐桴)** HSU FOU, (徐桴) —Courtesy same: Sheng-shan (聖禪). Native of Chenhai, Chekiang. 1916, chief of statistical department, civil governor's office, Canton. 1926, chief auditor, Commander-in-Chief's headquarters, Northern Expedition. 1927, quarter-master-general, Nanking. 1928, chief department of military discipline. Commissioner of Finance, Fukien Provincial Government, since September 1928. — p. 946

**HSU Fu-ssu, (徐佛蘇)** HSU FU-SSU, (徐佛蘇).—Born 1880; native of Chekiang. Graduate, Tokyo Higher Normal College. Sometime adviser to President Yuan Shih-k'ai, but implacable opponent of his monarchical scheme; delegate to Shanghai peace conference; director, currency bureau; chairman, commission for discussion of China's finances. — p. 946

**HSU, George Chien** HSU, GEORGE CHIEN, see HSU CH'IEN. — p. 946

**HSU Hsi-an, (許錫安)** HSU HSI-AN, (許錫安).—Courtesy name: Chih-ch'i (志祺). Prefers to sign himself: Shi-an Shu (Khaw). Born 1891 in and native of Tsingkiang, Fukien. Graduate, Peking Union Medical College. President, Westminster College, Ch'uanchow, Amoy (Fukien), since 1925. — p. 946

**HSU Hsin-liu, (徐新六)** HSU HSIN-LIU, (徐新六).—Courtesy name: Chen-fei (振飛). Prefers English rendering: Singloh Hsu. Born 1890 in and native of Hangchow. Graduate, Nanyang College. 1911, B.Sc. (Birmingham). 1913, B. Com. (Manchester). Sometime sub-manager, Bank of China, Peking office; technical expert, delegation to Paris Conference. General Manager, National Commercial Bank, Shanghai Head Office, since 1926. April 1929, elected councillor, Shanghai Municipal Council. — p. 946

**HSU, Jabin** HSU, JABIN, see HSU CHIEN-P'ING. — p. 946

**HSU Lan-chou, (許闌洲)** HSU LAN-CHOU, (許闌洲).—Courtesy name: Chih-t'ien (芝田). Born 1873 in and native of Nankun, Chihli. 1913, made major general for successful suppression of Hunghutzu (bandits). Sometime acting military governor of Heilungkiang; chief of staff to high Inspecting commissioner of Manchuria. — p. 946

**HSU Mo, (徐謨)** HSU MO, (徐謨).—Born 1892 at and native of Soochow, Kiangsu. 1916, LL.B. (Peiyang). 1921, M.A. (George Washington). 1920-2, attaché, Legation in Washington, and secretary, delegation to Washington Conference. 1922-6, professor of law and dean, College of Arts, Nankai University, Tientsin. 1926, managing editor, Yi Shih Pao ("Social Welfare"), Tientsin. 1927, judge, Shanghai Provisional Court; chief judge, Local Court, Sungkiang (Kiangsu). Director, Department of European and American Affairs, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, since August 1928. Concurrently special commissioner for foreign affairs, Shanghai, since May 1929. — p. 947

**HSU Shih-chang, (徐世章)** HSU SHIH-CHANG, (徐世章).—Courtesy name: Tuan-fu (端甫). Born 1886. Native of Honan. 1911, B. Com. (Liege). Sometime managing director, Tientsin-Pukow and Pukow-Sinyangchow Railways; vice-minister of communications; deputy governor, Bank of Communications. 1922, retired. — p. 947

**HSU Shih-ch'ang, (徐世昌)** HSU SHIH-CH'ANG, (徐世昌).—Courtesy name: Chü-jen (菊人). Born 1858 in Tientsin; native of Honan. 1886, Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. 1903, senior councillor, Board of Commerce. 1906, grand councillor of state. 1907, viceroy of Manchuria. 1909, president, board of communications; concurrently director general, Tientsin-Pukow Railway. 1910, re-appinted grand councillor of state. 1911, associate prime minister and vice-president of Privy Council; chief of general staff grand guardian of the ex-emperor. May 1914, minister of interior. September 1918-June 1922, president of China. 1921, D. Litt. honoris causa (Paris) conferred in absentia, Res. Tientsin. — p. 947

**HSU Shih-ying, (許世英)** HSU SHIH-YING, (許世英).—Courtesy name: Chün-jen (儁人). Born 1872 in and native of Chiupu, Anhwei. Sometime minister of justice; civil governor of Anhwei, minister of communications; secretary general, Reorganization Conference. 1925-6, premier; acting minister of finance. Chairman, National Famine Relief Commission, since December 1928. — p. 947

**HSU Shu-hsi, (徐淑希)** HSU SHU-HSI, (徐淑希).—Prefers English rendering: Shuhsi Hsu. Born 1894; native of Kwangtung. 1924, Ph.D. (Columbia); lecturer in international law, Hongkong University. Professor and head of political science department, Yenching University, Peking, since 1925. Chairman, Commission on Studies, Yenching University, since July 1927. Author of (doctoral dissertation) "China and her political entity (A Study of China's foreign relations with reference to Korea, Manchuria and Mongolia)." — p. 947

**HSU, Singloh** HSU, SINGLOH, see HSU HSIN-LIU. — p. 947

**HSU, Showin Wetsen** HSU, SHOWIN WETSEN, see HSU WEI-CHEN. — p. 947

**HSU T'ing-hu, (徐廷瑚)** HSU T'ING-HU, (徐廷瑚).—Courtesy name: Hai-fan (海帆). Born 1889; native of Yihsien, Hopei (Chihli). Graduate, Paoting agricultural college. 1916, master of science (Paris). 1922 and 1926, principal, agricultural department, Hopei University. 1925, principal, Charhar Industrial College; vice-director, Agricultural College, Peiping. 1928, acting president, Hopei University. Director, department of agriculture, Ministry of Agriculture and Mining, since August 1928. — p. 947

**HSU Tse-mou** HSU TSE-MOU, see HSU CHIH-MO. — p. 947

**HSU Tsu-shan, (徐祖善)** HSU TSU-SHAN, (徐祖善).—Courtesy name: Yen-mou (燕謀). Prefers English rendering: T. S. Shu. Born 1890 in and native of Wusih, Kiangsu. Graduate, Nanking naval school. 1915, M.A. in naval architecture (Massachusetts Inst. of Tech.) Sometime assistant naval attaché, Legation in London; superintendent, Kiaochow and Tientsin Customs. — p. 947

**HSU Tung-fan, (徐東藩)** HSU TUNG-FAN, (徐東藩).—Courtesy name: Shou-ch'eng (壽城) Native of Kinghua, Chekiang. 1916, B.A. (Birmingham). 1916-8, studied at London, Lausanne and Zürich universities. 1919-21, chief diplomatic secretary to civil governor of Shantung; counsellor, Delegation to Washington Conference. 1922, member, Joint Commission for Settlement of Shantung Question. 1923, director, drafting department, Directorate General of Sino-Russian Affairs. 1924-7, commissioner for foreign affairs, Shantung. 1928, vice-president, Treaty Commission, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. Councillor, same Ministry, since December 1928. — p. 948

**HSU Wei-chen, (徐維震)** HSU WEI-CHEN, (徐維震).—Courtesy name: Hsü-ying (旭瀛). Prefers English rendering: Showin Wetsen Hsu. Born 1881 in and native of Tunghsing, Chekiang. Graduate, Nanyang College. 1909, LL.B. (Indiana). 1911-1912, Chinshih of imperial examinations; judge, Supreme Court. 1919-26, president, Taiyuan High Court, Shansi; Chinese secretary general, Commission on Extraterritorial Jurisdiction. 1927-8, president, Shanghai Provisional Court. Councillor, Judicial Yuan, since December 1928. — p. 948

**HSU Yuan, (許沅)** HSU YUAN, (許沅).—Courtesy name: Ch'iu-feng (秋颿). Born 1878 in and native of Chinkiang, Kiangsu. Sometime commissioner of foreign affairs in Tientsin, Kaifeng, and Shanghai. Member, Treaty Commission, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, since September 1928. — p. 948

**HSUAN T'ung** HSUAN T'UNG, see P'U YI. — p. 948

**HSUEH Hsueh-hai, (薛學海)** HSUEH HSUEH-HAI, (薛學海).—Courtesy name: Hui-tung (滙東). Prefers to sign himself: Hsueh Hai Hsieh. Son-in-law of late Yuan Shih-kai. Born 1887 in and native of Wusih, Kiangsu. 1917, B.S. (Wisconsin). Sometime adviser to Chihli civil governor and commissioner of foreign affairs, Tientsin. — p. 948

**HSUEH Tu-pi, (薛篤弼)** HSUEH TU-PI, (薛篤弼).—Courtesy name: Tzu-liang (子良). Born 1892 in and native of Chien-hsien, Shansi. Graduate, Taiyuan Provincial Law College. 1912, judge, Hotung Local Court, Shansi. 1914, secretary to General Feng Yü-hsiang. 1919-20, magistrate of Changteh. 1921, magistrate of Changan and director of opium suppression in Shensi. 1922, commissioner of finance in Shensi and Honan. 1923-4, vice-minister of justice; chief secretary of the Cabinet. 1924-6, officiating minister of justice; director, Peking Octroi; mayor of Peking; civil governor of Kansu. 1928, minister of interior. Minister of Health, since October 1928. — p. 948

**HU Han-min, (胡漢民)** HU HAN-MIN, (胡漢民).—Courtesy name: Chen-t'ang (展堂). Born 1884; native of P'anyü, Kwangtung. Graduate, Tokyo Law College. 1905, met Dr. Sun Yat-sen and organized Tung Meng Hui (Revolutionary Party). Edited a revolutionary newspaper at Hongkong prior to 1911. At outbreak of Revolution, elected Provisional Tutuh (military governor) of Canton, but accompanied Dr. Sun to Nanking as chief secretary of Provisional Government. March 1912, re-elected Tutuh, Canton. 1913, fled to Japan after failure of Second Revolution. 1917, chief secretary, Generalissimo's headquarters, Canton. 1920, civil governor, Canton. 1924-5, acting generalissimo during Dr. Sun's absence from Canton; visited Moscow as Canton delegate to investigate agrarian problems and Soviet system. May 1927, chairman, Nationalist Government, Nanking. Summer of 1927, autumn of 1928, travelled in Europe. State Councillor and President, Legislative Yuan, since October 1928. Member, Kuomintang Central Executive Committee, since 1924. Member, Kuomintang Central Political Council, since 1925. Author of many volumes on revolution and socialism. — p. 948

**HU Hung-yu, (胡鴻[illegible])** HU HUNG-YU, (胡鴻[illegible]).—Courtesy name: Chen-jo (徵若). Born 1888 in and native of Wusih, Kiangsu. Graduate, Nanyang College. 1909, B.S.; 1910, M.A. (Pennsylvania). Sometime Nanking manager, Kiangsu Bank; director, railway through traffic administration. Chief of Traffic Section, Ministry of Railways, since December 1928. — p. 948

**HU Lin, (胡林)** HU LIN, (胡林).—Courtesy name: Cheng-chih (政之). Born 1893; native of Szechwan. Chüjen of imperial examinations. Graduate, Tokyo Imperial University. 1912-4, editor, Ta Kung Ho Pao (Shanghai); judge, Chinkiang local court. 1915-7, councillor, Ministry of Interior; editor, Ta Kung Pao ("L'Impartial"), Tientsin. Founder and chief editor, Kuo Wen News Agency, Shanghai and Peking, since 1921. — p. 949

**HU Pin, (胡贇)** HU PIN, (胡贇).—Courtesy name: Ting-an (定安). Born 1898 in and native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. Graduate, Chekiang Medical College. 1925, M.D. (Berlin). 1927, graduate, Public Health Academy, Prussia. 1927-8, professor of public sanitation, Southeastern Medical University, Shanghai; dean, Shanghai Women's Medical College. Commissioner of Health, Municipal Government of Greater Nanking, since 1928. — p. 949

**HU Ping-hsia, (胡彬夏)** HU PING-HSIA, (胡彬夏).—Prefers English rendering: Pingsa Hu. Wife of Chu T'ing-ch'i (T. C. Chu). Born 1888; native of Wusih, Kiangsu. 1902, studied in Japan 1907, went to U.S.A. 1914, B.A. (Wellesley). 1915-21, president, National Y.W.C.A., Shanghai. 1920, secretary, Chinese-American Bank of Commerce, Shanghai. 1921, president, Shanghai Women's Club. 1925, vice-president, Government College for Girls, Peking. 1926-7, dean, school of studies, China University, Peking. Author of "The Walnut Hill Principals"; editor of "Chinese Students' Annual" (U.S.A.), "Ladies Journal" (Shanghai), and "Some aspects of Chinese Civilization." — p. 949

**HU Po-yuen, (胡愽淵)** HU PO-YUEN, (胡愽淵).—Prefers English rendering: P. Y. Hu. Born 1889 in and native of Wuchin, Kiangsu. 1916, B.S. (Mass. Inst. Tech.). 1917, Petroleum engineer (Pittsburgh). Mining engineer in U.S.A. for two years. Mining engineer, Lung Yen Iron Mining Co., Peking, for five years. Smelting superintendent, Ta Yeh Iron Works, Pinghsiang, for four years. Director, department of mines, Ministry of Agriculture and Mining since March 1928. — p. 949

**HU Shih, (胡適)** HU SHIH, (胡適).—Courtesy name: Shih-chih (適之). Born 1891 in Shanghai. 1914, B.A. (Cornell). 1917, Ph.D. (Columbia). 1917-8, advocated "literary revolution." First to write poetry in spoken language. 1917-26, professor of philosophy, Peking National University. 1922-6, dean, department of English literature, same University. 1926, one of three Chinese members on British Boxer Indemnity Advisory Committee. Professor of Philosophy, Kwang Hua University, Shanghai, and President, Chinese National Institute, Woosung, since July 1927. Author of "The Development of the Logical Method in Ancient-China" (doctoral dissertation); also (in Chinese) of "Outline of Chinese Philosophy", and "Ancient History of China." Editor of "Endeavour" (Chinese weekly) for a number of years. Recognized as one of foremost Chinese modern thinkers, although a moderate radical. — p. 949

**HU Shih-tseh, (胡世澤)** HU SHIH-TSEH, (胡世澤).—Courtesy name: Tzu-tseh (子澤). Prefers English rendering: Victor Chi-tsai Hoo. Born 1894 in Washington, D.C.; native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. Graduate, Annen School (Petrograd) and Ecole des sciences politiques (Paris). 1918, docteur en droit, University of Paris. Sometime secretary, delegation to Paris and Washington Conferences; technical expert, delegation to League of Nations Assembly, Geneva; chargé d'Affaires in Berlin; assistant director, drafting department, Customs Conference. Secretary, Ministry for Foreign Affairs, Nanking, sisce December 1928. Author of "The Basic Principles of Sino-Russian Relations" (doctoral dissertation in French). — p. 949

**HU Tsung-to, (胡宗鐸)** HU TSUNG-TO, (胡宗鐸).—Born 1892; native of Huang-mei, Hupeh. Graduate, Paoting military academy. Sometime defence commissioner of Western Kwangsi. 1926, divisional commander, Nationalist 7th army. 1927, commander, 19th army. 1928, garrison commander, Wuchang, Hanyang and Hankow; member, Wuhan branch, Kuomintang Central Political Council. April 1929, cashiered for insubordination, together with other Kwangsi generals in Wuhan. — p. 949-950

**HU, Victor Chi-tsai** HU, VICTOR CHI-TSAI, see HU SHIH-TSEH. — p. 950

**HU Wei-teh, (胡惟德)** HU WEI-TEH, (胡惟德).—Courtesy name: Hsing-wu (馨五). Prefers to sign himself: Hoo Wei-teh. Born 1871; native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. Studied in Language School, Shanghai. Chüjen of imperial examinations. 1904, minister to Russia. 1907, junior councillor, Board of Foreign Affairs. 1908, minister to Japan. 1910, senior vice-president, Board of Foreign Affairs. 1911-2, acting minister of finance and foreign affairs. 1915, minister to France, Spain and Portugal. 1920-5, minister to Japan. 1926, minister for foreign affairs, and premier of Regency Cabinet. — p. 950

**HU, Wenfu Yiko** HU, WENFU YIKO, see below. — p. 950

**HU Yi-ku, (胡詒穀)** HU YI-KU, (胡詒穀).—Courtesy name: Wen-fu (文甫). Prefers English rendering: Wenfu Yiko Hu. Born 1876; native of Tz'uch'i, Chekiang. Graduate, St. John's University. 1908, B.A.; 1909, LL.B. (Illinois). Sometime acting chief justice, Supreme Court; chief judge, 3rd civil division, Supreme Court. Vice-president, Shanghai Provisional Court, since January 1927. Editor of "English and Chinese Pocket Dictionary." — p. 950

**HUA Nan-k'uei, (華南圭)** HUA NAN-K'UEI, (華南圭).—Courtesy name: Tung-ch'i (通[illegible]). Prefers English rendering: T. T. Hoa or Hoa Nan Kuai. Born 1876; native of Wusih, Kiangsu. Graduate, Peking National University. 1910, M.S.A. (Paris). 1911, Chinshih of imperial examinations. Sometime chief engineer, Peking-Hankow Railway. Director, Bureau of Public Works, Municipal Government of Greater Peiping, since August 1928. — p. 950

**HUANG Fu, (黃郛)** HUANG FU, (黃郛).—Courtesy name: Ying-po (膺白). Born 1883 at Hangchow; native of Shanghai. 1910, graduate, Tokyo Military Academy; joined Tung Meng Hui. 1911, chief of staff to Gen. Ch'en Ch'i-mei. 1913, participated in unsuccessful Second Revolution; fled to Japan. 1921, adviser, delegation to Washington Conference 1922, chairman, finance commission. 1923, minister of foreign affairs and education. November 1924, acting premier. 1925, delegate, Customs Conference; appointed minister to Germany but did not proceed. 1927, mayor of Greater Shanghai. December 1927-May 1928, minister for foreign affairs, Nanking. Author of three volumes relating to the future of China after the European War. — p. 950

**HUANG Hsien-chao, (黃憲昭)** HUANG HSIEN-CHAO, (黃憲昭).—Prefers anglicized Cantonese rendering: Hin Wong. Born 18[illegible]8 in Honolulu of Cantonese parents. Graduate, Oahu College (Honolulu); studied in Columbia University. 1912, B.S. in journalism (Missouri). 1921, delegate, International Press Congress, Honolulu; representative of Canton newspapers at Washington Conference. 1917-20, director of intelligence bureau, Canton Military Government. Editor-in-chief, Canton Times, for many years. Founder and first editor, Canton Daily News. Canton correspondent of Reuter's Agency and China Weekly Review (Shanghai), since 1923. — p. 950

**HUANG Jung-liang, (黃榮良)** HUANG JUNG-LIANG, (黃榮良).—Courtesy name: Tzu-ch'eng (子誠). Prefers English rendering: Y. L. Hwang. Born 1875; native of Wuwei, Anhwei. 1896, B.A. (Nanking). 1903, B.A. (Baker). 1905, M.A. (Columbia). Sometime consul general in Australia; commissioner for foreign affairs, Tientsin. 1920-6, minister to Austria; delegate to League of Nations Assembly. Principal of the Tientsin Anglo-Chinese College (June 1929). — p. 950
